**Locally**, `compose.yaml` (repository root) runs one Postgres (stack `ccms`, `127.0.0.1:5432`, user and password `ccms`) for every checkout. `setup-local` writes a database per checkout into `.env.local` (`ccms` for the main checkout, `ccms_<slot>` for an agent slot) and creates it when Postgres is up, so an unmerged migration on one branch never reaches another checkout's data. After regenerating a migration on top of the base, reset your checkout's database (`docker compose exec postgres dropdb -U ccms --force <name>`, then `setup-local` again): it still records the old one. `pnpm slot remove <slot>` drops the slot's own databases (`ccms_<slot>` and its perf profiles', never one with open connections); `pnpm slot prune-databases` lists those of slots already gone and drops them with `--yes` (`scripts/slot-databases.mjs`). `docker compose down -v` deletes it all.

**Health.** Two routes, no token. The body is plain JSON: `{ "status": "ok", "database": "ok", "pool": "ok", "blobs": "ok" }`. `database` is `unavailable` when the database does not answer within 2 seconds; `pool` is `saturated` while queries wait for a free pool connection (`CCMS_DATABASE_POOL_MAX`); `blobs` is `unavailable` when the blob store does not answer within 2 seconds (its ping is shared and reused for a second, like the database's); `status` is `degraded` for any of them. `/health/ready` answers 503 `blob-store-unavailable` for the last. The reason goes to the log, never the response.

- `GET /health` is **liveness**: always 200 while the server runs. Content and PDF routes work without the database, so a database outage must not make an orchestrator restart every instance. Use it for restart decisions.
- `GET /health/ready` is **readiness**: 200 when `status` is `ok`, else a JSON:API 503 error (code `database-unavailable` or `database-saturated`, with `Retry-After`). **A load balancer uses this one** to take an instance out of rotation while the database is down or the pool exhausted, and puts it back when it recovers; the instance is not restarted.

Concurrent checks share one ping (on a connection of its own, outside the pool, so a full pool never makes the database look down and a hung database never holds a pool connection), reused for a second. Saturation is read from the pool and costs nothing.

**The worker's health** (`CCMS_ROLE=worker`, #527; `src/health/api/worker-health.server.ts`). A worker mounts no route: it answers only `GET`/`HEAD /health` and `/health/ready` on `HOST:PORT`, with a plain `node:http` server and no token, as above, plus `"role": "worker"` and `queue`: `{ ready, running, lagSeconds, consumers, level }` (see "Jobs", "Roles"; `null` when the queue cannot be read within 2 s). Any other path is a JSON:API 404, any other method 405 (`Allow: GET, HEAD`); a request body is never read; headers are bounded (8 KiB, 5 s), keep-alive is 1 s. Its readiness does not depend on the queue's lag (a backlog is not a reason to restart a worker): it is the database, the pool and the blob store, as the API's. The API's `/health/ready` never depends on a worker. In compose the worker binds `127.0.0.1`, for the image's health check only.

**Migration lock policy.** A migration holds the advisory lock (at most 120 s waiting for another process's migration), then each statement waits at most `CCMS_DATABASE_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 10 s) for a table lock; past it the attempt rolls back, and is repeated up to `CCMS_DATABASE_MIGRATE_ATTEMPTS` times (default 3) with 1 s, 2 s, 4 s ... pauses, so a rolling deploy rides out a long transaction instead of crash-looping. Only a lock timeout is repeated. For deployments where even that is too fragile, migrate once before starting servers (`CCMS_DATABASE_MIGRATE=false` on them, `db-migrate` as a deploy step, which takes the same settings) and raise the timeout there.

## Blob store

The `blobs` component (`src/blobs/`): binary files (images now; later attachments, downloads and cached PDFs) live in object storage, never in Git. Git keeps only their hashes: a module refers to an image as `sha256:<hash>`, so a commit or release tag pins exactly its images.

- **Port** `BlobStore` (`core/blob-store.ts`): `put(stream, { contentType, maxBytes })` returns `{ sha256, size, created }` (`created`: this put stored the bytes; false when a complete copy was there); `get(sha256)` a `StoredBlob` (`stream` with `size` and `contentType`) or `undefined`; `head`, `exists`; `delete`, for retention only; `ping`, for readiness. **Content-addressed**: the key is the SHA-256 the store computes while writing (`core/digest.ts`), so a key always names exactly its bytes; the same bytes again are a no-op (the first content type stays), and a damaged copy (another length) is replaced. Nothing is visible under a hash until it is completely written. More than `maxBytes` is 422 `too-large`, with nothing stored. A hash that is not 64 lowercase hex characters, or a content type that is not a plain lowercase `type/subtype`, is refused before it reaches a key or a path. A store that cannot be reached throws `UnavailableError` (503 `blob-store-unavailable`), never "missing".
- **Adapters** (`repos/`), picked by `CCMS_BLOB_DRIVER` (`blobs.module.ts`), so swapping them is a setting. `S3BlobStore`: any S3-compatible service (`CCMS_S3_ENDPOINT`, `CCMS_S3_REGION`, `CCMS_S3_BUCKET`, the key pair or the SDK's default credential chain, `CCMS_S3_FORCE_PATH_STYLE`); one object per blob, keyed by its hash; a put spools to a private temporary folder while hashing, then uploads with `x-amz-checksum-sha256`, so the service refuses bytes that do not match, and is retried from the start of the file (it is idempotent). `FsBlobStore` (`CCMS_BLOB_FS_ROOT`), for single-machine installs: `<ab>/<hash>` plus `<hash>.json` with the type, written and synced in `tmp/`, then renamed into place. `FakeBlobStore` (`tests/fakes/`) for unit tests. One contract suite (`tests/blob-store-contract.ts`) runs against all three in `blob-stores.spec.ts`; the S3 run needs `CCMS_TEST_S3_ENDPOINT` (e.g. `CCMS_TEST_S3_ENDPOINT=http://127.0.0.1:8333 pnpm nx test api` with the compose service up). The core lint rule keeps `@aws-sdk/*` out of `core/`.
- **Serving** `GET /assets/:sha256` (`content.read`): only an image or an attached file a module the reader may see refers to, at `?ref=` (else 404, exactly like a missing one). Streamed from the store with its stored type and `Content-Length`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox` (an SVG never runs scripts), `Content-Disposition: inline` for raster images and SVG and `attachment` for any other type and for every file an attachment block the reader may see attaches (see "Attachments"), and the caching of every content route: `private, max-age=31536000, immutable` for a commit-ID `?ref=`, else `private, no-cache` with the scope-keyed ETag (the bytes of a hash never change, but who may see them can). Never a redirect to a bucket URL: keep the bucket private.
- **The old layout:** an image the store does not hold is still read from `assets/<sha256>` in the repository (`GitContentRepo`), for serving and for PDFs, until that layout is removed. `pnpm nx run api:blobs-import` (`import-git-assets.main.ts`) copies every `assets/` file at the head of every branch and tag into the store, with its sniffed type; it changes nothing in Git and can run again. Before the old layout is removed on the strength of an import, run it again with uploads stopped (or check `exists` for every imported hash): an upload's clean-up ("Image uploads") may remove a blob a concurrent import reported imported, and the Git fallback hides that only while the layout exists.
- **Locally**, `compose.yaml` runs SeaweedFS (Apache-2.0) on `127.0.0.1:8333` with local credentials. `setup-local` writes the S3 settings with a bucket per checkout: `ccms` for the main checkout (created when the service starts), `ccms-<slot>` for an agent slot (created by setup-local, `scripts/local-bucket.ts`); `pnpm slot remove` deletes a slot's bucket. In production, bring your own S3-compatible bucket.
- **Listing and age** (#255): `list(after?)` pages through the blobs in hash order (`{ blobs: [{ sha256, size, storedAt }], next? }`: S3's own pages of 1,000 keys, other keys skipped; one `<ab>/` folder per page for the file system), and `head`/`get` give `storedAt`, the object's or file's modification time: what "unused" reads for a blob no upload records. The callers of `delete` are deleting an unused file ("Media library"), an upload's clean-up of the blob it just stored, and retention later.
- **Retention** (not built yet; deleting an unused file by hand is, see "Media library") would be the unattended caller of `delete`. It needs a record of uploads with their time (`asset_uploads`, the upload route's, #25: see "Image uploads"); the set of hashes referred to by every commit reachable from any branch or tag (not only the heads: a release tag pins its images for good); and a grace period, so an upload not yet committed is not taken. It deletes only a hash that is unreferenced and older than the grace period, checking the references again right before deleting (a commit may have taken it meanwhile). It also sweeps blobs that have no upload record and no reference past the grace period (by their stored time): the backstop for an upload whose own clean-up failed (see "Image uploads").

## Image uploads

Part of the `assets` component (#25): authors add images while editing. The bytes go into the blob store; Git gets only the hash, when a module that refers to it is committed.

- **`POST /change-sets/:changeSetId/assets`** (`content.edit`) takes `multipart/form-data` with one file part named `file` and nothing else (another field, a second file, a part under another name, a form cut off or malformed: 400 `invalid-body`; another media type: 415 `unsupported-media-type`; a form is sent with `X-Ccms-Request`, else 403 `request-header-required`: see "Cross-site requests"). Only the change set's author, while it is a draft, as for locks and saves (403 for anyone else who holds `content.read.draft`, 404 exactly like a missing change set for anyone who does not; any other state 409 `invalid-state` with `meta.state`). These are checked **before a byte of the body is read**, and again in the recording transaction (which locks the change set's row, so an abandon cannot slip in between).
- **Type** from the bytes, never from the part's `Content-Type`, its file name or the URL: PNG (its signature and the `IHDR` chunk every PNG starts with) or JPEG (`FF D8 FF` and a marker opening its first segment: APPn, DQT, DHT, a frame header or a comment). Anything else is 422 `unsupported-image-type` with `meta.accepted`. **SVG is refused**, not sanitised: an SVG is a document that can carry scripts, event handlers, `javascript:` URLs, `foreignObject` and references to other files, and a sanitiser would put an XML parser and a DOM implementation on the upload path, with its own record of bypasses, for a format a PNG export covers. Images already in the repository (the old layout, which may hold SVGs) are still served, with the sandboxing headers.
- **Size:** at most `CCMS_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` (default 10 MiB, at most 1 GiB), passed to `BlobStore.put` as `maxBytes`: past it 422 `too-large` (`meta.max`) and nothing stored. Reading stops there: a `Content-Length` larger than the limit plus 16 KiB of multipart framing is refused before reading, the bytes read off the request are capped at the same (also without a `Content-Length`, so a chunked body with an endless preamble, epilogue or parts the parser skips, those without a `form-data` disposition, is cut off as `too-large`), the parser (`busboy`) hands on at most one byte past the limit of the file, a request already gone before reading starts is refused (400) rather than waited for, and the answer to a request whose body was not read to its end carries `Connection: close`, so the rest is never taken in.
- **Storing:** the bytes are streamed into `BlobStore.put` as they arrive (`api/multipart-file.ts`); the file's end is handed over only once the whole form has been read and found well-formed, so a request cut off or holding anything else stores nothing. Then one transaction records the upload and an `asset.upload` audit event (subject `asset/<hash>`, `data: { changeSetId, size, contentType }`). **No Git commit**: the branch is untouched until a save point commits a module whose image block names `sha256:<hash>` as its `asset`.
- **Idempotent:** the same image into the same change set again is 200 with the first upload (no new blob, record or event; only the record's `last_uploaded_at` moves, for retention); a new one is 201 with `Location` (its preview). The same image into another change set is a record of its own (the blob is stored once). A change set holds at most 1,000 images (409 `too-many-uploads`, `meta.max`): a full one is refused before a byte is read (an image it holds already too: its author has the reference), and the count is taken again after the insert, in the transaction holding the change set's row, which rolls back past the cap (uploads at the same time). No per-actor quota yet: an author's drafts together are bounded only by 1,000 images each.
- **The answer** is a `ChangeSetAsset` (`id` `<changeSetId>/<hash>`): `attributes` `reference` (`sha256:<hash>`, for the module), `contentType`, `size`, `fileName`, `uploadedBy`, `uploadedAt`; `relationships.changeSet`.
- **Previewing a fresh upload:** `GET /assets/:hash` serves an image only once a module the reader may see refers to it (see "Blob store"), which an image in a working copy does not yet. `GET /change-sets/:changeSetId/assets/:hash` (`content.read`) serves an image uploaded into that change set to its author only, as their working copies are (403 for another reader of drafts, 404 for anyone else; any hash not uploaded into it, malformed or not, 404): the author sent those very bytes, so it shows nothing they did not have. The same headers as the asset route (stored type, `nosniff`, the sandboxing CSP), `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`. Once the module is committed, `GET /assets/:hash?ref=change-sets/<id>` serves it to whoever reads that module there.
- **Table** `asset_uploads` (`repos/asset-uploads.tables.ts`, migration `0010_asset_uploads`), for retention: `change_set_id` (references `change_sets`) and `sha256` (the primary key, so one record per change set and image), `size`, `content_type` (`image/png` or `image/jpeg` for an image; any lowercase `type/subtype` for an attachment, since `0012_attachments`), `file_name` (the uploaded file's name as a `FileName`, or null; since `0012_attachments`), `uploaded_by_kind`/`uploaded_by_id`, `uploaded_at` (the first recording transaction's time), `last_uploaded_at` (the latest upload's: retention's grace period runs from it, since that upload handed out the reference again); indexes by hash and by `last_uploaded_at`. The formats are checked by the database too. **A refused recording cleans up:** when the transaction refuses after the bytes were stored (the change set left draft or filled up meanwhile: 409; the database timed out), a blob this very put stored (`created`; never one that was there before, which content or another change set may use) is deleted again, unless an upload record refers to it. The check and the delete hold the hash's advisory lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock`), which recording takes too, and recording checks under it that the blob is still there (else 503 `upload-interrupted`, `Retry-After: 1`: upload it again), so a concurrent upload of the same image into another change set is never left with a record of a removed blob. The clean-up is best effort and logged when it fails. A crash between storing and recording, or a failed clean-up, leaves a blob with no record, visible to nobody (the preview needs the record, the asset route a module's reference): retention must treat a blob it finds no upload record for as uploaded at its stored time (or keep it), never as old. A clean-up's S3 delete that timed out on the client may still be applied after the lock is released; a later upload record of that image would then name a missing blob (the preview answers 404, and uploading it again stores it). The window is the S3 request timeout and is not closed today.

### Fonts

Part of the `assets` component (#555): fonts for page designs, uploaded as images are.

- **Upload:** `POST /change-sets/:changeSetId/assets?kind=font`. Everything of "Image uploads" holds (the author, the draft, the form, `X-Ccms-Request`, idempotence, the 1,000 files per change set, the storage limit, the clean-up), plus at most 100 fonts per change set (`MAX_FONTS_PER_CHANGE_SET`, 409 `too-many-uploads`, counted before reading and again in the recording transaction).
- **Size:** at most `CCMS_MAX_FONT_BYTES` (default 10 MiB, at most 64 MiB). A font is read whole into memory before anything is stored (its structure decides it), at most one byte past the limit: past it 422 `too-large` (`meta.max`).
- **Format** by structure, never by the name or the part's type (`core/font-file.ts`, `inspectFont`): TrueType (`00 01 00 00`, `true`), OpenType (`OTTO`), WOFF (`wOFF`) or WOFF2 (`wOF2`), with a table directory whose every table lies within the file, the tables a font needs (`head` with its magic number, `name`, `cmap`, `maxp`, `hhea`, `hmtx`, and `glyf` with `loca` or `CFF `/`CFF2`), a WOFF's length equal to the file's and each table inflating to its stated length, a WOFF2's brotli stream decompressing to exactly its tables' length, and a family name. Decompression is bounded (64 MiB). Nothing else is parsed in the API process: no glyph, no hinting, no rendering. A font collection (`ttcf`) or anything else is 422 `unsupported-font-type` (`meta.accepted`, `meta.reason`: `not-a-font` or `malformed`). Stored as `font/ttf`, `font/otf`, `font/woff` or `font/woff2`, whatever type the same bytes were stored with before.
- **Renderer:** the compiling child process (never the API process) decodes WOFF and WOFF2 and hands the fonts to Typst, which ignores one it cannot read: a malformed font never fails a render, the default font stands in.

## Attachments

Part of the `assets` component (#236): files of any kind readers download from a module (a ZIP of configuration files, a JSON sample, a datasheet, a firmware file), held by an `attachment` block (`@openfolia/content-model`, "Attachments"). They take the image path: the same upload route, blob store, record and audit event, the same reading scope.

- **Upload:** `POST /change-sets/:changeSetId/assets?kind=attachment` (without `kind`, or `kind=image`, it is the image upload as before). Everything of "Image uploads" holds (the author, the draft, the form, `X-Ccms-Request`, idempotence, the 1,000 files per change set, the clean-up), except the type and the size:
  - **Type** decided by the server (`core/attachment-types.ts`), never from the part's `Content-Type`: bytes that show a dangerous kind win over any name (HTML by the WHATWG sniffing patterns, also after a byte order mark or as UTF-16, SVG, PE, ELF and Mach-O executables, `#!` scripts, OLE compound files: legacy Office documents and installers), so `report.json` holding HTML is HTML; else the type of the file name's extension (a fixed table: `.zip`, `.json`, `.pdf`, `.csv`, office formats, …); else a signature the bytes show (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, PDF, ZIP, gzip, 7z); else `application/octet-stream`. The first 4 KiB are read for it before anything is stored.
  - **Rules** (settings, `.env.example`): `CCMS_ATTACHMENT_TYPES_ALLOW` (when set, only a file whose media type or extension it names; a list of only commas is refused at startup), `CCMS_ATTACHMENT_TYPES_DENY` (refused on top), and the **dangerous types**, refused unless `CCMS_ATTACHMENT_TYPES_OPEN` names them: HTML and XHTML, SVG, JavaScript, Windows, Linux and macOS executables and installers, Java and Android archives, HTA, shell scripts, Office documents that carry macros (the legacy `.doc`, `.xls`, `.ppt` formats and any OLE compound file, and the macro-enabled `.docm`, `.xlsm`, `.pptm`, …) (`DANGEROUS_TYPES`), and the extensions that run or render when opened whatever the bytes are (`.html`, `.svg`, `.exe`, `.ps1`, `.bat`, `.sh`, `.jar`, `.lnk`, …: `DANGEROUS_EXTENSIONS`; a name that is all extension, such as `.hta`, has that extension). Each list holds media types (`application/zip`), top-level types (`text/*`) and extensions (`.zip`). **Mismatch** first: bytes with a known signature (an image, PDF, ZIP, gzip, 7z, HTML, SVG, an executable) under an extension that stands for another known type are refused (`reason: mismatch`, even where the type is opened): PNG bytes named `page.html`, HTML named `data.json`, a PDF named `.zip`; a ZIP container (`.docx`, `.xlsx`, `.odt`, `.jar`, `.apk`) agrees with ZIP bytes, and `.bin` or an unknown extension claims no type. The allow list takes a file by its stored type, or by its extension only where the extension stands for that type (`.dat` on the list does not take ZIP bytes named `data.dat`); the deny list refuses a file any of its types (stored, the extension's, the bytes') or its extension names, so denying `application/zip` denies a `.docx` too. A dangerous file is opened by an entry naming its media type, or its extension when that extension stands for exactly that type (`.html` opens a `page.html` of HTML, never an executable named `page.html`). An allow list never opens a dangerous type. Refused: 422 `unsupported-attachment-type` with `meta.contentType`, `meta.extension` and `meta.reason` (`mismatch`, `not-allowed`, `denied`, `dangerous`), before a byte is stored. **Why settings and not `openfolia.yaml`:** what a server stores and hands out is the operator's security decision, not the content's; a content repository (which authors change) must not be able to open HTML on the server.
  - **Size:** at most `CCMS_MAX_ATTACHMENT_BYTES` (default 50 MiB, at most 1 GiB), with the same early refusals as an image's.
  - **The file name** the client sent (decoded as UTF-8, as browsers send it) is kept as a `FileName` (`toFileName`: its last path segment, forbidden characters as `_`, at most 255 characters; none when nothing is left) in `asset_uploads.file_name` (migration `0012_attachments`, which also lets `content_type` hold any lowercase `type/subtype`) and in the audit event's `data.fileName`; the answer's `fileName` (null when none). Image uploads keep theirs too. The same bytes uploaded again keep the first upload's name and type in a change set; the blob store keeps the first type it stored the bytes with, and an upload of bytes it already holds is recorded and answered with that type (what downloads are served as).
  - **Virus scanning** (not built) would hook into `UploadAssetAction` between reading the head and `BlobStore.put` (a scanner reading the stream as it is stored, refusing before the file is recorded) or right after the put, before recording, removing a refused file as an unrecorded one is.
- **Downloads** (`GET /assets/:sha256`, and the author's preview `GET /change-sets/:changeSetId/assets/:hash`) of an attached file always send `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="<ASCII>"; filename*=UTF-8''<percent-encoded>` (RFC 8187: the ASCII form with every other character, `"`, `\`, `%` and `;` as `_`; nothing of the name reaches the header unencoded), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox`, so no attached file ever renders in the app's origin, an opened HTML or SVG file included. The name is the `fileName` of the first attachment block the reader may see (by module ID and language) attaching it, of those that fit the stored file (`nameFits`: a dangerous extension only when it stands for exactly the stored type, any other only when it stands for the stored type, for no known type, or the file is stored as `application/octet-stream`); with none that fits, the hash. So a block in content cannot offer an allowed ZIP as `evil.html`; the `attachment-file-name` check names such a block; a file an attachment block attaches is a download even when an image block shows it too (an `<img>` loads it all the same). The preview shows only PNG and JPEG in place (the image dialog's) and sends every other upload as a download under its upload's name (else the hash). Images keep their rules.
- **Listing:** `GET /assets` lists attached files with the images: `attributes.fileName` (the first readable attachment block's, else the upload's; null for neither), `meta.usedAs` (`image`, `attachment`). `filter[type]=attachment` keeps the files an attachment block the reader may see attaches and the uploads of `filter[changeSet]` of a type no image upload has (a media type filter works as for images); `filter[q]` also matches an attachment block's name, description and file name, and an upload's file name.
- **Checks:** `missing-attachment` (blocking, category `attachment`), as `missing-image`; `attachment-file-name` (blocking): the block's `fileName` does not fit the stored file (`nameFits`, below).
- **Outputs:** the HTML preview links the file through the same asset route (`@openfolia/render-html`), the PDF prints its name, facts and description with a note that the file is downloaded from the online version (`@openfolia/render-typst`; the PDF renderer is not given the file).

## Safety symbols

- **`GET /symbols/:id`** (`content.read`, #208; `GetSymbolAction` in `assets/core`): the artwork of a configured safety symbol (`safetySymbols` in `GET /config`), `symbols/<id>.png` or `symbols/<id>.svg` (the PNG when both exist, as the PDF picks), in the repository at `?ref=`, the file the PDF prints (`ReadableContent.symbolArtwork`, `symbolFile`). Symbols are configuration: every reader of the ref reads them, as `GET /config` (no scope applies; a ref the reader may not open is 404 as for every content read). 404 for an ID that is not configured or has no artwork; 422 `invalid-content` when the bytes are not what the extension says (sniffed like images, `sniffImageType`: an `.svg` that is no SVG, a `.png` that is no PNG); 400 for an ID that is no configuration key, `unsupported-parameter` for any query parameter but `ref`.
- **Served as images are** (`assetFile`): the sniffed type, `Content-Disposition: inline`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox`, so an SVG opened on its own runs nothing; clients show it as an image and never inline its markup (the editor rasterises SVG artwork to PNG before showing it). Cached like every content route (`private, max-age=31536000, immutable` for a commit-ID `?ref=`, else `private, no-cache` with the ETag, 304 on `If-None-Match`).
- **In the HTML** (both previews and the manual's reader HTML): see "HTML previews".

## Image list

`GET /assets` (#72, `assets` component, `ListAssetsAction`): the images for the insert-image dialog, paged like every list.

- **What is listed:** the images a module the reader may see refers to (`sha256:<hash>` in an image block) at `?ref=`, one `Asset` per hash (`id`), ordered by the ID (then language) of the first such module, ties by first appearance. The same reading scope as `GET /assets/:sha256`, through `ReadableContent.assetUsage()`: an image only modules outside the scope show is not listed, `meta.usedIn` names only modules the reader may see, and `meta.page.total` counts only what is listed, so neither says what lies outside the scope. A reference that is not `sha256:` and 64 hex characters is left out (nothing could serve it). Images in the blob store that no readable module refers to, such as uploads into other change sets, are never listed.
- **Attributes:** `reference` (`sha256:<hash>`, for an image block's `asset`), `contentType`, `size` and `width`/`height` in pixels as the file's header states them (PNG, JPEG's first frame header, GIF, WebP; EXIF orientation not applied; `null` for an SVG or a header not within the first 256 KiB). From the blob store, else from `assets/<hash>` in the repository (the old layout, type sniffed). A file neither holds is listed with those `null` and `meta.missing: true` (a module refers to an image that is gone). **Meta:** `usedIn` (`{ moduleId, lang, title }`, by module ID and language, at most 50), `usedInCount` (all of them), `uploaded` and `missing`.
- **Search:** `filter[q]` (1 to 200 characters after trimming; empty or longer is 400 `invalid-parameter`) keeps the images it is found in, case, diacritics and runs of whitespace aside (folded as `GET /search` folds; a query that folds to nothing finds nothing), as a substring of: the alt text or title (plain text) of an image block showing it, or the title or ID of a module showing it. Only modules the reader may see are searched (`AssetUse.searchText`, folded once with the scoped usage it belongs to, per commit and reading key), so an image is never found, listed or counted through a module outside the scope. An upload no module shows yet is found by its file name (`asset_uploads.file_name`, #236). A whole SHA-256 (any case) also finds the file of that hash, among the files the list holds anyway (on the unused list too; the storage view's largest files link with it, #404). It combines with `filter[type]` and `filter[changeSet]` and runs before paging, so `meta.page.total` counts the matches.
- **Filters:** `filter[lang]` and `filter[unused]` (see "Media library"); `filter[type]`, a top-level type (`image`: every image) or a media type (`image/png`), lowercase, applied before paging; a missing file has no type, so a type filter never matches it; any other form is 400 `invalid-parameter`. Other parameters are 400 as on every route.
- **Drafts: `filter[changeSet]=<id>`.** Images uploaded into a change set are in no commit until a save point commits a module referring to them, so reading at the change set's branch alone would not show a fresh upload to its author. With `filter[changeSet]`, the images uploaded into that change set (`asset_uploads`) come first, last uploaded first, with `meta.uploaded: true` (preview them at `GET /change-sets/:changeSetId/assets/:hash` until a module at the ref shows them), then the images of the ref as above, each listed once. It widens the list rather than narrowing it, so it is only for the change set's author, as the preview is: 403 `forbidden` for another reader of drafts, 404 `not-found` for anyone else and for an ID that names no change set. It does not move the ref: the editor reads at the change set's branch, `?ref=change-sets/<id>&filter[changeSet]=<id>`, so the images its modules show are listed too, with their uses there.
- **Reading the facts** (`AssetInfoReader`, `core/asset-info.ts`): only for hashes the reader may see (listed above), never more than the first 256 KiB of a stored file, at most 8 files at once, only the page's files unless `filter[type]` needs every one's type. The facts of a hash never change, so they are kept in memory (at most 20,000, least recently used dropped), and so is what must not be read again on every list: a file of the old layout whose bytes are not its name and a file neither store holds (for a minute, as an upload may store it), each per commit and reading key, since what the repository gives depends on what the reader may see. Only the facts of a blob, which depend on its bytes alone, are shared between readers. The old layout's files are read whole (the content repository has no partial read); the result is kept. Kept facts are not checked again, so `missing: false` can outlast a blob's removal by retention (not built yet). A blob store that does not answer, also mid-read, is 503 `blob-store-unavailable`, never "missing"; after one failed read no further file is read for that request. Where-used is derived from the modules at the ref (kept per commit and reading key with the asset scope); the module index (#45) can take it over behind `ReadableContent.assetUsage()`.
- **Caching:** the answer also depends on uploads and the blob store, so the ETag is a hash of the answer (as for modules' status), `private, no-cache`, never `immutable`.

## Media library

The API behind the media library (#255, `assets` component): renditions for a grid of images, which languages use each file, and deleting what nobody uses, never breaking a release.

- **Renditions:** `GET /assets/:sha256?width=320|640|1280` (and `GET /change-sets/:changeSetId/assets/:hash?width=` for an author's own upload) answer a WebP at most that wide, never upscaled and at most four times as tall (a very tall image comes out narrower), with EXIF orientation applied and no metadata. The same checks as the original, first (a rendition is 404 exactly where the original is); then a file other than a PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP (by its stored type, sniffed for the old layout) has none: 400 `invalid-parameter` (`source.parameter: width`), as is any other width. Made on first request (`Renditions`, `core/renditions.ts`) with sharp (`SharpImageResizer`, `repos/`, behind the `ImageResizer` port) and kept in the blob store under its own hash (the store stays content-addressed); `asset_renditions` (migration `0016_asset_renditions`) finds it by the source's hash and the width. The next request is served from the store; a row whose blob is gone is made again. Headers and caching as for the original (`image/webp`, `nosniff`, the sandboxing CSP).
- **Decoding limits** (settings, `.env.example`): every libvips loader but PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP from memory is blocked for the process (`sharp.block`), so SVG (librsvg), TIFF, HEIF, PDF and the rest never decode whatever the bytes claim; at most `CCMS_RENDITION_MAX_PIXELS` source pixels (default 40 million), checked from the header before decoding and by libvips' `limitInputPixels` during it (422 `too-large`, `meta.maxPixels`); at most `CCMS_RENDITION_MAX_SOURCE_BYTES` (default 50 MiB; 422 `too-large`, `meta.max`), read into memory only up to that; only the first frame of an animation; `CCMS_RENDITION_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 10 s) by libvips' timeout and a timer of its own (503 `rendition-timeout`, `Retry-After`; the source is not tried again for 5 minutes, shorter than for bytes that do not decode, since a busy machine may be the cause; after the timer the request, and its concurrency slot, waits until libvips has stopped, so slow decodes never run past `CCMS_RENDITION_CONCURRENCY`). One libvips thread per image (`sharp.concurrency(1)`), read sequentially. Bytes that do not decode are 422 `unprocessable-image`. One making per source and width at a time (more requests wait for it), at most `CCMS_RENDITION_CONCURRENCY` (default 2) per process with four waiting per slot, past which 503 `rendition-busy` (`Retry-After`). A source that failed to decode is not tried again for 10 minutes (per process, at most 1,000 kept). sharp's cache is off. Recording a rendition takes the source's hash lock and records nothing for a stored source deleted meanwhile.
- **Usage per language:** each `Asset` of `GET /assets` has `meta.languages` (the languages of the readable modules using it, all of them, sorted); `filter[lang]` keeps the files a readable module in that language uses. Image per language needs no model change: each language's module file has its own image blocks, so a translated module shows another image by pointing its own block at another hash; the translation import (#51) never changes a target file's image references. "Replace in <language>" is the editor's, on that language's module.
- **Unused files:** `GET /assets?filter[unused]=true` lists, instead, the files nobody uses: every upload record (`asset_uploads`, any change set) and every blob in the store (`BlobStore.list`), less renditions, less what anything refers to (`AssetReferences`, below), less the archived release PDFs (the files of `done` release outputs, `ArchivedFiles`, `core/archived-files.ts`, over `release_outputs`; kept for good, #241) that no upload records (#429: one an upload records is listed, so its uploads can go), less what was last uploaded (into any change set) or stored within 24 hours (`UNUSED_GRACE_MS`, #242's default). Last uploaded or stored first. `filter[q]` matches an upload's file name, `filter[type]` works as usual, `filter[changeSet]` keeps that change set's uploads (its author only), `filter[lang]` finds nothing. `false` is as if left out.
- **Where a file is used** (`AssetReferences`, `core/asset-references.ts`), whoever may read it: the module files at `main` and every other branch that is not a change set's, every tag, the head of every open change set (a merged or abandoned one's branch is kept but no longer counts), every file uploaded into an open change set (its author's draft, placed or not, so another editor neither sees it in the unused list nor deletes it; after a merge or abandonment it counts only where content refers to it), every kept import's uploaded bundle (`HeldBlobs`, `core/held-blobs.ts`, over `imports.upload_sha256`: `{ in: "import" }`; #332) and every working copy (`WorkingCopyStore.referencedAssets`, a regular expression over the stored JSON). A file counts with every `sha256:<hash>` its text mentions, read as text, not parsed, so a module that does not parse still keeps its files. Read in this order (working copies, open change sets, then every ref at once) so a reference moving on meanwhile (a save point commits a working copy before removing it; a merge moves the base branch before the change set leaves the open states) is seen at one place or the next. More than 10,000 open change sets: 503, never a guess.
- **Index of where files are used** (#476, `AssetReferenceIndex`, `core/asset-reference-index.ts`): what the module files at a commit mention is kept per commit in Postgres (`asset_reference_commits`, one row per commit with its hashes as an array: 38 MB for the 187 commits of the `large` perf profile, where a row per hash took 300 MB; migration `0038_asset_references`, `DrizzleAssetReferenceStore`), so a check lists the refs (one Git call) and reads the index, never every module file of every ref. A commit no ref's commit is indexed for is built when first asked for, whatever moved the ref (a save point, a merge, a release, an import's save point, a push), from the files that changed: each module file revision's hashes are kept in memory by its Git blob ID (100,000 revisions), so a new commit reads only the files no commit this process indexed had. Each read takes one snapshot of the table (`repeatable read`) and builds whatever it asked for and did not find, so a commit dropped meanwhile (by another server, or by hand) is built again, never taken as mentioning nothing; three misses in a row are a 503 `content-unavailable`. Commits no ref points to any more are dropped an hour after they were built (`PRUNE_AFTER_MS`). The hashes of the last set of commits asked for are kept in memory, so the unused list asks the database again only once a ref has moved. Rebuildable like the module index: `index-rebuild` drops it and indexes every branch and tag again; dropping the table by hand is just as safe. The unused list then costs the database queries above, the blob store's listing and a Git call; the grace period, the import bundles held and deleting's checks under the hash's lock are as before.
- **Deleting:** `DELETE /assets/:sha256` (`DeleteAssetAction`) deletes an unused file's blob, its renditions (rows and blobs, except bytes another source's rendition, an upload or content shares) and its upload records, with an `asset.delete` audit event (`data`: `size`, `contentType`, `uploads`, `renditions`, `fileKept`). The same bytes as an archived release PDF: only the upload records (and renditions) go, the file stays, and the event says `fileKept: true` (#429); an archived release PDF no upload records is refused, `{ in: "release-output" }`, nothing audited. 204; 404 when neither the store nor an upload record has it; 409 `asset-in-use` with `meta.usedIn` (`{ in: "main" | "release", ref }` for released refs, `{ in: "branch" | "change-set" | "working-copy" | "import" | "branding" | "release-output" }` unnamed, `{ in: "recent-upload" }` with `meta.deletableAt`, `{ in: "rendition" }`; at most 50). Attachments follow the same rules. **Who:** `content.read.draft` and `content.edit`, each in a role whose scope restricts nothing (unused files include other people's abandoned draft uploads, so never a contributor, #137) (#255 asks for no restriction on languages, as an upload has no module; the other dimensions are required unrestricted too, after the security review, so a role kept to some content decides about no files); 403 otherwise, also for `filter[unused]`.
- **No reference slips in between check and delete:** the action looks first (409 without touching anything), then marks the file deleted under its hash lock (`asset_deletions`, the same advisory lock uploads and renditions take; under it, a kept import's bundle is refused too, `HeldBlobs.holds`, so an import storing the same bytes meanwhile either is seen or finds them gone, 503 `upload-interrupted`), reads where it is used again, and only then deletes, under the lock again (records and the audit event, then the renditions' blobs, the source's last); a use found then, or any failure of the last step, clears the mark. **Saving a working copy** takes, in one statement and in hash order, the lock of every file its image and attachment blocks name (`fileReferences`: real references only, at most `MAX_FILES_PER_WORKING_COPY`, 200 distinct files, else 422 `too-many-files` before any lock, so a copy cannot exhaust the lock table) and refuses one marked deleted (409 `asset-deleted`, `meta.assets`), so a save either commits before the mark (and the second read sees it) or is refused. Save points commit only what working copies hold and merges only an open change set's head, both counted. Uploading the same bytes again clears the mark (and moves the upload's time, so a delete in progress refuses). Not covered: content written into the repository outside the API (a push) between the check and the delete; such content gets the `missing-image` check, and uploading the file again restores it. **A release output finishing** onto the same bytes records them under the same lock (`ReleaseOutputStore.finish` and `finishGroup` take it before any row, #593; never during the render): a delete then either sees the output `done` (only the uploads go, the file stays) or removed the blob first, which the render, still under the lock, finds gone; it stores the bytes again and records them once more (gone twice, the job retries, `output-file-gone`).
- **Kept facts:** the list's facts of a deleted file are dropped in this process (`AssetInfoReader.forget`); another process may list it as not missing for up to its cache's life.

## HTML previews

`GET /modules/:lang/:id/preview` and `GET /manuals/:id/variants/:variant/:lang/html` answer `text/html`: a complete page (`preview/`: `api/`, `core/` actions `PreviewModuleAction` and `PreviewManualAction`, the renderer behind the `HtmlRenderer` port) around the fragment `@openfolia/render-html` writes. Only successes are HTML: every error, a 406 included, is a JSON:API error document, and carries none of the page's headers.

- **Reads like every other read.** `content.read`, `?ref=` (a branch, tag or commit ID, a change set's branch for its author: authors preview their own drafts, #126), the reader's scope and audience, through `ReadableContent`. A module or manual the reader may not see, one that is missing, a language or variant that does not exist and a ref that is not theirs answer alike (404). The manual route resolves exactly as the PDF and the resolved manual do (only published variants); the module route resolves the module on its own with `ReadableContent.resolveModule`: the conditions inside it apply for `?variant=`.
- **Parameters.** `ref`, `theme` and, for a module, `variant` (a slug, `model=large,software=2.3`, as in `GET /manuals/:id`; optional: none set, so a condition on a variant attribute does not hold; an attribute or value the configuration does not hold is a 422 `unknown-variant`). Any other is a 400 `unsupported-parameter`; `Accept` that excludes `text/html` is a 406.
- **Theme.** `theme=auto` (default) links the dark values (`/preview/theme-dark.css`, the theme's `.dark` block as `:root` values) with `media="(prefers-color-scheme: dark)"`, so the page follows the viewer without a script; `light` and `dark` force one (`dark` sets `class="dark"` on `<html>`), for a host with its own switch, such as the editor.
- **Direction.** `<html lang dir>` is the manual's or module's language and its direction (`directionOf`: the script's, or `languages.direction` in `openfolia.yaml`, #458; an entry there for a language not configured does nothing and is reported by `GET /config` in `meta.directionIssues`, code `unknown-direction-language`, #396); the fragment inside carries its own per element. A translation job's XLIFF names both languages' directions on each `<file>` (`srcDir`, `trgDir`).
- **Links are relative to the page's URL** (`../../../preview/render-html.css`, `../../../assets/<sha256>?ref=<ref>`), so they resolve wherever the API is mounted, e.g. behind the editor dev server's `/api` proxy, and every request goes to the origin that served the page. Images are `GET /assets/:sha256` at the same `?ref=` as given (none given: none sent), so the asset route's scope applies to them; a branch that moves between the page and its images shows the newer image or none, never another reader's. Safety symbols with artwork are `<img>`s of `GET /symbols/:id` at the same `?ref=`, the configured label as their `alt`; a symbol without artwork is left out (the PDF prints a grey placeholder).
- **Stylesheets and fonts** come from `@openfolia/theme` and `@openfolia/render-html`, read once at start (`PackagePreviewFiles`: only names in its map are served, never a path from a request): `theme.css`, `theme-dark.css`, `render-html.css`, `page.css` (the page's own background) and `fonts/*.woff2`. They need the token like every route (the same bytes for everyone, so no permission), `private, no-cache` with an ETag of the bytes.
- **Security headers.** `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; script-src 'none'; object-src 'none'; style-src 'self'; style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self'; font-src 'self'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'self' <CCMS_EDITOR_ORIGINS>`: no script of any kind and nothing from another origin. Inline styles are allowed as **attributes only**, because the renderer writes `style="width:NN%"` on images and table columns (without it the layout falls back to automatic widths); there is no `<style>` element and no `style-src 'unsafe-inline'`. `frame-ancestors` is this origin (a dev server proxying the API is "self") plus the origins in `CCMS_EDITOR_ORIGINS` (comma-separated, validated as plain origins, since the value goes into a header); `X-Frame-Options` is not sent, as it cannot name an allow-list. Also `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`.
- **Caching** as every content route (and as `GET /assets/:sha256`): `private, max-age=31536000, immutable` for a commit-ID `?ref=`, else `private, no-cache`, with the reader-scoped ETag and a 304 on `If-None-Match`; `X-Ccms-Commit` and `X-Ccms-Issues` (the issue count the reader may see) as for the PDF.
- **Speed:** the e2e test (`tests/preview.e2e.spec.ts`) holds a module preview, cold, to under one second on the sample repository.
- **Live, from the working copies** (#237): `?workingCopy=true` on the module preview, both hidden-content lists, a manual's read (`GET /manuals/:id`, a derived manual gathered from the working copies, its own rule included, #669), the manual's HTML and its PDF reads the change set's working copies (what its editor autosaved, not committed yet) in place of their files: `?ref=` must name the change set's branch (`change-sets/<id>`, else 400 `invalid-parameter` on `workingCopy`). Every working copy of the change set stands in, a module's and a manual's (its manifest) alike, so a module reference resolves to its own working copy where there is one, else to the branch (`ContentOverlay` and `ReadableContent.withOverlay` in `content/core`, `OpenWorkingCopiesAction` and `@ReadsWorkingCopies()` in `change-sets`; `standIn` of `@openfolia/content-repo`'s `resolve`). Only for the change set's author, answered as reading a working copy is: 403 `forbidden` for anyone else who holds `content.read.draft`, 404 for anyone who does not (exactly as a missing change set). A working copy stands in only where its author may read it (`draftReadPermission`, as `GET …/working-copy`), and everything read through it is filtered by the reader's scope as files are; the same conditions and variant resolution apply. Images: one uploaded into the change set links to `GET /change-sets/:id/assets/:hash` (its author's), any other to `GET /assets/:hash?ref=` as before; the PDF reads only images the branch shows the reader and those uploaded into the change set, so a working copy naming any other hash reads nothing. `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`, never the commit's ETag nor a 304 for it (the working copies change under the same commit), and nothing cached for the branch is shared with it (the reading key carries the change set). `false` (the default) reads the branch as committed.
- **What is hidden** (#81): `GET /modules/:lang/:id/hidden-content?variant=` and `GET /manuals/:id/variants/:variant/:lang/hidden-content` list, as JSON:API and paged, what the conditions leave out of the preview of the same module or manual (resolved the same way, with the same parameters besides `theme`, the same 404, 400 and 422): `HiddenContent` resources with `attributes` `kind` (`block`, a variant block of a module; `module`, a module entry; `group`, a group of the manual), `module`, `blockId` (the variant block's own ID) and `condition`, and `meta` `label` (plain text: a block's first text, at most 120 characters, or the module's or group's title) and `expression` (the condition as written, `model in (large)`). Only the outermost hidden content, each once, in document order (`HiddenContent` of `@openfolia/content-model`), and only what the reader could read: a module entry outside their scope or above the manual's audience is never named. A module without a `?variant=` hides every variant block, since no condition on an attribute holds. `meta.commit` names the commit read. The editor's preview (screen 11) lists them.

## Search

The `search` component (`src/search/`): `GET /search?filter[q]=` finds modules by title, keywords, FAQ questions and text, manuals by title and terms (one result per term and language) by their preferred variant (as the title), their allowed and forbidden variants (as keywords) and their definition (as text), at `?ref=` like every content read (a branch, a tag, a commit ID, a change set's branch for its author), among what the reader may see.

- **Port** `ContentSearch` (`core/content-search.ts`), two adapters chosen by `CCMS_SEARCH_ADAPTER`: `PostgresContentSearch` (`postgres`, the default, #54; see "Postgres search" below) and `InMemoryContentSearch` (`memory`: word matching alone, in each server's memory, for small installs), fake `FakeContentSearch` (`tests/fakes/`). Both work on `ReadableContent`, never the raw snapshot, so they index only what their actor may read, and both pass one contract (`tests/content-search-contract.ts`: case and diacritics, deprecation, excerpts and offsets, the order of title, keyword, question and text matches, filters, paging, terms, and the reader's scope).
- **Query:** `filter[q]` is trimmed, 1 to 200 characters (else 400 `invalid-parameter`; at most 8 words are used). Case and diacritics do not matter (`ä` is `a`, `ß` is `ss`), a word matches anywhere in a title, keyword or text, and every word has to match: in the title, or each in one of the keywords, or all in one FAQ entry's question (a field of its own, #151), or all in one block's text. Paged like every list; any other query parameter is 400.
- **Filters** run before paging and ranking, on what the reader may read: `filter[lang]` (modules and terms of one language), `filter[resourceType]` (`Module`, `Manual` or `Term`, lower case too), and the module filters shared with `GET /module-summaries` (`content/core/module-filter.ts`, parameters in `content/api/module-filter.params.ts`): `filter[type]` (a module type), `filter[audience]` (the level a module counts as: its own, or the last, most restricted level when it names none, as resolution and the access check place it, #699; a module naming a level the configuration no longer lists counts as none), `filter[facet]=<facet>:<value>` and `filter[keyword]` (an exact keyword, case and diacritics aside), the last two repeatable (at most 32 each), every value required. A module filter leaves manuals and terms out, and with one `filter[q]` may be left out: then every module it lets through is a result (`matchedOn: filter`, the title as excerpt), ordered by title. Without either, 400 `invalid-parameter`. Values are checked for their form only (a configuration key, a keyword of 1 to 64 characters without a comma): one the configuration does not know, or that only content outside the reader's scope has, finds nothing, so neither the status nor the total says what lies outside the scope. **Deprecated:** `filter[type]=module` or `manual` without `filter[resourceType]` still means the resource type, as before `filter[resourceType]` existed; with `filter[resourceType]` given, `filter[type]` is always a module type.
- **Results** are `SearchResult` resources, one per module and language, manual, or term and language (`id` `term:<lang>/<termId>`; its `blockId` is always absent, its title the preferred variant): `attributes` `resourceType` (`Module`/`Manual`/`Term`), `resourceId` (`<lang>/<moduleId>`, the manual ID or the term ID), `lang` (modules and terms), `title`, `blockId` (the block, list item or step the text matched in, or the FAQ entry whose question matched; absent unless the text or a question matched), and `relationships.resource`. `meta` holds `matchedOn` (`title`, `keyword`, `question`, `text`, or `filter` without a query), `excerpt` (**plain text**, never markup: the title, the matching keywords separated by `, `, the matching question, or at most about 160 characters of the matching block around the first match) and `matches` (`{ start, end }` offsets into the excerpt in UTF-16 units, in order, not overlapping), so a client highlights by slicing text and never needs HTML.
- **Order:** the title equals the query, starts with it, has a word starting with it, contains it; then keyword matches (a keyword equal to the query first); then FAQ question matches (a word starting with it first); then text matches (a word starting with it first); ties by title, then type, language and ID, so paging is stable. That is the in-memory search's order; the Postgres search keeps the order of the kinds and ranks within each by how well the lists agree ("Postgres search").
- **Scope:** results never include content outside the reader's scope and `meta.page.total` counts only visible results, so neither says what lies outside it (`search.e2e.spec.ts` checks both, and that a narrow reader and a wide one on the same commit do not share results).
- **Index (in memory):** built from `ReadableContent` on first use, per commit and reading key (the scopes the reader sees with), and kept in memory: at most 16 indexes (`INDEXES_KEPT`), least recently used dropped, each about twice the size of the text the reader may read; a failed build is not kept. The sample repository answers a cold search in well under 300 ms (a test holds it). The Postgres search keeps the same entries per commit and reading key (`search-entries.ts`, shared), for its word list, its excerpts and its final scope check.

### Postgres search

`PostgresContentSearch` (`repos/postgres-content-search.ts`, #54) adds to the word matching above what a word index can do, without a search cluster: Postgres is there already.

- **Four lists, fused.** (1) **Words** anywhere in a field, exactly as the in-memory search finds them (partial words such as `gerät` in `Ladegerät`, stop words, symbols); (2) **full text**: a `tsvector` per part of a module (its head, i.e. title and keywords, then each block and FAQ question), stemmed by the module's language (`text-search-configs.ts`: the primary language tag to one of Postgres' configurations, `simple` where none fits; manuals `simple`), title weighted A, keywords B, a question C, text D, each word of the query of 3 or more characters a prefix (`'start':*`), a shorter one a whole word, all of them in one part, ranked by `ts_rank_cd`; (3) **typos** in titles and keywords: `pg_trgm` word similarity of each query word of three or more letters, at least 0.45 (`TYPO_SIMILARITY`); (4) **meaning**, with an embedder: the parts whose embedding is closest to the query's (cosine, at least `CCMS_EMBEDDER_MIN_SIMILARITY`). Each list holds at most 500 documents (`CANDIDATES`); rows a list cannot tell apart share a rank. **Order:** by what matched (`title`, `keyword`, `question`, `text`, then a hit only a typo found, then `meaning` alone: a hit no word matched), then by reciprocal rank fusion of the lists (1 / (60 + rank), summed), then by title, type, language and ID, as before. `meta.matchedOn` is the best of what matched (a typo hit says `title` or `keyword`, where the typo is); a `meaning` hit has no `matches`, its excerpt is its closest part's text (the title for the head). A full-text match shows where its stemmed words are (`ts_headline` over the folded text, mapped back to the original: `starts` for `starting`).
- **Rows by content** (`repos/search-index.tables.ts`, migration `0045_search_index`, which also creates `pg_trgm`): `search_documents` (one per module and language, manual, or term and language, keyed by `<scheme>:<config>:<SHA-256 of what is matched>`, with what the filters ask: kind, language, module type, audience, facets, keywords), `search_blocks` (its parts, with their `tsvector` and the hash of their text), `search_texts` (the text to embed, by hash), `search_embeddings` (by model and text hash, `real[]`). An unchanged module is the same row at every commit, so a new commit (a release, a save point) indexes only the modules it changed, and a text is embedded once per model whatever document holds it.
- **Scope** (`search_scopes`, `search_scope_members`): which documents a reader may find at a commit is written once per commit and reading key (a SHA-256 digest of both; it names no reader), from `ReadableContent`, the first time such a reader searches; drafts and change-set branches alike, by the same draft rule as every read. Every query joins it first and the filters are a `where` on the documents, so nothing outside the scope is ranked, counted or paged; every row a query answers is checked again against the reader's entries and the filters in the server. **Member sets** (#643, migration `0058_search_scope_sets`): a scope is one row naming its member sets (`search_scopes.sets`); a set (`search_member_sets`) is one bucket of the scope's documents, by the hash of each document's key into about √n buckets (a power of two, at most 1,024; `memberSets`), held as an array of document numbers (`search_documents.id`, never reused) and stored once by a hash of its sorted keys, so a commit shares every set with the commit before it but those of the buckets its changes fall in, and two readers whose scopes are cut into as many buckets (scopes of about the same size) share a set whenever their documents in that bucket are the same; a scope whose size crosses a power of four is cut anew. The sets of a scope are disjoint and together exactly its documents; a query reaches a document only through them, each set looked up by its key (`offset 0`, so the plan never scans every set however small the statistics say the table is). On the `large` profile a commit's scope is 1 row and 256 sets (about 80 documents each), 0.7 MB with its indexes, where it was 20,400 rows and 8.8 MB. Writing a scope locks the sets it reuses and the documents of the sets it writes until the scope names them, and writes again any document or set the sweep dropped since, so a search never fails on a sweep running beside it. The GIN indexes the sweep asks (which scope names a set, which set names a document) are built without the pending list (`fastupdate = off`), which every lookup would otherwise read in full. The sweep drops old scopes, then the sets no scope names, then the documents no set names (below).
- **Cost of a query:** the ranking is one transaction: one full-text query per configuration of the index (one statement over all of them measured slower: a `tsquery` from a join loses the index on `b.tsv`, 86 ms against under 1 ms for short words on the `large` profile), then typos, then meaning, and the excerpts one more, all within the time budget below; the query becomes at most 8 words of letters and digits (`queryTokens`), passed as parameters (never SQL, never `tsquery` syntax); only words of 3 or more characters are prefixes in the full text (`MIN_PREFIX_LENGTH`: `'a':*` matches nearly every part; the word list still finds a shorter word anywhere); each list capped. The hits of recent searches are kept per index for paging (8), for good once the index's text is all embedded; before that, a first page ranks afresh (so what the job embeds meanwhile is found) and a later page uses its first page's ranking for a minute (`UNSETTLED_KEPT_MS`, so the pages neither overlap nor miss a hit); never when the query could not be embedded, and a ranking made while pgvector was off is not used once it is on. A statement cut off is a 503 `database-timeout` with `Retry-After`. After writing 1,000 or more new documents at once the server runs `analyze` on the search tables, so a fresh index is not planned as empty. Any text indexes: what goes to `jsonb` is made well formed and loses U+0000, and the embedded text is cut at a character, never inside one.
- **Limits** (#539). **Time:** a search's statements share one budget, `CCMS_SEARCH_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 5 s), counted from its first statement (`SearchBudget`): before each, `statement_timeout` is set to what is left (at most 5 s, `STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_MS`), and none starts once it is spent, so however a query is built its statements hold a connection no longer than the budget (the embedding check `#settled` included; the query's embedding is asked for before, holds no connection, and takes at most the budget too, with at most `CCMS_SEARCH_CONCURRENCY` asked for at once per server (another waits up to 2 s, then the search goes on without meaning); so a search answers within about 2 s + the embedding + 2 s + the budget); past it, 503 `database-timeout` with `Retry-After` (the whole answer, never partial results: a partial page would page and count differently from the next request's, and the same code clients already retry on). Not counted: writing a commit's scope the first time it is searched, and the check that it is there (shared by its readers, two builds at once), and preparing pgvector (once per server). **At once:** at most `CCMS_SEARCH_CONCURRENCY` searches (default 4, well under `CCMS_DATABASE_POOL_MAX`) run their statements at once on a server; another waits up to 2 s (`SEARCH_WAIT_MS`), with at most 4 per running search waiting, then answers 503 `search-busy` with `Retry-After`. **Per reader:** `CCMS_SEARCH_PER_MINUTE` searches per minute (default 120), per person or service account, on both adapters (`SearchContentAction`, a sliding window, the style review's `SlidingWindowLimit`), checked before anything is searched, so it also bounds a reader's embedder calls, and at most 2 of a reader's searches in progress at once (`SEARCHES_PER_READER_AT_ONCE`, also 429), so one reader cannot take every slot from the others; past it, 429 `rate-limited` with `Retry-After`. All three hold per server process, in memory: N servers let a reader search N times as often and run N times as many searches at once (the pool is per server too).
- **Embedder** (`Embedder` port, `core/embedder.ts`): off by default (`NoEmbedder`): no semantic search, and nothing is sent anywhere. `CCMS_EMBEDDER_URL` with `_MODEL` and `_DIMENSIONS` turns it on (`HttpEmbedder`): any endpoint taking `{ model, input }` and answering `{ data: [{ embedding, index }] }`, a model run beside the API or a hosted one. **Then module text leaves the server**: the indexed text (titles, keywords, blocks, FAQ questions; what readers searched, drafts for their authors too) and each query. At most 32 texts of 4,000 characters per request, `CCMS_EMBEDDER_TIMEOUT_MS` per request, redirects refused (the text and the key go to that URL only), answers read up to 16 MiB and checked (one finite vector of the dimensions per text); errors and logs name a status at most, never the URL, the key or the text. The key also as `CCMS_EMBEDDER_API_KEY_FILE` (the images' entrypoint). A search whose query cannot be embedded goes on without meaning (logged).
- **Embedding** (`search.embed`, `EmbedSearchTextsAction`): a background job, enqueued when a search indexed new text, as a server starts, and at most every 5 minutes by a search that finds text of its index not embedded (the job gave up, or the text came as it finished), embeds every text some document still uses without an embedding of the model (a swept draft's text, or an old commit's, is never sent, also not to a new model), 32 at a time, walking the texts by hash (`hash > last`: one pass over any backlog, and another from the start when it embedded something, for text indexed behind it, at most 3), each batch stored as it comes (an attempt cut off keeps what it did; a text swept meanwhile gets none). pgvector is needed only then: the server creates the extension on the first semantic search (another server creating it at once is fine), then an HNSW index for the model (`vector(<dimensions>)` over the stored arrays, partial by model) in the background, built `concurrently` so the job's inserts never wait for it (one a cut-off build left invalid is dropped and built again), the meaning list scanning until it is there; with pgvector 0.8 or later the index scan goes on past rows outside the scope (`hnsw.iterative_scan`). Without pgvector it logs that, searches without meaning, and tries again 10 minutes later (deploy/README.md, "Semantic search").
- **Sweep and retention** (#540, `sweep()`, `SearchIndexSweeper`): every hour (the first 5 minutes after start, the next a minute later when a run's bounds stopped it and no step failed; after a failure, an hour later) on each process that runs background work (`CCMS_ROLE` `all` or `worker`), with a grace period of `CCMS_SEARCH_SWEEP_AFTER_DAYS` (default 7): (1) scopes no reader searched for that long, one per transaction, each followed by the member sets no scope names any more (a batch per transaction, locked, checked again and deleted; one a scope being written holds is skipped), then a walk of 10 windows of 10,000 sets by ID for a set left unnamed so (a run cut off, a set held while its scope went); (2) documents no scope names that are as old, with their parts, and then, once that committed, the texts only they used marked unused (`search_texts.unused_since`); (3) a window of unmarked texts no part uses marked too, whatever left them so (two servers dropping documents that share a text at the same moment, a text locked while it was checked): a walk by hash from a random point per process, so every text is reached; (4) texts unused for the grace period, with their embeddings of every model (a text a part uses again is unmarked instead; indexing a marked text unmarks it, under its row lock, so it is kept); (5) a window of embeddings whose text is gone (a text swept while being embedded); (6) the embeddings of a model no server has had configured for `CCMS_SEARCH_MODEL_KEPT_DAYS` (default 7; each server records its model as it prepares semantic search, hourly while it searches by meaning, as it stores embeddings and as it sweeps: `search_embedding_models`), logged, then the model's vector index. **How long deleted text is kept:** the text of a deleted draft, or of content changed since an old commit, stays while a scope names its document, i.e. until a week after any reader last searched a commit with it, then a week more as unused text, with its embeddings: about two grace periods after it was last searched, then it is gone from the database. After turning the embedder off or changing the model, the old model's embeddings go after `CCMS_SEARCH_MODEL_KEPT_DAYS`. **Bounded per run:** each batch selected first by an index, then worked on by key (a plan that does not grow with stale statistics), 1,000 rows per statement, at most 200 scopes and the sets naming 1,000,000 documents, 10 windows of 10,000 rows per walk, new statements for 60 s (`SWEEP_RUN_MS`), each statement within 30 s and waiting at most 5 s for a lock; rows another transaction holds are skipped; a step that fails is logged and the next runs; what is left is the next run's. Measured on PGlite with a synthetic index of the `large` profile's size: see the PR of #540.
- **Rebuildable:** derived from Git and the embedder; `index-rebuild` empties the scopes, documents and parts but keeps the texts and embeddings, marked unused, so indexing again embeds only text that is new and the sweep drops what is not indexed again within the grace period; `index-rebuild -- --drop-embeddings` drops the texts and every embedding too, and **everything is embedded again** (every searched text sent to the embedder again, paid for again with a hosted one). The next search of a commit writes it again: a scope missing is always written with a check of which documents the database has (a document swept meanwhile is written again), whatever the server knew.
- **Tests:** PGlite runs `pg_trgm` and pgvector (`@electric-sql/pglite-pgvector`), so every list runs in the tests: the contract and the Postgres-specific specs (word forms, typos, query text as data, rows by content, meaning with a concept embedder, `tests/fakes/concept-embedder.ts`), and `semantic-search.e2e.spec.ts` (the app's `HttpEmbedder` against a local endpoint, the job, the reader's scope).

## Terms

The `terms` component (`src/terms/`): the terminology as content (#135), one concept per file (`terms/<id>.yaml`, the content model's `Term`: status, domain, a deprecated term's `replacedBy`, per language the preferred, allowed and forbidden variants, a definition as inline content and a usage note). Read at `?ref=` like every content route, with `content.read`; terms carry no audience, so every reader of the ref reads every term in every language. Edited only through a change set (`term/<id>` subjects, `…/terms/:termId/working-copy`; see "Change sets"), deleted the same way (a working copy deletion).

**Term links** (#245): a module's text links a term with the `term` mark. The checks report a link to a term that does not exist or cannot be read (`unknown-term`, blocking) and to a deprecated one (`deprecated-term`, a warning, naming `replacedBy`), at the block it is in. A commit that deletes a term still linked, by any module in any language as the commit leaves the head (`term-usage.ts`, read past the reading filter like the configuration's check), is refused: 422 `term-in-use`, one error object per link the actor may read (`source.pointer` `/module/<lang>/<id>/blocks/<block>`, `meta.subject`, `meta.at`, `meta.term`, at most 100), and links in modules they may not read as one error object without a pointer, naming only the terms. The previews and the PDF link each term to its glossary entry (render-html, render-typst), the manifest's `glossary: true` adding the glossary.

- **`GET /terms`**: `Term` resources by ID. `attributes` hold the stored term (`schemaVersion`, `status`, `domain`, `replacedBy`, `languages`, the definition as inline content: text runs with marks, and line breaks), `meta.issues` what `validateTerm` finds (`unknown-language`). Filters run before paging, every one given has to hold: `filter[lang]` (terms holding the language; `filter[q]` then looks in it only), `filter[status]` (`proposed`, `approved`, `deprecated`), `filter[domain]` (case, diacritics and runs of white space aside) and `filter[q]` (1 to 200 characters after trimming: part of a preferred, allowed or forbidden variant or of the definition's text, folded as `GET /search` folds; matched as one string, without stemming). Without a filter, a file in `terms/` that is no term is listed (`UnreadableTermResource`, `meta.error`), so it does not hide; a filter leaves such files out. Paged like every list; any other query parameter is 400.
- **`GET /terms/:termId`**: one `Term` (`TermDetailResource`) with `meta.usage.modules` and `meta.linkedIn` (#245): the module files (each language apart) whose term links name it, by title, at most 50 named (`usage` counts them all), from the module index (`terms` on its rows, migration `0033_module_index_terms`, which drops the commits indexed before so they are indexed again), only those the reader may see; 404 `not-found` when there is no file, 422 `invalid-content` for a file that is no term, 400 for a malformed ID. `export` is no term ID (the content model reserves it), so `GET /terms/export` is always the export.
- **`GET /terms/export?format=tbx|csv&lang=`** (part of the delivery API): every term at the ref, by ID, as a download (`Content-Disposition: attachment`, `terms.tbx`, `terms-<lang>.csv`, …), with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `X-Ccms-Terms` (how many terms it holds). `format=tbx`: TBX-Basic (ISO 30042:2019, DCA style, `application/x-tbx+xml`; the mapping is the content model's `termsToTbx`, "Term export" in its README); `format=csv`: one row per term and language (`text/csv`, `termsToCsv`; cells a spreadsheet would run as a formula start with `'`: a formula sign, ASCII or full width, as the first character other than whitespace, or a leading tab or carriage return). `lang` keeps one language (a term without it is left out). Files that are no term are left out. A file, not JSON:API, but its errors are: 400 `invalid-parameter` for no or an unknown `format` or a malformed `lang`, `unsupported-parameter` for any other query parameter. The export writes only; there is no TBX import (yet), so no XML is ever parsed from a request.
- **Search**: terms are a result type of `GET /search` (see "Search").
- Not yet: term links in text and a glossary (#245), the Terminology screen (#246), suggestions while writing (#247), the terminology check (#55). Deleting a term that modules still link is refused once links exist (#245).

## Page designs

The `page-designs` component (`src/page-designs/`, #390): how a manual's pages look, as project files (`design/<id>.yaml`, the content model's `PageDesign`: page size and margins, colours, fonts, logo, title page fields, the preset's regions with translatable text and placeholders, cover images per variant, the back page, the running header and footer, headings, body, tables, steps, notes and the safety layout; "Page designs" in its README). A manual names its design in its manifest (`design`), the configuration one for every manual naming none (`pdf.design` in `openfolia.yaml`); without either, a manual gets the built-in design, which is no file. Read at `?ref=` like every content route, with `content.read`; designs carry no audience, so every reader of the ref reads every design. Edited only through a change set (`design/<id>` subjects, "Change sets").

- **`GET /page-designs`**: `PageDesign` resources by ID, paged like every list. `attributes` hold every setting as the file holds it, defaults filled in; `meta.issues` what `validatePageDesign` finds (an unknown placeholder, language, region or variant attribute, too little contrast against white, fonts not confirmed for embedding, a missing cover image or manufacturer: `severity`, `code`, `message`, `at` a dotted path, `block` a region's block). A file in `design/` that is no design is listed too (`UnreadablePageDesignResource`, `meta.error`). Any query parameter but paging is 400.
- **`GET /page-designs/:designId`**: one `PageDesign`, with its assets checked against the blob store as well, else the repository's older layout at that commit (`assets/<sha256>`, `ReadableContent.pageDesignAsset`, #554) (`pageDesignAssetIssues`: `missing-asset` for a logo, font, cover image or region file not stored, `invalid-font` for a font file that is no TrueType, OpenType, WOFF or WOFF2 file, told by its first bytes); 404 `not-found` when there is no file, 422 `invalid-content` for a file that is no design, 400 for a malformed ID (`builtin` included: the content model reserves it).
- **Asset references**: the files a design at a commit names count as used (`AssetReferenceIndex` reads `design/` beside the module files), so deleting an unused file never takes a design's logo or font (migration `0041_page_design_subjects` drops the commits indexed before, so they are indexed again).
- **In the PDF** (#391): resolving a manual (`ReadableContent.resolve`) resolves its design for the variant and language too (`ManualResolution.design`, `resolvePageDesign`: texts in the manual's language, placeholders but the page ones filled); the built-in design and a design that does not exist (`designIssues`: `unknown-page-design`) give the default PDF as before. `TypstPdfRenderer` passes it to `renderTypst` and compiles with the preset's template (`designTemplate`, `@openfolia/render-typst`'s README, "Page designs"), unless the repository has its own `templates/manual.typ`, which wins and gets the design as data. A logo or cover image the blob store does not hold is left out (the design's own checks report `missing-asset`), so the PDF still renders; the design's fonts are loaded only when the design confirms they may be embedded. The design is part of the render key (`renderKey`), so a working copy's design change makes a new PDF at the same commit. A release's PDF (`RenderReleaseOutputAction`) passes the release's facts (`releaseFacts`: its `revision` as the release, its `publishedAt` as the date, #395), which fill the design's `release` and `date` placeholders and title page fields; a preview or a download leaves them out. Page images and the manual builder's preview are the same PDF's. What the layout cannot fit is logged (the render log) and, through `PdfRenderer.layoutWarnings`, a warning in the manual's checks: `page-design-overflow` (a title or back page region clipped to its slot, `params.at` the region) and `running-head-truncated` (a running header or footer shortened with an ellipsis, `params.at` and `params.text`). A designed manual's checks render its PDFs for that even with `pdf.standard: none`; one that cannot be rendered then is a `pdf-layout-not-checked` warning (`params.reason`), so the checks never look clean by omission (#554). A PDF that PDF/UA-1 refuses was laid out first: its layout warnings come with the refusal (`PdfAccessibilityError.warnings`) and are reported beside the `pdf-accessibility` checks (#554).
