A new way in is ended by adding it to `WaysIn`. **A code is bound to the address it was mailed to** (#646, `email_sign_in_codes.sent_to`, migration `0057_email_code_sent_to`): the code and its link work only while that address (ignoring case) is still the person's, so one that reached an old address is dead whatever path changed it, including a request for a code that read the person just before an address change and stored its code just after `WaysIn` ran. Storing a code checks the address too, under a share lock on the person's row: a code whose address stopped being theirs meanwhile is neither stored nor mailed. **A request for a code and the ending of ways in take turns** (#650): the request holds the person's row (`EmailCodes.hold`, a share lock) before it reads them, and ending their code (`EmailCodes.revokeAll`, which every `WaysIn` call runs) takes that row too (`FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, as an `UPDATE` of it would). So a password set or changed, a deactivation or a new address either waits for a request already holding the person, and then ends the code it stored, or is waited for, and the request reads the person after it. A code asked for before a password change therefore never outlives it, whether or not the person had a password before (a first one, through an invite, has no password row to lock, which is why the lock is on the person). Every change that ends ways in takes the person first, then their links, then their code (`WaysIn.holdPerson`, `EmailCodes.holdToEnd`; setting a password through a link holds the person before it takes the link, changing one's password before the password, and a first provider sign-in that claims the person before it closes their invite), so two of them never wait for each other in a cycle. **A sign-in by email code, or by its link, takes the person first too** (#660, `EmailCodes.holdToEnd`), then their code, then their invite: before, it took the code and then the invite while setting a password through that invite took the invite and then the code, and the two deadlocked. Every transaction that takes a person's ways in now takes the person first, so whichever of the two comes second waits and then finds the invite (or the code) gone, and is refused as usual. `no key update` keeps the person's row out of any deadlock with a session started at the same moment: a new session's foreign key to the person only needs a key share. A request whose person's address changed between finding them and holding them mails nothing. `src/access/repos/email-code-ways-in.postgres.spec.ts` runs the two at once on a real server when `CCMS_TEST_PG_URL` names one (PGlite runs one transaction at a time).
- **Trying it locally**: `node backend/api/scripts/mock-oidc.ts` runs a test provider (no passwords; `MOCK_OIDC_*` settings in the file) that `tests/sign-in.e2e.spec.ts` and the editor's browser flows (`specs/sign-in.spec.ts`) sign in through.

## Branding

The `branding` component (`src/branding/`, #169): what an administrator sets in place of the neutral defaults (`frontend/editor/src/docs.config.ts`, `frontend/theme`), without a code change or a redeploy. Operational state in Postgres (migration `0043_branding`), never in the content repository. Separate from a manual's page design (#390), which is content: the logo a PDF prints comes from the content repository's templates per release, so a release stays reproducible; the app's branding changes only the editor's shell.

- **Fields** (`core/branding.ts`), each null for its default: `productName` (1 to 80 characters, no control characters), the images `logoLight`, `logoDark`, `mark` (the collapsed sidebar), `favicon` and `appIcon` (both square), as `sha256:<hash>` of an image uploaded for the branding, and the colours `primary` and `accent` (`#rrggbb`).
- **`GET /branding`** is public (`@Public()`: the shell reads it before sign-in; it holds nothing sensitive): `Cache-Control: public, no-cache` and an ETag (a hash of the document), 304 for `If-None-Match`. `meta.tokens` holds the theme tokens the colours give, light and dark, each `#rrggbb`; `meta.contrast` every pair checked, with its ratio.
- **`PATCH /branding`** (`admin.branding`): the fields given, null for the default (all null: reset). Locks the one row (`branding`, made on the first change), so changes happen one at a time; audited as `branding.update` with each field changed, from and to (a name, hashes, colours: nothing personal). A change that changes nothing writes and audits nothing. 422 per problem (`source.pointer` the field): `unknown-image` (a hash not uploaded for the branding), `image-not-square` (favicon, app icon), `insufficient-contrast` (`meta.theme`, `meta.pair`, `meta.ratio`, `meta.required`).
- **Colours** (`core/brand-colours.ts`, pure): the primary colour is used as given on light pages (buttons, links, the focus ring, the selected item's icon) with white or dark text on it, whichever reads better; for dark pages the same hue and chroma made lighter, at least to OKLCH lightness 0.75 and until it reads on the dark page and its cards. The hover and selection highlight is a light tint of the primary colour; the light sidebar's surface and line a tint of the accent (else of the primary colour). Checked against the theme's own surfaces (`THEME_SURFACES`, which a test keeps equal to `frontend/theme/src/theme.css`): text 4.5:1 (primary on the page and cards, the text on the primary colour, the theme's text and muted text on the tints), shapes 3:1 (the primary colour on the sidebar), in light and dark; a failing pair refuses the change. Only the tokens of `BRAND_TOKENS` are ever set: surfaces, text, status and the safety colours (ISO 3864 / ANSI Z535) are never brandable.
- **Images:** `POST /branding/images` (`admin.branding`, `multipart/form-data` with one part `file`, `X-Ccms-Request`), read whole into memory (at most 1 MiB, 422 `too-large`, reading stops there), the type from the bytes: PNG or WebP (SVG is refused, not sanitised, as for content images; 422 `unsupported-image-type`), at most 4096 pixels a side (422 `image-too-large`), and one still picture (#559): the chunks are walked to the end (`imageFrames`, `assets/core/image-frames.ts`), an animated PNG (`acTL`, `fcTL`, `fdAT`) or WebP (`VP8X`'s animation flag, `ANIM`, `ANMF`) is 422 `animated-image` (the pixel cap holds per frame), and a file not well formed up to its end is 422 `unsupported-image-type`. Stored in the blob store under its hash and recorded in `branding_images` (hash, type, size, width, height, who and when) with a `branding.image-upload` audit event; the same image again is 200 with the first record (its upload time moved to now). Recorded under the branding's row lock and then the file's hash lock (`AssetUploads.lockHash`, #623), which every blob delete takes while it checks what holds the bytes, once the blob is still there (503 `upload-interrupted` otherwise). **The cap:** at most 200 images in use or uploaded within a day (409 `too-many-uploads`, refused before reading and again under the row lock); an older unused one no longer counts and is swept. **The storage limit** (#559): counted as media once per hash (`StorageQuota.addMedia`, in the recording transaction under the hash's lock) unless a content upload records the same bytes already, and a content upload of bytes the branding holds counts nothing; no room left is 409 `storage-limit-reached` before reading, and so is an image past the room left, audited `storage.upload-refused` (`data.target` `branding`). A refusal after the bytes were stored removes them again when this upload stored them and nothing took them on meanwhile (a content upload, another branding upload, an import, a rendition, a release's file).
- **Unused images** (#559): `GET /branding/images` (`admin.branding`, paged, last uploaded first) lists the uploads with `meta.usedIn`, the fields naming each. `DELETE /branding/images/:hash` (`admin.branding`, 204) removes one no field names, however recent (409 `branding-image-in-use`, `meta.usedIn`; 404 when not uploaded), and `BrandingImageSweeper` (two minutes after start-up, then hourly, in a `worker` or `all` process; `SweepBrandingImagesAction`, at most 100 a run) removes those unused for a day, as `service:branding`. Both go through `BrandingImageRemoval`: the record goes, audited `branding.image-delete` (`sha256`, type, size, dimensions, `fileKept`), and the bytes too unless a content upload records them, they are an import's bundle, a rendition or a release's file (`StorageQuota.apartFromMedia`), or content refers to them anywhere (`AssetReferences`: modules and page designs at every branch and tag, open change sets, working copies). That is decided by the protocol of deleting an unused file: under the branding's row lock and the hash's lock the bytes are marked deleted (`AssetUploads.markDeleted`, so no working copy naming them saves meanwhile), then where they are used is read, then under both locks again everything is decided again, the record removed, the bytes deleted (and taken off the storage used) or kept, and the mark cleared. A `PATCH /branding` naming the image meanwhile is seen under those locks (409 `branding-image-in-use`), and so is a content upload of the same bytes (they stay); the same image uploaded again for the branding moves its upload time, so the sweep keeps it another day. When another deletion of the same bytes held the mark and ended meanwhile, nothing changes (503 `branding-image-busy`; the sweep tries again next time). A failure leaves the image as it was. The sweep stops between images at shutdown and after 5 minutes.
- **Serving:** `GET /branding/images/:hash` is public and serves only a hash a field of the branding names now (an upload not in use, a content image, anything else: 404, like a missing one), as the type recorded at upload, with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, the sandboxing `Content-Security-Policy` of the asset routes and `Cache-Control: public, no-cache` with the hash as ETag and a 304 on `If-None-Match` while it is in use (a removed logo is not kept by caches). Not `GET /assets/:hash`: that route is behind the token and serves only what a module the reader may see refers to.

## Installation settings

The `settings` component (`src/settings/`, #588): the installation's own settings, operational state in Postgres (`installation_settings`, one row, made by the first change; migration `0049_self_approval`), changed at once by holders of `admin.roles`. Never the project configuration (`GET /config`, `openfolia.yaml` in Git), which changes in a change set: a team of one could never approve the change set that turns self-approval on.

- **`selfApproval`**: who may approve their own change set (`never`, `review`, `review-and-sign-off`; "Review workflow").
- **`GET /settings`** and **`PATCH /settings`** (both `admin.roles`; type `InstallationSettings`, id `installation`): what is left out stays; an unknown setting or value is 400. The row is locked, so changes happen one at a time; each setting changed is audited as `settings.<setting>` with `from` and `to` (`settings.self-approval`, subject `settings/installation`); a change that changes nothing records nothing. The workflow reads the rule through its own port (`SelfApprovalRule`), which this component provides (`StoredSelfApprovalRule`).

## Audit log

The `audit` component (`src/audit/`): every change of state is recorded with who, when, what and why, in the same transaction as the change.

- **Recording:** `AuditLog.record(tx, actor, { action, subject: { type, id }, comment?, data? })` inside `UnitOfWork.run` (see "Database"), so an action that fails after recording leaves no event. `action` is `<subject>.<verb>` in lowercase keys (`role.assign`); `subject.type` a lowercase key (`role`, `module`, `change-set`) and `subject.id` its API ID; `data` a JSON object of the change's details (at most 65,536 characters as JSON; no `__proto__` key). Text with U+0000, which Postgres cannot store, is refused too. Only the actor's kind and ID are stored, never its roles; never put tokens or personal data beyond IDs in `comment` or `data`. A malformed event throws, rolling the change back.
- **Table** `audit_events` (`repos/audit.tables.ts`, adapter `DrizzleAuditLog`): `id` (identity, increasing in insertion order, which is not always commit order: not a cursor for following the log), `at` (the transaction's start), `actor_kind`, `actor_id`, `action`, `subject_type`, `subject_id`, `comment`, `data`, `tx_id` (the recording top-level transaction, `xid8`, for the sink's order; set by the trigger `audit_events_stamp` whatever an insert gives, so no caller can place an event ahead of the cursor), with the formats checked by the database too. No foreign keys: an event outlives the user or role it names. **Append-only in the database:** a statement trigger refuses every `UPDATE` and `TRUNCATE` (migration `0002_audit_events`), and `audit_events_delete_guard` every `DELETE` but retention's (migration `0050_audit_sink_retention`), so no code path, buggy or not, can rewrite history; there is no update or delete path in code either, only the call of `audit_events_prune` (below). Only the table's owner could drop or disable the triggers: for that too, run migrations as the owning role and the API as a role set up as in "Database roles" below.
- **Reading:** `GET /audit-events`, newest first, paged, with `filter[subject]` (`<type>` or `<type>/<id>`, e.g. `module/en/battery-charging`), `filter[actor]` (`<kind>` or `<kind>/<id>`, e.g. `user/sample-author`), `filter[action]`, and a time range: `filter[from]` (inclusive) and `filter[to]` (exclusive), ISO 8601 with an offset. It needs `admin.roles` (403, `forbidden`, otherwise) until the open question on who may read the log says otherwise.
- **Export** (#379): `GET /audit-events/export?format=csv|jsonl` takes the same filters and holds exactly the events the list pages through for them, newest first, as a download (`attachment`, `nosniff`, `Cache-Control: no-store`). CSV: RFC 4180, a header row, CRLF, the columns `id`, `at` (ISO 8601 UTC), `actor kind`, `actor id`, `action`, `subject type`, `subject id`, `comment`, `data` (JSON text), every cell through the shared formula guard (`csvCell`: a value a spreadsheet would run starts with `'`). JSON Lines (`application/jsonl`): one event per line, `id` and the list's attributes. **Streamed:** `ExportAuditEventsAction` hands out `AuditLog.stream`, which reads keyset batches of 500 below the last ID as the client takes them, so the log is never in memory whole and an event recorded meanwhile neither shifts nor repeats one. The first batch is read before the answer begins (a database failure then is a JSON:API error); a failure later cuts the connection, so a broken download never looks complete. **Audited:** before anything is sent, the export itself is recorded and committed as `audit-log.export` (subject `audit-log/audit-events`, `data: { format, filter }` with the filter values given); when that fails the export is refused (500), so nobody takes the log out unseen. Same permission as reading; 400 for a missing or unknown `format`, a malformed filter or any other parameter.
- **Sink** (#379): `CCMS_AUDIT_SINK_URL` copies the log, as it grows, to an HTTPS endpoint or a syslog server over TLS (settings: `.env.example`, `deploy/README.md`, "Audit log sink and retention"). Port `AuditSink` (`core/audit-sink.ts`), adapters `HttpsAuditSink` and `SyslogAuditSink`, run by `AuditSinkConsumer` in a `worker` or `all` process (`SendToAuditSinkAction`) at start-up and every `CCMS_AUDIT_SINK_POLL_MS`.
  - **Order, at least once:** events go in `(tx_id, id)` order: increasing `id` within one transaction, the transactions in the order they got their ID (their first write), and only events of transactions older than every one still running (below `pg_snapshot_xmin(pg_current_snapshot())`), so an event that commits late is never stepped over (the outbox's rule). The cursor (`audit_sink`, one row: the last `(tx_id, id)` sent) moves by compare-and-swap after a delivery succeeded; it is in the database, so a restart goes on from there. A failed delivery keeps the cursor, and the same batch goes again after 30 s, doubling to at most 10 min, for as long as it takes: never out of order, never skipped. An event can arrive twice (a retry after a delivery the receiver took but did not answer, a lease that ran out): receivers dedupe by `id` together with `at` (a resend has both the same; after a restore an `id` can come again with another `at`, see "After a restore"). Several servers share the work by a lease on the row (`claimed_by`, `claimed_until`, three times the timeout and at least a minute): one sends at a time. A sink set on an installation with history sends the whole log first, oldest first.
  - **HTTPS:** each batch (up to 100 events) is one `POST` of `{"events": [...]}`, each event as a JSON Lines export line holds it (`id` first), `application/json`, signed like a webhook: the header `X-Ccms-Signature: t=<unix seconds>,v1=<hex>`, HMAC-SHA256 over `<t>.<body>` keyed with `CCMS_AUDIT_SINK_SECRET` (verify as in "Webhooks"). A 2xx is a delivery; anything else, a redirect too (none is followed), a failure. No connection is reused, the attempt is cut off after `CCMS_AUDIT_SINK_TIMEOUT_MS`, only the status line is read. The certificate is checked (against `CCMS_AUDIT_SINK_CA_FILE` when set). The operator sets the URL, so unlike a webhook's it may be a private address.
  - **Syslog over TLS** (`syslog+tls://host[:port]`, port 6514 by default): RFC 5425 framing (the message's length in octets, a space, the message) of RFC 5424 messages, one per event, over one connection per batch: `<110>1 <at> <host> openfolia <pid> <action> - <BOM><event as JSON>` (facility log audit, severity informational; MSGID the action, cut to 32 characters). The certificate is checked as for HTTPS. Syslog has no acknowledgement: a batch counts as delivered once it is written and flushed to the connection, so an event the collector drops after that is not sent again. Collectors often cut long messages (8 KiB by default in some): raise their limit, as an event's `data` may be up to 64 KiB.
  - **Batches** hold up to 100 events and about 1 MiB of them as JSON (a larger single event goes alone). A sink that keeps refusing a batch (a collector's size limit, say) holds the cursor there, and retention removes nothing past it: the log says so on every try.
  - The APP-NAME and the `User-Agent` come from `SINK_APP_NAME`, the header from the webhooks' `SIGNATURE_HEADER`: one constant each. Logs name the error (`timeout`, `connection`, `http-status 503`) and the wait, never the URL, a header or an event.
- **Retention** (#379): `CCMS_AUDIT_KEEP_DAYS` (1 to 36,500; unset: kept for ever) removes events recorded more than that many days ago, at start-up and daily (again after 10 minutes when a run removed all it may: a backlog), in a `worker` or `all` process (`PruneAuditLogAction`), 5,000 per call and at most 20 calls per run. With a sink set, never an event it has not sent yet. Each call is one call of the database function `audit_events_prune(before, max_rows, kept_tx, kept_id)`, which deletes, records the removal as `audit-log.prune` (actor `service/audit-retention`, subject `audit-log/audit-events`, `data: { before, removed, firstId, lastId }`) in the same transaction, and answers the count; it refuses a `before` less than 23 hours back (a day, with room for clocks apart), whoever calls it, and refuses to run past a sink position that lies ahead of the database or while the sink is `blocked`. The function is `SECURITY DEFINER` (it runs as its owner, with its `search_path` pinned to `pg_catalog`, the schema, `pg_temp`) and sets a transaction-local flag (`ccms.audit_prune`) that `audit_events_delete_guard` lets through. **The flag is a second line only:** any session can set a setting of that name itself. The guard that holds is the grants: the API's role has no `DELETE` on the table, only `EXECUTE` on the function.
- **Database roles** (recommended; with one role owning everything, as `deploy/compose.yaml` runs it, the triggers and the function's checks are the guard). Migrations run as the owner; the API as a role of its own:

  ```sql
  -- as the owner, after the migrations; ccms_app is the API's role
  GRANT SELECT, INSERT ON audit_events TO ccms_app;          -- never UPDATE, DELETE or TRUNCATE
  GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION audit_events_prune(timestamptz, integer, xid8, bigint) TO ccms_app;
  ```

  The migration revokes `EXECUTE` on the function from `PUBLIC`, so a role not granted it cannot remove anything. The API needs its usual rights on every other table (`audit_sink` included: `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`).

- **After a restore** onto another database server (a new stack from a backup): transaction IDs start again there, so the restored `tx_id`s and the sink's cursor lie ahead of every new transaction. Reading on would skip the new events, so the sink refuses: it sends nothing and logs an error, and `audit_events_prune` refuses to remove anything. Once found, this holds (`audit_sink.blocked`) until the recipe below clears it, even after this server's count has caught up. Run it once, as the owner, best right after `pg_restore` and before the first start, else with the API stopped, as a script (`psql -f` or on stdin: `VACUUM` cannot run inside the transaction a single `psql -c` makes). Every event takes transaction 1, in ID order (so the unsent restored ones go in ID order, not their old transaction order: the one exception to the order above); the cursor stands before the first restored event not sent and never past the old one, so events recorded since the restore (higher IDs: the identity sequence is restored) are sent too, and at worst some are sent again:

  ```sql
  BEGIN;
  UPDATE audit_sink SET last_tx_id = '1',
    last_id = LEAST((SELECT min(e.id) - 1 FROM audit_events e
                     WHERE (e.tx_id, e.id) > (audit_sink.last_tx_id, audit_sink.last_id)),
                    audit_sink.last_id)
    WHERE last_tx_id <> '0';
  UPDATE audit_sink SET blocked = false;
  ALTER TABLE audit_events DISABLE TRIGGER audit_events_append_only;
  UPDATE audit_events SET tx_id = '1';
  ALTER TABLE audit_events ENABLE TRIGGER audit_events_append_only;
  -- The identity sequence came back at the backup's value: the old stack may have recorded
  -- (and sent) higher IDs since. Put it past the highest ID the collector holds.
  SELECT setval(pg_get_serial_sequence('audit_events', 'id'),
                GREATEST(<highest id the collector holds>, (SELECT max(id) FROM audit_events)));
  COMMIT;
  VACUUM audit_events;
  ```

  A restore drill (a second stack from a backup, `deploy/README.md`, "Restore drill") must not send to the production collector: leave `CCMS_AUDIT_SINK_URL` unset there and skip the recipe.

  Needed whenever a sink is set then, or later (with the cursor at the start, the restored events lie ahead too); without a sink ever, nothing is. The restore (`pg_restore --no-acl`) also drops the grants: run the "Database roles" `GRANT`s and `REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION audit_events_prune(timestamptz, integer, xid8, bigint) FROM PUBLIC` again. The outbox's consumers (notifications, webhooks, the change feed) need the same once: "Outbox", "After a restore".

- **Upgrading to `0050_audit_sink_retention`:** it adds `tx_id` with the default `pg_current_xact_id()`, which is stable, not volatile: Postgres stores the value once (`attmissingval`) and does **not** rewrite the table (the upgrade spec checks the table's file is unchanged), and no scan. Every existing event takes the migration's own transaction ID, so the sink sends them first, in ID order. The pending migrations run in one transaction, so the `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock the column takes is held until they commit, through the build of the two new indexes (`(tx_id, id)` and `at`, without `CONCURRENTLY`; seconds per million events): meanwhile reading the audit log waits, and a write that records an event fails with 503 after `CCMS_DATABASE_WRITE_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS`. On a very large log, upgrade in a quiet moment.
- **The actor** comes from the request: `@CurrentActor()` (`base/api/current-actor.decorator.ts`) reads `request.actor`, which `ActorGuard` sets (see "Users, roles and permissions"), and answers 401 when nothing set it. In e2e tests `actAs(actor)` (`tests/test-app.ts`) stands in for the resolution, after the token check.

## Change sets

The `change-sets` component (`src/change-sets/`): a change set (shown as "revision" in the editor) is a draft of content changes on its own Git branch; Postgres holds its operational state.

- **Subjects** (#253): what a change set locks, edits in working copies, commits and lists as changes. Six kinds, each one entry of the registry `SUBJECT_KINDS` (`core/subject-kinds.ts`: its file's path, what a working copy holds, how it is validated, printed and read, who may change it), so a later kind is one entry, not a change across locks, working copies, save points and diffs:
  - `module` (`{ type: "module", lang, moduleId }`, `modules/<lang>/<id>.adoc`) and `manual` (`{ type: "manual", manualId }`, `manuals/<id>.yaml`), as below;
  - `config` (`{ type: "config" }`, `openfolia.yaml`; named `config` in the API, stored with the ID `openfolia.yaml`): the working copy is the whole `ProjectConfig`, sent as `StrictProjectConfig` (an unknown member at any level is a 400), printed canonically (`printConfig`: comments and the file's own layout are not kept). Changing it needs `content.edit` through a role whose scope restricts nothing **and** `admin.roles` (`checkConfigChange`; 403 `forbidden` for its lock, its working copy and its save point otherwise). A change that would make content on the branch invalid is refused (below); it is never deleted (422 `not-deletable`);
  - `term` (`{ type: "term", termId }`, `terms/<id>.yaml`, the content model's `Term`): needs `content.edit` through one role covering everything in each language the term holds, as committed and as saved (its scope restricts nothing but languages, `allowsAllInEach`); a term with nothing committed is locked by any editor whose role restricts nothing but languages, and checked once its content names its languages; a committed file that is no term only by a role covering every language. Its languages must be configured (`unknown-language`, an issue of the draft, refused at the save point).
  - `design` (`{ type: "design", designId }`, `design/<id>.yaml`, the content model's `PageDesign`, #390; `…/page-designs/:designId/working-copy`): the working copy is the whole design with every setting's default filled in (an unknown member is a 400), printed canonically (`printPageDesign`). Changing it needs `content.edit` through a role whose scope restricts nothing (`checkDesignChange`, no `admin.roles`: a design decides no role); deleting it is a working copy deletion. A save point (or a revert taking out a design the change set added) that deletes a design the configuration (`pdf.design`) or a manual (`design`) still names, as that commit leaves them, is 409 `design-in-use` (`designUsages`, #554): `meta.designs`, and `meta.usedIn` the places (`{ in: "config", design }`, `{ in: "manual", design, manual }`; a manual the actor may not read is `{ in: "manual", design }` without its ID, once per design; at most 50, `meta.more`); every manifest counts, read past the reading filter. Its issues are `validatePageDesign`'s, `at` a dotted path into the design (a region's block after `#`, `regions.footer.en#doc`); an error is refused at the save point. The files it names (logo, fonts, cover images, a region's images and attachments) follow the rule for a module's files ("Working copies"), a file a committed design names counting as one the editor may read.
  - `template` (`{ type: "template", templateId, path }`, `templates/<templateId>/<path>`, one text file of a design's own template, #396; `…/page-design-templates/:templateId/files/working-copy?path=`): the working copy is the file's text (`{ text }`, at most 256 KiB), written as it is. Changing it needs what a design needs (`checkDesignChange`), as a template lays out every manual whose design names it; its path follows the content model's template file rules, checked in the database too (migration `0062_template_file_subjects`). Its diff names no fields.
  - Terms, page designs and the configuration have no audience: every reader of a branch reads them, so their locks, working copies and changes are visible to all who may read the branch. Review treats `openfolia.yaml`, `terms/` and `design/` as files outside every module's scope (only a role restricting nothing covers them, "Review workflow"); a change to `openfolia.yaml` needs every sign-off there is, a term or page design change only `review`.
- **Configuration usage check** (`configUsages`, `core/config-usage.ts`): a configuration that takes something away (a language, a module type, an audience, a facet or value, a variant attribute or value or an attribute's kind, a text attribute or a safety symbol; labels, order, additions and archiving are never checked, `narrows`) is checked against every module, manifest and term at the branch head, read past the reading filter: each validation error it causes that the old configuration does not is a usage (a term in a removed language included). Saving the working copy refuses them (422 `config-in-use`, one error per usage, at most 100: `source.pointer` `/module/<lang>/<id>/blocks/<at>` or `/manual/<id>/blocks/<at>`, `meta.subject`, `meta.issue`, `meta.at`), so an editor learns at once, and the save point checks again against the head as the commit leaves it (the files it writes or deletes in place of the committed ones). The check at saving reads committed files only, not the change set's other working copies: a configuration that only fits once a module's working copy is committed is saved after that save point. **Merging** checks the tree the merge makes (`Workflow.mergeUsages`): the change set's files and, unless it changed `openfolia.yaml`, the base's configuration, so a value removed on one side and used on the other since the change set started is 422 `config-in-use` and the change set stays approved but unmerged. **Taking a file back out** (`reverts`) checks the files put back against the configuration the change set keeps (and the head's files against the base's configuration when that goes back). The way out for a variant value in use is to archive it (`archived: true`): it stays valid, and `GET /variants` marks it so pickers hide it.

- **Table** `change_sets` (`repos/change-sets.tables.ts`, migration `0003_change_sets`): `id` (a UUID version 7 made by the server; its first 48 bits are the creation time), `title` (one line, 1 to 200 characters), `description` (up to 10,000), `author_kind` and `author_id` (the actor, as the audit log names it), `base_ref` (the branch it started from and will merge back into), `base_commit` (where that branch was then), `base_released` (whether that commit was released content then; migration `0007_change_set_base_released`, #126), `branch`, `state` (`draft`, `in-review`, `approved`, `merged`, `abandoned`; see "Review workflow"), `review_head` (the commit submitted for review; set exactly while in review, approved or merged) and `merge_commit` (set exactly once merged; both migration `0009_change_set_workflow`), `created_at`, `updated_at`. The formats are checked by the database too; `branch` must be `change-sets/<id>`.
- **Branches** are `change-sets/<id>`, named by the server, never by a client. They are created at the base branch's current commit with `GitWriter.createBranch` (compare-and-swap: "must not exist") and read like any branch (`?ref=change-sets/<id>`). `GitChangeSetBranches` refuses any branch outside that namespace. `baseRef` must name a branch (not a tag, a commit or a change set's branch), else 422 `invalid-base`; it defaults to `CCMS_DEFAULT_REF`, or to the branch `HEAD` names when that is `HEAD` (a default that names no branch is 422 too, asking for `baseRef`). A base whose commit is not released (see "Released refs") also needs `content.read.draft`; without it, it is refused exactly like a branch that does not exist (`ReadableRefs`, `content/core/readable-refs.ts`), so the answer does not tell whether a draft branch exists. A released base needs only `content.edit`, so an editor can tell whether a branch at a released commit exists. A refused default is not named in the answer (it may be the branch `HEAD` points to).
- **`POST /change-sets`** (`content.edit`) takes `{ data: { type: "ChangeSet", attributes: { title, description?, baseRef? } } }` (`application/vnd.api+json`; an `id` or any other member is 400 `invalid-body`) and answers 201 with the `ChangeSet` and `Location`. **Git first, then the database:** the branch is created, then the row and the `change-set.create` audit event are written in one transaction. When that transaction fails, or the process stops between the two, the branch is left without a change set. It is not deleted on the spot (a commit that reported a failure may still have landed); instead `ReconcileChangeSetBranchesAction`, run by `ChangeSetReconciler` a minute after start-up and every ten minutes after, removes change-set branches that have no row and are older than an hour by the time in their ID, only when their head is in the history of a branch or tag outside `change-sets/` (a branch holding commits of its own is left, and logged as an error for a person to look at), after asking the database once more, each by compare-and-swap on the commit just seen; a run looks at no more than 100 orphans, and each history walk gives up after 30 s (shutdown waits at most 5 s for a running pass). Names under `change-sets/` that are no change-set ID are logged and left alone.
- **`GET /change-sets`**: newest first, paged, `filter[state]` and `filter[author]` (a user or service ID). **`GET /change-sets/:changeSetId`**: one; an ID that names none, malformed or not, is 404 `not-found`. A holder of `content.read.draft` reads every change set; anyone else (any actor) only their own (#126: authors always see their own work): another author's is not listed (their `filter[author]` lists nothing) and is 404 exactly like a missing one (#125).
- **A branch that is gone** (#317: deleted outside the API, or the content repository restored or replaced). Both routes above say so in each change set's `meta.branchMissing` (`ChangeSetBranchPresence`, derived from Git, never stored). A read at its branch (`?ref=change-sets/<id>`) answers 404 **`change-set-branch-missing`** (`meta.branch`) instead of the generic `not-found`, but only to a reader who may see that change set as `GET /change-sets/:changeSetId` finds it (`AuthoredBranches.visibleChangeSet`); for anyone else, and any other ref that does not resolve, it stays `not-found`, so it tells nobody a change set exists. It is a `NotFoundError` still, so everything that treats a missing ref as not found does so. Routes of the change set that open its branch by name answer the same 404 `change-set-branch-missing` (taking a lock, saving or reading a working copy, a save point); those that read its head by commit keep their 409 `branch-missing` (changes, checks, comments, submit). The branch is **never recreated**: the person leaves the change set, or abandons it; an admin (`admin.roles`, with `content.read.draft`) may abandon another author's change set once its branch is gone (`data.branchMissing` on the event), nobody else's. The reconciler does not fail on it: `FindMissingChangeSetBranchesAction`, its last step, finds the open change sets whose branch is gone (one read of the branches, then keyset pages of 100, at most 10 pages per state, within the pass's budget) and the reconciler logs each once per process (again only once it came back and went again); the commits step no longer reports such a branch as a failure.
- **`POST /change-sets/:changeSetId/abandon`** (`content.edit`, and only the change set's author: 403 `forbidden` for anyone else who holds `content.read.draft`, until the open question says otherwise; for an actor without it another author's change set is 404, exactly like a missing one, #125), with no body or `{ meta: { comment } }`: an open change set (`draft`, `in-review`, `approved`) becomes `abandoned`, with a `change-set.abandon` audit event (the comment, `data: { from, to }`) and an outbox row on the same topic (keyed by the change set: it is abandoned once), in one transaction that locks the row from the check to the change; any other state is 409 `invalid-state` with `meta.state`. Nothing is deleted in Git: the branch stays readable. The change set's locks are released in the same transaction, each with a `lock.release` audit event (`data.reason: "change-set.abandon"`), and its working copies are dropped (`data.workingCopies` on the abandon event counts them when there were any; see "Working copies").
- **`PATCH /change-sets/:changeSetId`** (`content.edit`, only the change set's author, answered for anyone else as abandon is, #125) takes `{ data: { type: "ChangeSet", id, attributes: { title?, description? } } }` (`description: null` clears it; any other member is 400 `invalid-body`; an `id` other than the path's is 409 `id-mismatch`) and answers 200 with the `ChangeSet`. Only an open change set (`draft`, `in-review`, `approved`; else 409 `invalid-state`). One transaction holds the row from the check to the change and writes a `change-set.edit` audit event, `data: { from, to, descriptionChanged }` with the old and new title (when it changed) and whether the description changed (its text is not kept in the append-only log); a value that is the same changes nothing and is not audited (#267).
- Request bodies are read as JSON:API (`setUpRequestBodies`, `base/api/request-bodies.ts`, up to 1 MB); other media types are 415. A body-less `POST` (`abandon`, a transition without a comment) still sends `Content-Type: application/vnd.api+json` ("Cross-site requests").

## Locks

Part of the `change-sets` component: two people never change the same module or manual at once. A lock is Postgres state, never Git.

- **Table** `locks` (`repos/locks.tables.ts`, migration `0004_locks`; the subject kinds of #253 in `0015_project_file_subjects`): `id` (a UUID version 7 made by the server, new for every lock taken), `subject_type` (`module`, `manual`, `config`, `term`, `design` or `template`; `template` in `0062_template_file_subjects`) and `subject_id` (`<lang>/<moduleId>`, the language in its canonical case, the manual's ID, `openfolia.yaml` or the term's ID: the audit log's names), `change_set_id` (references `change_sets`), `holder_kind` and `holder_id` (the actor that took it), `since` (the taking transaction's time). **One lock per subject**, a unique index: of two transactions taking the same subject at once, exactly one gets it; the other waits for it and is refused. The formats are checked by the database too. The subject need not exist: a change set locks a new module's ID to create it.
- **`POST /change-sets/:changeSetId/locks`** (`content.edit`) takes `{ data: { type: "Lock", attributes: { subject } } }`, `subject` being `{ type: "module", lang, moduleId }`, `{ type: "manual", manualId }`, `{ type: "config" }`, `{ type: "term", termId }`, `{ type: "design", designId }` or `{ type: "template", templateId, path }` ("Subjects" under "Change sets"), and answers 201 with the `Lock` and `Location`, with a `lock.acquire` audit event in the same transaction. Only the change set's author takes locks for it (403 otherwise; for an actor without `content.read.draft`, another author's change set is 404 exactly like a missing one, #125), only while it is a `draft` (409 `invalid-state` with `meta.state`). **Scope** (#121), the rule saving a working copy applies to the committed file (`checkCommittedScope`, `core/subject-scope.ts`), on the change set's branch: a committed module must lie within a role holding `content.edit` (language, type, audience, facets) and be one the author reads; a committed manual's audience within a role holding `content.edit` and one they read it with, naming no module they may not see; a subject with nothing committed (or a broken file) only for an author who edits and reads all of its language (a module) or manuals of every audience. Every refusal is the same 403, so a hidden subject and a missing one answer byte for byte alike. The new content is checked again when it is saved. Since the check reads the change set's branch, taking a lock needs what saving does: `content.read` (its author opens their own branch without `content.read.draft`, #126). The change set's row is locked from the state check to the end, so abandoning it cannot leave a lock behind. A subject this change set holds already is 200 with its lock (nothing changes, nothing audited). One another change set holds (the same person's too) is **409 `locked`**, with the holder in `meta` for an actor who may know of that lock (one `GET /locks` lists to them): `lockId`, `holder` (`{ kind, id }`), `changeSetId`, `since`, and, while that change set is there (#472), `changeSetTitle`, `changeSetState` and `hasChanges`: whether it has changes to the subject, a working copy of it or a commit changing its file (a module's translation record too) between its base commit and its branch's head (`LockHolding`; a branch that cannot be compared counts as changed). An actor who may know of the lock may read its change set anyway (a draft reader reads every one; anyone else knows only their own locks). Anyone else gets a bare `locked`. One holder holds at most 1,000 locks across their change sets (409 `too-many-locks`, `meta.max`; counted under a per-holder advisory lock, so two change sets cannot race past it). A subject locked and released again under the request more than three times running is 409 `lock-contended` (try again).
- **`GET /locks`** (as change sets: any actor; without `content.read.draft`, only the caller's own locks, within their `content.read` scope on their own branch, #126): newest first, paged, `filter[holder]` (a user or service ID), `filter[changeSet]` (an ID), `filter[subject]` (`module/<lang>/<moduleId>` or `manual/<manualId>`: "who holds this one?"). **`GET /locks/:lockId`** reads one. Only locks a reader may know of are there: its subject, as committed on its change set's branch, within their `content.read.draft` scope (a subject with nothing committed only for a reader of all of its language, or of manuals of every audience); the list is filtered before it is paged, so totals count only those, and a lock outside the scope is 404 exactly like a missing one. A reader whose role restricts nothing is listed by the database alone; anyone else's locks are checked one by one (the language first, in the database, then the content, a few at a time), which opens the branch as a read does and so also needs `content.read`; a branch that cannot be opened or read hides its locks. A `Lock` has `attributes` `subject`, `holder`, `since` and its change set in `relationships.changeSet`.
- **`DELETE /locks/:lockId`** (`content.edit`, the holder only: 403 for anyone else who may read the lock, 404 exactly like a missing lock for anyone who may not) releases it: 204, with a `lock.release` audit event. With **`?ifUnchanged=true`** (#472: the editor frees a module from another of the holder's change sets), only while the lock's change set has no changes to the subject, as `hasChanges` above reads them: else **409 `has-changes`** (`meta.changeSetId`) and the lock stays. The working copy and the branch's head are read again inside the transaction that holds the lock's row `FOR UPDATE`; a save and a save point hold that row `FOR SHARE` until they are written, so neither slips in between the check and the release (a branch moved since the first read is compared again). Any value but `true` or `false` is 400 `invalid-parameter`. **`POST /locks/:lockId/break`** (`admin.roles`) with `{ meta: { comment } }` (required) removes someone's lock: 204, with a `lock.break` audit event holding the comment and the holder, and a `lock.break` outbox row (keyed by the lock) that tells the holder (see "Notifications"). Both name the lock by its ID, so a lock released and taken again meanwhile (a new ID) is 404, never removed by mistake.
- **`POST /locks/break`** (`admin.roles`) with `{ meta: { comment, changeSet } }` or `{ meta: { comment, holder: { kind, id } } }` (exactly one of the two; the comment is required) removes every lock of a change set, or of a holder who left or lost `content.edit`, in one transaction and answers 200 with `{ meta: { count } }` (0 when there was none; 404 for an unknown change set). **One `lock.break` audit event per lock** (the comment, `data.lockId`, `changeSetId`, `holder` and `bulk`: `change-set` or `holder`), as breaking one and abandoning audit, so a subject's audit history stays complete under `filter[subject]`. One `lock.break` outbox row per change set whose locks it broke (its subjects and count), so each holder is told once per change set. Bounded by the holder cap (1,000), which a change set's locks come under too. It cannot race with taking a lock: by change set it locks the change set's row (as acquire does), by holder it takes the per-holder advisory lock acquire counts under; a lock taken after it is a new one and stays.
- **Lifetime:** a lock lasts while its change set is open; there is no automatic expiry. It ends when its holder releases it, an admin breaks it, or its change set is abandoned or merged (`LockStore.removeAllOf` in that transaction, `data.reason` `change-set.abandon` or `change-set.merge`). Submitting and approving keep the locks: nobody else changes those modules while they are reviewed, and a returned change set goes on with them. Audit events of locks name the module or manual as their subject (`filter[subject]=module/en/battery-charging` on `GET /audit-events` shows its lock history), with `data.lockId` and `data.changeSetId`.

- **Contention** (#149): every transaction that holds or removes several locks takes their rows in lock-ID order (`LockStore.holdAll`, `removeAllOf`, `removeAllHeldBy`: a save point, a bulk break, an abandon), so two of them never wait for each other in a cycle. A transaction Postgres still breaks off for contending with another (a deadlock, `40P01`, or a serialization failure, `40001`: `ContendedError`, raised by the unit of work) is run once more by the lock actions (`runContended`: acquire, release, break, bulk break, abandon), then answered 409 `lock-contended`, never 500; any other action passes it on as that 409 directly (a save point too, which writes to Git inside its transaction and so is never run again by itself; it runs again from its preflight only when something it read moved). `src/change-sets/repos/lock-contention.postgres.spec.ts` shows it on a real server (PGlite runs one transaction at a time): it runs when `CCMS_TEST_PG_URL` names one (see `.env.example`), else it is skipped.

## Working copies

Part of the `change-sets` component. Routes: `/change-sets/:changeSetId/modules/:lang/:moduleId/working-copy`, `/change-sets/:changeSetId/manuals/:manualId/working-copy`, `/change-sets/:changeSetId/config/working-copy`, `/change-sets/:changeSetId/terms/:termId/working-copy` and `/change-sets/:changeSetId/page-designs/:designId/working-copy`, each with `PUT` (save), `GET` (read) and `DELETE` (discard). For the configuration and terms, see "Subjects" under "Change sets": who may change them, and the usage check a configuration save runs. The editor autosaves every few seconds into a **working copy** in Postgres, so nothing typed is lost and Git only gets meaningful commits (a save point commits working copies to the change set's branch; see "Save points"). A working copy is Postgres state; reads with `?ref=` on the change set's branch keep showing what is committed.

- **Table** `working_copies` (`repos/working-copies.tables.ts`, migration `0005_working_copies`): one per change set and subject (primary key `change_set_id`, `subject_type`, `subject_id`; the subject as locks name it), `schema_version` (the content model's, today `1`, and equal to `content.schemaVersion`), `content` (`jsonb`: the module's tree, the content model's `Module`, the manual's manifest, which may have no entries yet, the `ProjectConfig` with a `schemaVersion` member added for storage (`CONFIG_SCHEMA_VERSION`, 1; the file has none), or the `Term`), `base_commit` (the branch commit the first save started from; later saves keep it), `version` (1 for the first save, one more for each), `updated_by_kind`/`updated_by_id`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. The formats are checked by the database too. A stored copy is read back through the current content model's schema: one it does not read (once the schema version moves) is a 500 naming it, never content passed on unchecked; a model migration has to migrate stored working copies with the files.
- **`PUT …/modules/:lang/:moduleId/working-copy`** and **`PUT …/manuals/:manualId/working-copy`** (`content.edit`) take `{ data: { type: "WorkingCopy", attributes: { content } }, meta: { version, baseCommit } }` and answer 200 with the `WorkingCopy` at its next version. The shape is checked (400 `invalid-body` with a pointer; bodies nest at most 128 levels, `MAX_BODY_DEPTH`, so a recursive schema never exhausts the stack), the content must name the path's subject (422 `subject-mismatch`) and is at most 512 KiB of JSON (422 `too-large`, `meta.max`); content holding a table past the content model's caps (1,000 columns, 50,000 cells: rows × columns, or a module's tables 1,000,000 cells together, #686; a module or a page design's region blocks) is 422 `table-too-large`, one error per such table (or one at `blocks`, or `regions`, for the total) with its `source.pointer` and the caps in `meta.columns`, `meta.cells` and `meta.totalCells`, never stored (a table that large would be laid out by every reader of it); its other validation issues come back in `meta.issues` without refusing it (a draft may be incomplete). Only the change set's author, while it is a draft (409 `invalid-state`), and only while the change set holds the subject's lock (409 `lock-required`), held `FOR SHARE` until the save is written so it cannot be released meanwhile. `meta.version` is the version last read or saved (0: none yet); any other is **409 `version-conflict`** with the current one in `meta.version`, so a stale tab never overwrites a newer save (compare-and-swap in the database). `meta.baseCommit` is the commit the content is based on: the `baseCommit` last read or saved (for a subject not on the branch yet, the change set's `baseCommit`). The stored base, or the branch head, is kept as it is; an older commit is accepted when the subject's file is the same there as at the head (the head is stored then; for an author without `content.read.draft`, who opens no draft commit by its ID, an older commit counts when it is one of their branch's own, from its base commit on, at most 1,000 back, and the branch's diff since decides, #126), else the save is **409 `stale-working-copy`** (`meta.head`: read the file there, and save with it once the content takes it into account). So content edited on an old file (a tab that read before another tab's save point) never silently replaces the newer file; the save point checks the same. A change set holds at most 1,000 working copies (409 `too-many-working-copies`, `meta.max`; a working copy outlives its lock, so the lock limit alone does not bound them). **Scope**: 403 with one answer whatever the reason, so a refusal tells nothing about what the author may not see (a missing module and a hidden one answer byte for byte alike). The committed file is looked up past the reading filter (`ReadableContent.moduleMetaForCheck` and `manifestForCheck`, for checks only, never in a response), so a hidden file is checked, never skipped as missing. A module's new content and its committed file must lie within a role holding `content.edit`, and the author must read the committed file. A manual's new and committed audience must lie within a role holding `content.edit` (`allowsManual`) and the committed one within what the author reads, and the committed manifest must name no module they may not see (worked out from the manifest itself, not from the `outside-scope` notes only draft readers get), since saving what they see would drop the rest. A subject with nothing committed (or a broken file) is written only by an author who edits and reads all of its language (a module) or every audience (a manual): for anyone else, a new subject and a hidden one would otherwise answer differently. **No commit and no audit event per save**: the commit at a save point is audited.
- **Files it names** (#335, `ReadableAssets` in `assets/core/readable-assets.ts`, the rule imports use too, #332): a module's image and attachment blocks may name only a file the editor may read already. That is one a module they may see uses, on the change set's branch (what it refers to already, and the base it started from) or at its base ref now (`workingCopyFilePlaces`, `core/file-scope.ts`; a base they may not open adds nothing); or one uploaded into a change set they are the author of (`AssetUploads.uploadedForAuthor`, the scope of the author's preview). Working copies never count, so one draft cannot vouch for another's file. Any other hash (another author's upload, an import's uploaded bundle, a file only content outside their scope uses, bytes nobody stored) is 422 `unknown-asset` (`meta.assets`), the same answer whatever the store holds. The blob store is never asked, so the answer does not tell whether a file exists, and nothing out of scope reaches the branch, `GET /assets/:sha256?ref=change-sets/<id>` or the `missing-image` and `missing-attachment` checks. A save point checks the same again.
- **`GET`** (`content.read`): the working copy, else the file committed on the branch (`version` 0, `meta.saved` false, `baseCommit` the commit read), with validation issues. Only for the change set's author (403 for anyone else who holds `content.read.draft`; for an actor without it another's change set is 404, exactly like a missing one, as #125 asks of change-set routes); a working copy outside the author's draft-reading scope (for an author without `content.read.draft`, their `content.read` scope, #126) is 404 like a missing one, and the committed file is read through `ReadableContent` (a manifest names only the modules they may see). A manual's working copy is the author's own text and comes back as saved.
- **`DELETE`** (`content.edit`, the author only, answered for others as `GET` is): 204, the committed file stays; no lock needed (it clears up after a broken lock too); 404 when there is none. Not audited.
- **Lifetime:** until discarded, committed (a save point drops it) or the change set is abandoned (in the abandon transaction) or, once that exists, merged. Releasing or breaking a lock leaves the working copy, which cannot be saved again without the lock.

## Save points

Part of the `change-sets` component. **`POST /change-sets/:changeSetId/commits`** (`content.edit`) commits the change set's working copies to its branch: with no body every working copy, or `{ data: { type: "Commit", attributes: { subjects?, deletes?, message? } } }` (subjects as locks name them; `deletes` removes modules and manuals in the same commit; a module's translation record in that language, `modules/<lang>/<id>.translation.yaml`, goes with it, #287, so a module created there again starts with none). When to call it is the client's choice (closing a module, submitting, every few minutes); the server has no timer.

- **One commit**, through `GitWriter` (compare-and-swap on the branch head read), with the actor as author and committer (`<id> <<id>@<kind>.openfolia.invalid>`: the ID, no personal data) and the trailers `Change-set: <id>` and `Actor: <kind>/<id>`. The message is the client's or names what changed. Each file is printed canonically (`printModule`, `printManifest`, `printConfig`, `printTerm`: the subject kind's printer) at the path made from the subject key (never from the content's `id` or `meta.lang`), and must read back to itself (`print(parse(text)) === text`). Answers 201 with a `Commit` resource (`id`: the commit; `written`, `deleted`, `meta.unchanged`). A working copy that prints to exactly the committed file is dropped without a commit; when nothing changes at all, no commit is made: 200 with `{ meta: { commit: null, head, unchanged } }`.
- **Checks, again at commit time**, against the branch head and the actor's current roles: only the change set's author (404 for one who may not read change sets), only a draft (409 `invalid-state`), the change set must hold every subject's lock (409 `lock-required`, `meta.subject`), the scope as for a save (`checkCommittedScope`: one 403 whatever the reason, so a hidden file and a missing one answer alike; a delete of a missing file is 422 `nothing-to-delete` only for an actor who may change a missing subject), the files a module names (422 `unknown-asset`, as for a save: `ReadableAssets`, so a working copy saved before the rule, or naming a file the author may no longer read, is never committed), the content model's schema version, and at most 32 MiB of working copies and 1,000 files per commit (422 `too-large`, `too-many-changes`, `meta.max`). **Invalid content is refused**: 422 `invalid-working-copy`, one error object per validation error, `source.pointer` naming the subject and block (`/module/en/charging/blocks/<id>`), `meta.issue` the validation code; a manifest with no entries yet cannot be stored. A working copy whose file changed on the branch since its `baseCommit` is **409 `stale-working-copy`** (`meta.subjects`, `meta.head`), never a silent overwrite. A subject named twice is 422 `duplicate-subject`; a message git would read as ending in trailers is 422 `invalid-message`. A configuration in the commit is what the other files in it are validated against, and is refused while it would make the head, as the commit leaves it, invalid (422 `config-in-use`, "Subjects"); deleting it is 422 `not-deletable`.
- **Order: preflight, intent, then Git first and the database** (#154, #155). A **preflight** holds nothing past a short read: it reads the working copies (with a first check of the size caps and the locks), runs every check above against the branch head, prints the files and builds the commit's tree in Git (`ChangeSetBranches.prepare`: objects only, no ref moves). Then a **commit intent** is written and committed in its own transaction: the actor, the change set, the parent (the head read), the tree, the subjects and the working copies at the versions read. The **write step** is one transaction that holds the change set's row (`FOR UPDATE`: no save, abandon or other commit of it runs meanwhile) and the locks (`FOR SHARE`: none is released or broken), checks again the state, the locks and that no working copy moved (its version and when it was first saved: a copy discarded and saved again starts over at version 1, so the version alone would not tell), makes the Git commit of exactly that tree (`ChangeSetBranches.commit`, compare-and-swap on the head read), then drops the committed working copies (by compare-and-swap on their version), records the commit (an **outbox** row and the audit events: `change-set.commit` with the message as comment and `data: { commit, parent, branch, written, deleted }`, plus `<kind>.write` or `<kind>.delete` per file (`module.write`, `manual.delete`, `config.write`, `term.write`, …), `data: { changeSetId, commit }`) and consumes the intent. So the row is held for the write only (a 1,000-file save point on the compose Postgres: about 0.7 s of 22 s; `src/change-sets/repos/save-points.postgres.spec.ts`). A branch that moved under the commit, or a working copy saved or discarded since the preflight, is tried once more from the preflight, then 409 `branch-moved` (`meta.head`) or `working-copy-changed` (`meta.subjects`); an intent whose commit certainly did not land is removed again. After a 201 the committed working copies are gone: the client's next save is version 0 with the returned commit as `baseCommit`.
- **Timeouts.** The save point's transactions, abandoning a change set and breaking locks (one or in bulk) are **bounded** (`UnitOfWork.run(work, BOUNDED)`): each sets `lock_timeout` (`CCMS_DATABASE_WRITE_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 5 s) and `statement_timeout` (`CCMS_DATABASE_WRITE_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 30 s) for itself only (`SET LOCAL`). Past either the transaction rolls back and the answer is **503 `database-timeout`** with `Retry-After: 1` (`TimedOutError`), never a 500, and it is not retried by itself. A save point that times out after its Git write is a crash between the two (below): recovered, and the next save point finds nothing left to commit.
- **A crash between the two** leaves the commit on the branch, unrecorded, and its working copies and intent in place. `CommitRecorder.catchUp` records it: at the change set's next save point, and by `ReconcileChangeSetCommitsAction`, which `ChangeSetReconciler` runs after the branch pass (a minute after start-up, then every ten minutes): for each change-set branch whose head is neither its base commit nor recorded and whose change set has an intent, it holds the change set's row and walks back from the head, recording each commit whose single parent and tree are an intent's, **as that intent's actor and with its subjects** (`data.recovered: true`), and consuming the intent. It drops the working copies the intent consumed that are still those very copies (version and first save); one saved since stays, so nothing typed is lost, and its `baseCommit` moves on to the commit (one version further) when the file did not change between that base and the commit's parent, so it is not stale for good. **Nothing a commit says about itself counts**: its `Change-set` and `Actor` trailers and its author are for people reading the history, and anyone who can push to a change-set branch can write them (a merge commit carries them too). A commit that matches no intent (pushed by hand with copied trailers, a merge commit with two parents) is never recorded: the branch is logged as an error (`unrecorded`) and left for a person, and the walk goes on past it, so a push on top cannot keep a save point under it out of the record. Intents older than an hour (`INTENT_TTL_MS`; their commit never landed, or sits under such a commit) are removed by the reconciler, oldest first, after one more catch-up under the row lock, and also when the branch is gone (deleted from outside; any other failure keeps them) (`expired`, logged).
- **Taking changes back out** (#267): **`POST /change-sets/:changeSetId/reverts`** (`content.edit`) takes `{ data: { type: "Revert", attributes: { subjects } } }` (1 to 1,000 subjects as locks name them) and answers 200 with `{ meta: { commit, head, restored, deleted, discarded } }`. One commit made exactly as a save point's is (`SavePoints`, `core/save-point.ts`: the same preflight, intent, write step and recovery) puts each subject's file back as it is at the change set's base commit, byte for byte, and deletes a subject the change set created (a module's translation record in its language, `modules/<lang>/<id>.translation.yaml`, goes back with it, #287: put back, or deleted when the base has none; a module whose record alone changed, as marking it current does, counts as changed and has only its record put back); in the write step's transaction their working copies are dropped and their locks released (one `lock.release` audit event each, `data.reason: "change-set.revert"`), with a `change-set.revert` audit event on the change set (`data: { commit, subjects, restored, deleted, workingCopies? }`) beside the commit's own. A subject whose file is the same as at the base but that has a working copy makes no change in Git: the copy is dropped, the lock released (`commit` null when that is all). The same checks as a save point (the author only, a draft only, every subject's lock held: 409 `lock-required`); scope as for changing or deleting the file at the head where the head still has it, and, where the base has it, as for changing it there (so one the change set deleted is checked at the base alone; one 403 whatever the reason). A working copy saved on a subject between the preflight and the write step is a move (tried once more, then 409). A subject the change set does not change (the same file as at the base, no working copy) is 422 `not-changed`; one whose file at the base is not UTF-8 text is 422 `not-restorable`. The configuration and terms are taken out the same way (scope as for changing them, "Subjects"); what the revert leaves must fit the configuration it leaves ("Configuration usage check"): 422 `config-in-use` otherwise. A revert whose commit is recovered after a crash (`CommitRecorder.catchUp`) is recorded as a save point; its locks then stay for the author to release.
- **Changing a module's ID** (#265, `RenameModuleAction`, `core/module-rename.ts`, in `ModuleRenamesModule`, since it takes comments and translation jobs along): **`POST /change-sets/:changeSetId/module-renames`** (`content.edit`) takes `{ data: { type: "ModuleRename", attributes: { from, to } } }` and answers 201 with a `ModuleRename` (`id`: the commit; `attributes`: `from`, `to`, `branch`, `parent`, `message`, `languages`, `rewritten`; `meta.comments`). One commit made exactly as a save point's is (`SavePoints`: preflight, intent, write step, recovery) writes every language of the module under the new ID (`modules/<lang>/<to>.adoc`: the file's bytes as they are, printed canonically only when it names itself), moves its translation record (`modules/<lang>/<id>.translation.yaml`; a record left at the new ID by a module once deleted there, #287, is deleted), deletes the old files, and rewrites every file at the branch head naming the old ID: module references and cross-references (`xref`) at any depth of every other module, in every language, and manifest entries, groups included, and the `replacedBy` of every other module deprecated in favour of it (#360: its source-language file, where a module's deprecation lives; a manual's `replacedBy` names a manual, so no manual is rewritten for that; the module's own, when it names itself, follows it) (`renameModuleReferences`, `renameManifestModule`, `renameReplacement` in `@openfolia/content-model`; printed canonically). Such a module is a file rewritten like the others: listed by `module-references`, locked, scope-checked, refused with a working copy, and named by `module.write` and the outbox, so it passes `unknown-replacement` after the rename. The files naming it are found by reading every module and manifest at the head past the reading filter (`everythingForCheck`, as the configuration check does), not from the module index, which does not hold cross-references; a file that cannot be read is not rewritten (what it names is unknown). **Translations stay current:** a cross-reference is part of its block's source hash, so the target-language translation records of the source-language modules rewritten (`recordRewrites`) take the new hash wherever they held the old one (a record that cannot be read is left as it is), and so do the moved records of a module naming itself; each such record's module, in the record's language, is touched like a file rewritten (#354): locked, scope-checked, refused with a working copy, and named by `module.write` and the outbox. In the write step's transaction the change set's comments on the old ID follow it (`CommentStore.moveModule`, block IDs kept), with one `module.rename` audit event per language (subject the module under its old ID, `data: { changeSetId, commit, from, to, rewritten, comments }`: `rewritten` the number of files rewritten, translation records included, which the commit's own `module.write` and `manual.write` events and its outbox row name one by one), beside the commit's own events. Only the author, only a draft (409 `invalid-state`). **Locks:** the change set must hold the lock of the module under both IDs in every language it has, and of every module and manual it rewrites, translation records included (409 `lock-required`, `meta.subject`, also for one another revision holds); the client takes them first. The old IDs' locks stay with the change set (released when it is merged or abandoned), so nobody changes the old files on another branch meanwhile. **Refused:** an ID that is no module ID, or the same one (422 `invalid-id`, `meta.member`); a new ID that has a file in any language (409 `id-taken`, `meta.moduleId`); a working copy of any subject it touches (409 `uncommitted-working-copies`, `meta.subjects`: commit or discard it first, so nothing typed is lost or left naming the old ID); blocks of the module in an open translation job (409 `translation-at-provider`, `meta.langs`: the job's import would name the old ID); more than 1,000 files (422 `too-many-changes`); a file to move that is not UTF-8 text (422 `not-renamable`). **Scope:** one 403 whatever the reason (`RENAME_OUTSIDE_EDIT`): each language of the module and each file naming it as for changing it (`checkCommittedScope`), and the new ID in every configured language (and any other it has a file in) as for a module with nothing committed (an author who edits and reads all of that language, as creating a module needs) or, where it has a file, as a committed one, checked before `id-taken`. Every language, not only the module's own, so a hidden module at the new ID in another language is not told apart (a 403 against a `lock-required` would tell it): a rename needs an author who edits and reads all of every configured language. A module with no file is 404 only for an actor who edits and reads all of every configured language; anyone else gets that 403. **`GET /change-sets/:changeSetId/module-references/:moduleId`** (`content.edit`, the same checks but the new ID's) answers a `ModuleReferences` (`id` the module ID; `attributes.languages`, `attributes.references`: the subjects a rename rewrites, in subject-key order; `attributes.translations`: the modules, in a target language, whose translation records it brings up to date; `meta.workingCopies`: those, and the module's own languages, with a working copy; `meta.head`), so the client knows what to lock and commit first. **What stays:** releases and tags keep the old ID (they are commits, never rewritten), and so do retrieval chunks of a release; the old ID is not kept as an alias. **History:** module history follows the file's path (`GET /modules/:lang/:id/history`), so the module under its new ID starts with the rename as its first version (`change: added`, on the change set's branch; on `main`, the merge that brought it in), and its history before that stays under the old ID, whose newest version is the removal (still found and readable there). A rename whose commit is recovered after a crash (`CommitRecorder.catchUp`) is recorded as a save point: its comments then stay on the old ID (orphaned) and no `module.rename` event is written.
- **Table** `commit_intents` (`repos/commit-intents.tables.ts`, migration `0008_commit_intents`): `id` (identity), `change_set_id`, `actor_kind`, `actor_id`, `parent`, `tree` (commit IDs, checked by the database), `payload` (`written`, `deleted`, `consumes`: the subjects as the audit log names them and the working copies with their versions, and `message`), `created_at` (the database's clock, which expiry uses).
- **Table** `outbox` (`repos/outbox.tables.ts`, migration `0006_outbox`): `id` (identity, in insertion order), `topic` (`change-set.commit`), `key` (the commit ID; unique per topic, so a commit is recorded once), `payload` (change set, branch, commit, parent, actor, the subjects written and deleted), `created_at`, `delivered_at` (unused: consumers keep their own cursors), `tx_id` (the writing transaction's ID, since `0014_notifications`; see "Notifications").

## Review workflow

Part of the `change-sets` component (#21; `core/workflow.ts`, one action per transition in `core/actions/`). A change set goes `draft` → `in-review` → `approved` → `merged`, back to `draft` when a reviewer returns it, and `abandoned` from any open state. Each transition is its own route (as abandon is), so each documents the one permission it needs; each takes no body or `{ meta: { comment } }` (up to 10,000 characters) and writes **one audit event** on the change set (`change-set.<transition>`, the comment, `data: { from, to, head, … }`) and **one outbox row** (topic `change-set.<transition>`, for comments, the review screen and notifications to consume later), in the transaction that changes the state, which holds the change set's row from the checks to the end. Another author's change set is 404 exactly like a missing one for an actor without `content.read.draft` (#125); a transition from a state it does not take is 409 `invalid-state` (`meta.state`).

"Over all of it" below: the permission's scope covers every module and manual the change set changes (the diff from its base commit to its review head), as each is at the base commit and at the review head (`coversChanges`, `core/review-scope.ts`): a module within one role's scope (a broken file: a role covering all of its language), a manual's audience, and any other changed file only through a role whose scope restricts nothing. One 403 whatever the reason.

| Route      | From → to                                        | Who                                                                                |
| ---------- | ------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `submit`   | `draft` → `in-review`                            | its author, or an editor (`content.edit`) over all of it                           |
| `return`   | `in-review`, `approved` → `draft`                | a reviewer (`content.review`) over all of it, never its author                     |
| `approve`  | `in-review` → `approved` once nothing is pending | a reviewer (`content.review`) over all of it, never its author                     |
| `sign-off` | as approve                                       | `content.approve` over every changed module of a restricted type, never its author |
| `merge`    | `approved` → `merged`                            | a publisher (`release.publish`) over all of it                                     |

- **Submit** records the branch head as `reviewHead`: what approvals are of and a merge merges. The change set is then **frozen**: locks, saves and save points take only drafts (409 `invalid-state`) until it is returned. Refused while it has working copies (409 `uncommitted-working-copies`, `meta.count`: make a save point or discard them, so what is reviewed is what is committed) or changes nothing (409 `no-changes`). Save points a crash left unrecorded are recorded first.
- **Approvals** (table `change_set_approvals`, migration `0009_change_set_workflow`: change set, `requirement`, approver, `head`, `approved_at`; one per change set, requirement and approver). A change set needs `review`, plus `sign-off/<type>` for every module type marked `restricted: true` in the configuration (`openfolia.yaml` at the base commit, at the head or at the base branch's tip now, whatever changed) among the modules it changes (a broken module file counts under every restricted type). A change to `openfolia.yaml` itself needs every sign-off there is, each from an approver whose `content.approve` role covers all of that type (its scope restricts nothing but module types). Approve meets `review`; sign-off meets each restricted type whose changed modules all lie within the approver's `content.approve` scope (409 `no-sign-off-needed` when there is none to give; 403 when they cover none). The answer is the change set with `meta.review`: `required`, `granted`, `pending` and the approvals. Approving again is 409 `already-approved`. **Four eyes:** the author (everyone who wrote it: "Who counts as its author", below) never approves, signs off or returns their own change set (403), whatever roles they hold, unless the installation's self-approval rule lets them (below); they may merge it once it is approved (holding `content.read.draft`, as every reviewer and publisher must: the checks read its commits by ID).
  - A type restricted on the base branch since the change set started needs its sign-off too. If that happens between approval and merge, merge answers 409 `not-approved` with `meta.pending`, and a sign-off (or a return) follows. A base branch that is gone answers 409 `base-missing` to approve, sign-off, return and merge; only the author can still abandon it.
- **Approvals follow role changes** (L4, #180; `ApprovalStanding.standing`, `core/approval-standing.ts`, the one place approve, sign-off and merge ask). An approval counts only while its approver, looked up again in the user directory, still holds what it took: `content.review` over all of it for the review, `content.approve` over the restricted type's changed modules for a sign-off (`stillMeets`). One whose approver lost the role or the scope, or is gone, counts for nothing: approve and sign-off then leave the change set `in-review` with that requirement pending (and list it in `meta.review.lapsed`); merge answers 409 **`approval-lapsed`** (`meta.pending`, `meta.lapsed`: `{ requirement, approver }`) when nothing else meets its requirement, writing nothing; another approval that still holds does, and only approvals that hold are named in `Approved-by`. A change set stuck that way is returned and reviewed again. **The same person may give the review and a sign-off** (L5, decided in #180): four eyes only keeps the author out of both.
- **`GET /change-sets/:changeSetId/review`** (`content.read.draft`; the change set found as `GET /change-sets/:changeSetId` finds it) reads where the review stands, writing nothing: the `ReviewDocument` of approve, asked of `ApprovalStanding.standing` at the review head (at the branch's head while a draft), as approve, sign-off and merge ask it: `required`, that head's approvals, `pending`, `lapsed`, and `granted` always empty; a reader whose `content.read.draft` scope does not cover every change hears of the `review` requirement and the sign-offs they may give (`signsOff`) alone (the sign-offs would name restricted types changed outside what they see, which the changes list hides) (`GetChangeSetReviewAction`). The review screen shows it.
- **Return** clears the review head and removes the approvals (the audit event counts them in `data.approvals`; the audit log keeps every approval); the locks stay. It also takes an approved change set, e.g. after a merge conflict, so its author resolves it.
- **A head that moved** after submitting (something pushed to the branch outside the API) holds no approval: approve, sign-off and merge then return the change set to draft (a `change-set.return` event with `data.reason: "head-moved"`) and answer 409 `head-moved` (`meta.reviewHead`, `meta.head`).
