| `page-design-template-error`            | blocking | validation  | The page design’s own template does not compile (`params.design`, `template`, `file`, `line`, `column`, `problem`), at each manual laid out with it and at a design a change set changes (#396).                                                                                                                                                        |
| `page-design-unknown-template`          | blocking | validation  | The page design names an own template the content repository does not have (`params.design`, `template`): the preset lays the PDF out (#396).                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `page-design-overflow`                  | warning  | validation  | A page design region does not fit its place on the title or back page (`params.at`): the PDF cuts it off.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `running-head-truncated`                | warning  | validation  | A running header or footer does not fit on one line (`params.at`, `params.text`): the PDF shortens it with an ellipsis.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `page-design-missing-value`             | warning  | validation  | A placeholder or title page field of the manual’s design has no value for the manual and variant (`params.design`, `params.at`, `params.name`); the release and date, never known to the checks yet, are not reported (#554).                                                                                                                           |
| `page-design-missing-text-attribute`    | blocking | validation  | A text of the manual’s design uses a text attribute the variant has no value for (`params.design`, `params.at`, `params.name`) (#554).                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `page-design-missing-variant-attribute` | blocking | validation  | A condition in the manual’s design tests an attribute the variant does not set (`params.design`, `params.at`, `params.name`): what it holds is left out (#554).                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `pdf-layout-not-checked`                | warning  | validation  | With `pdf.standard: none`, a designed manual’s PDF (or one with a callout legend beside an image) could not be rendered (`params.reason`): its layout was not checked (#554).                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `callout-label-overflow`                | warning  | validation  | A callout legend beside its image does not fit the image's height even at `pdf.calloutLabelMinSize` (`params.module`, `params.image`, `params.callout`, `params.at`): the PDF cuts it off (#548).                                                                                                                                                       |
| `safety-message-split`                  | warning  | validation  | With a page design, a safety message is taller than a whole page (`params.module`, `params.block`, `params.text` its hazard, `params.at`): the PDF breaks it across pages instead of keeping it on one (#668).                                                                                                                                          |
| `passthrough`                           | warning  | validation  | Content kept verbatim from an import: convert it to blocks.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `empty-table`                           | warning  | validation  | Every cell of a table is empty.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `snippet-too-large`                     | warning  | validation  | A snippet holds a section or a module reference: snippets are one or a few blocks of text.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `inconsistent-variants`                 | warning  | validation  | The manual’s variants set different attributes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `above-audience`                        | warning  | validation  | A module above the manual’s audience is left out of it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `delivery-access-capped`                | warning  | validation  | `delivery.access` opens the addresses further than `CCMS_DELIVERY_MAX_ACCESS` allows: they are served at that (#553).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `missing-manufacturer`                  | warning  | validation  | With `release.checkManufacturer` on, the project has no manufacturer details (`manufacturer` in `openfolia.yaml`): the manual would be released without its imprint (#395).                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `missing-module`                        | blocking | reference   | The manual, or a module reference block, names a module that does not exist.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `module-cycle`                          | blocking | reference   | Module references form a cycle.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `module-ref-unknown-block`              | blocking | reference   | A module reference to one block of a module (#223) names a block that module does not have (`params.module`, `params.block`): nothing is included in its place.                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `chunk-set-too-large`                   | blocking | reference   | At a change set's head, the retrieval chunks of the commit, as the reader sees them, are too large together (#710): at each module that expanded the most (`params.chars`, `params.nodes`); once released, `GET /chunks` refuses it.                                                                                                                    |
| `chunks-not-checked`                    | warning  | reference   | A checks request's chunk cuts passed their budget (#711): from this module on, `params.modules` modules' chunks were not checked.                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `expansion-too-large`                   | blocking | reference   | A variant’s manual in a language holds more than 500,000 content nodes, 12,000,000 characters or 1,000,000 table cells (rows × columns) once its module references are included, or nests more than 100 deep (#679): nothing is resolved or rendered of it (422 `expansion-too-large`).                                                                 |
| `xref-unknown-module`                   | blocking | reference   | A cross-reference links to a module that does not exist.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `xref-unknown-block`                    | blocking | reference   | A cross-reference links to a block that does not exist.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `xref-outside-manual`                   | warning  | reference   | A cross-reference links to a module that is not in the manual.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `deprecated-module`                     | warning  | reference   | The manual includes a deprecated module, directly or through module references (`params.replacedBy`: its replacement).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `unknown-page-design`                   | blocking | reference   | The manual’s design (`design`) or the project’s default (`pdf.design`) is not in the content (`params.design`, `params.from`): the PDF uses the built-in one.                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `unknown-replacement`                   | warning  | reference   | A deprecated module or manual names a replacement that does not exist, or itself (`params.replacedBy`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `address-taken`                         | blocking | reference   | A slug or former address of one manual is another manual’s ID (`params.address`, `params.manual`, `params.as`) (#553).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `unknown-term`                          | blocking | reference   | A term link names a term that does not exist, or whose file cannot be read (`params.term`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `deprecated-term`                       | warning  | reference   | A term link names a deprecated term (`params.term`; `params.replacedBy`, the term to use instead).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `unknown-callout`                       | blocking | reference   | A callout reference names an image or a callout the module does not have (#498).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `hidden-callout`                        | warning  | reference   | A callout reference names a callout the variant leaves out: it has no number there (#498).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `unknown-callout-style`                 | blocking | validation  | A callout on an image names a style that is not configured (#498).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `missing-image`                         | blocking | image       | An image block refers to an image that is not stored.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `missing-attachment`                    | blocking | attachment  | An attachment block refers to a file that is not stored.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `attachment-file-name`                  | blocking | attachment  | An attachment block names its file with an extension that does not fit the stored file, or a dangerous one.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `missing-translation`                   | warning  | translation | A module has no translation into a target language (a manual falls back to the source language).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `outdated-translation`                  | warning  | translation | Blocks of the source module changed since this translation was made, or were never translated (`params.blocks`). At a manual (#656): its cover image text (`params.at` `coverImage`) or its page design's region text (`params.design`) in `params.lang`.                                                                                               |
| `page-design-missing-translation`       | warning  | translation | A text of the manual’s design has no version in the manual’s language (`params.design`, `params.at`): the source language’s is used (#554).                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `page-design-missing-font`              | warning  | validation  | A font file of the manual’s design is not stored or is no font (`params.design`, `params.at` the face, `params.asset`): the PDF uses the default font (#555).                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `missing-cover-image`                   | warning  | image       | The manual’s cover image, or a variant’s, is not stored or is no image (`params.at`, `params.asset`): the PDF goes without it (#394).                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `missing-cover-alt`                     | blocking | validation  | The alternative text of the manual’s cover image, or a variant’s, has no version in the source language (`params.at`), which every other language falls back to (#394).                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `missing-source-title`                  | blocking | validation  | The manual’s title, or a group’s (`params.at`), is written per language without a version in the source language, which every other language falls back to (#731).                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `unknown-cover-variant`                 | warning  | validation  | The manual gives a cover image to a variant it does not build, or to one that has one already (`params.at`): never shown (#394).                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `unknown-source-manual`                 | blocking | reference   | A derived manual’s source manual (`rule.source`) does not exist or cannot be read: it holds only its front and back matter (#451).                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `derived-source`                        | blocking | reference   | A derived manual’s source is a derived manual itself, or the manual itself: nothing is gathered (#451).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `no-gathered-types`                     | blocking | validation  | A derived manual’s rule names no module types and the configuration marks none `safety`: nothing is gathered (#451).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `safety-module-excluded`                | warning  | validation  | A module of a safety type in a derived manual’s source is left out by its rule, excluded or of a type it does not gather (`params.at`) (#451).                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `safety-notice-excluded`                | warning  | validation  | A safety notice of a derived manual’s source is left out by its notice gathering: every module including it excluded or of a type it does not search, or its hazard level not gathered (`params.at` the module holding it) (#670).                                                                                                                      |
| `unknown-excluded-module`               | warning  | reference   | A derived manual’s rule excludes a module its source manual does not include (`params.at`) (#451).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `empty-derived-manual`                  | warning  | validation  | A derived manual’s rule gathers no module from its source manual (#451).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `derived-audience-wider`                | blocking | validation  | A derived manual is for a wider audience than its source manual (#451): nothing is gathered, nor the source’s text attributes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `page-design-dropped-text`              | warning  | translation | A text of the manual’s design has a version in neither the manual’s language nor the source language (`params.design`, `params.at`): it is left out (#554).                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `page-design-region-languages-differ`   | warning  | translation | A region of the manual’s design has other blocks in the manual’s language than in the source language (`params.design`, `params.at`, `params.name` the source language) (#554).                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `missing-label`                         | warning  | translation | No output labels in the manual’s language: English is used.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `forbidden-term`                        | warning  | writing     | The text uses a forbidden variant of an approved term in the module’s language (whole words, any case; `params.term`, `text`, `replacement` the preferred variant, `note`, `field`, `start`, `end`), or a variant of a deprecated term (`params.status` `deprecated`). Blocking with `terminology.forbiddenIsBlocking` (forbidden variants only) (#55). |
| `style-rule`                            | warning  | writing     | The text matches a style rule of its language (`styleRules` in `openfolia.yaml`; `params.rule`, `message`, `replacement`, `text`, `field`, `start`, `end`), or a sentence is longer than `maxSentenceWords` (`params.rule` `max-sentence-words`, `words`, `max`). Blocking when the rule’s `severity` says so (#55).                                    |
| `writing-not-checked`                   | warning  | writing     | The writing checks stopped before the end of the module’s text (`params.stopped`: `chars`, `findings` or `steps`, their bounds) (#55).                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `placeholder-in-module`                 | blocking | validation  | A module holds a page design placeholder (`placeholder:<key>[]`), which only a design’s regions, header and footer may hold.                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `unknown-placeholder`                   | blocking | validation  | A placeholder names no known value.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

### Writing checks and the CLI check

**Terminology and style** (#55): every module checked also gets the writing checks of
`@openfolia/content-model` (`checkWriting`, its README "Writing checks"), compiled once per commit and
language from `openfolia.yaml` (`styleRules`, `terminology`) and the terms, and kept for the next
request (8 at most). A finding is a `forbidden-term` or `style-rule` at its block, with `params`
`field`, `start`, `end` (UTF-16 offsets in the field's plain text), `text`, `replacement` (if
any), and `term`, `status`, `note` (a term), `rule` and `message` (a rule) or `rule`
`max-sentence-words`, `words`, `max` (a sentence). Their severity comes from the configuration.
A module whose check stopped at a bound gets `writing-not-checked` (`params.stopped`).

**The CLI check** (`check-content`): every check at a ref, for CI. It boots the built API
in-process over the repository (`scripts/check-content.mjs`, an in-memory database) and checks
every module in every language and every manual (`CheckContentAction`), or one manual:

```sh
pnpm nx run api:check-content -- --content <repository> [--ref <ref>] [--manual <id>] [--format text|json]
```

`--content` defaults to `CCMS_CONTENT_DIR`, `--ref` to `HEAD`. It prints one line per check
(`blocking forbidden-term en/storage#dry: …`) and a count, or a JSON document. Exit code: 0 with
warnings only, 1 with any blocking check, 2 when it could not check (a bad argument, a ref or
manual that is not there). Images and files are looked up in the blob store `CCMS_BLOB_*` names,
if set. In a pipeline:

```yaml
- run: pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
- run: pnpm nx run api:check-content -- --content "$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/content" --ref "$GITHUB_SHA"
```

## Style review

The `style-review` component (`src/style-review/`, #248): an author asks a language model for style and consistency suggestions on the module they are writing, and accepts or dismisses each one (the editor, #247). **Off by default**, and nothing leaves the server unless an author asks.

- **The port** `StyleReviewer` (`core/style-reviewer.ts`): `review({ lang, units, styleGuide }, signal)` → proposed suggestions. Adapters: `NoStyleReviewer` (the default: `configured` is false, so the route is 404 `not-configured` before anything is read and no outbound call is possible) and `HttpStyleReviewer` (`repos/`), for any chat-completions style HTTP endpoint, hosted or self-hosted: one `POST` of `{ model, messages, temperature: 0, max_tokens: 4096 }` to `CCMS_STYLE_REVIEW_URL`, `Authorization: Bearer <CCMS_STYLE_REVIEW_API_KEY>` when a key is set, reading `choices[0].message.content`. It is used when `CCMS_STYLE_REVIEW_URL` and `CCMS_STYLE_REVIEW_MODEL` are set (`.env.example`); a key needs `https://` (or a loopback address). Never retried, redirects refused (the key goes to the configured host only), the answer read up to 1 MiB. Tests use `FakeStyleReviewer` (`tests/fakes/`) and, for the adapter, a local stub server.
- **`GET /style-review`** (`content.edit`, #247): `{ type: 'StyleReviewStatus', id: 'style-review', attributes: { configured } }`, whether a reviewer is set up, read once by the editor so "Review style" is not shown on a server without one. It says nothing of the endpoint or its model.
- **`POST /change-sets/:changeSetId/modules/:lang/:moduleId/style-review`** (`content.edit`, no body): reviews the module's working copy, else the file committed on the change set's branch, read exactly as `GET …/working-copy` reads it (only the change set's author: 403 for anyone else who holds `content.read.draft`, 404 like a missing change set for anyone else; within their reading scope; also needs `content.read`). It answers 200 with one `StyleReview` (`id` `<lang>/<moduleId>`): `attributes.suggestions`, at most 200 in document order (one answer, not a list resource: nothing is stored to page through), each a `StyleSuggestion` with `blockId`, `field` (which part of the block: `content`, `title`, `alt`, `hazard`, `name`, `description`; for an image callout, its ID and `legend`, #573), `range` (`start`, `end`: UTF-16 code units of the part's plain text, `plainTextOf` in `@openfolia/content-model`), `original` (the text it covers), `message`, `replacement` (null: a remark without a fix) and `rule: "model"`; `meta.version` and `meta.baseCommit` name the text reviewed (version 0: the committed file), `meta.dropped` counts proposals left out.
- **What is sent, and nothing else:** the module's translatable text (#243's units: each block's own text, `plainTextOf`, by block ID and part, and each image callout's legend by the callout's ID, #573; never `literal` or `passthrough`), its language and the configured style guide (`styleGuide` in `openfolia.yaml`, optional, at most 20,000 characters). No metadata (title, keywords, audience), no comments, no other module, no user name.
- **Advisory only.** The model's answer is untrusted data: the module's text is sent as JSON data, with the instruction that nothing in it is an instruction, and whatever comes back is only parsed. Each suggestion must name a unit and part that was sent and a non-empty range of its typed text, not touching an inline node (an attribute, a cross-reference, keys, a line break) nor splitting a surrogate pair (`isTextRange`), with a message of 1 to 1,000 characters and a replacement of at most 2,000 that fits in one text run (no control characters) and changes something; anything else is dropped. Nothing it says is acted on: the author applies a suggestion in the editor.
- **Limits:** at most 100,000 characters sent (text and style guide; 422 `too-large`, `meta.max`); `CCMS_STYLE_REVIEW_PER_MINUTE` reviews per person and minute on each server (default 6; 429 `rate-limited` with `Retry-After`); 8 reviews at once per server (503 `style-review-busy`, checked before the rate limit, so it uses up none of the caller's reviews; a call counts until the endpoint's answer ends); `CCMS_STYLE_REVIEW_TIMEOUT_MS` per review (default 30 s; 503 `style-review-timeout` with `Retry-After`, the request aborted). A failing endpoint, or an answer that is not as asked, is 503 `style-review-unavailable` (`Retry-After: 30`), without the endpoint's error.
- **Not stored.** One `module.style-review` audit event per review that went to the model or found nothing to send (subject the module, `data`: `changeSetId`, `outcome` (`ok`, `empty` when the module has no text and nothing was sent, `timeout`, `failed`), `suggestions`, `dropped`), never the text, the suggestions or the endpoint. Refusals before anything is sent (`too-large`, `rate-limited`, `style-review-busy`) are not audited, so a caller cannot fill the append-only log by asking too often. The key never appears in logs, errors or the audit log (the adapter logs an error status at most; tested).
- Not part of the checks (#71): checks are derived on every read, a review costs a model call and runs only on request. The CI command of #55 does not exist yet; a `--style-review` option can call this action once it does.

## Translations

The `translations` component (`src/translations/`, #243): which source blocks each translation was made from, so a module's translation into each language is known to be up to date, outdated, missing or at a provider. Derived from Git and the translation jobs, never stored but in the rebuildable module index: the list and the report read each translation compared with its record from there (`TranslationReads`, `core/translation-index.ts`, #480; see "Module index"), a single status (`GET /translations/status/:lang/:moduleId`) and an export from the files.

- **Where it comes from.** A translation is a module file in a target language with the source module's block IDs. Next to it, its translation record (`modules/<lang>/<id>.translation.yaml`, `TranslationRecord` in `@openfolia/content-model`, "Translation") names per block the source hash it was translated from, and the source commit; the import (#51) writes it. A block's state compares that hash with `sourceHash` of the source block's text now (`compareWithRecord`): its own translatable text after normalization, so a reformatting the canonical printer removes changes nothing, and an edit of a block changes only that block. A translation without a record (made before tracking, or started again after its file was deleted: the delete takes the record with it, #287) is outdated in every block; one that cannot be read counts as none; a translated file that cannot be read is outdated in every block, for a reader who may learn of it.
- **States**, per block and per module (`TRANSLATION_STATES`, `core/translation-status.ts`): `up-to-date`, `outdated` (the source changed since, or the block was never translated), `missing` (no file in that language) and `at-provider` (a block of an open translation job: `TranslationJobs.unitsAtProvider`, see "Delta exports" below). A block at a provider is `at-provider` whatever else; a module is `missing` or `outdated` while any block not at a provider is, else `at-provider` while any block is, else `up-to-date` (a source module with no translatable block: `up-to-date` with a file, else `missing`).
- **`GET /translations/status`**: one `TranslationStatus` (`id` `<lang>/<moduleId>`) per source module, as the reader sees it in the source language (files that cannot be read are left out), and target language the reader would see a translation in, at `?ref=`. `attributes`: `moduleId`, `lang`, `state`; `meta`: `title` (the source title), `totalBlocks`, `outdatedBlocks` (outdated or missing, and not at a provider), `atProviderBlocks`, `sourceCommit` (the record's; null without one). Ordered by source title, module ID and the configured order of target languages; paged, by status, or with `page[by]=row` (#689) by row of the module × language matrix (a module, a page design or a manual's own text): `page[size]`, the pages and `meta.page.total` then count rows, each page holding every status its rows have (the editor's matrix asks for this). Filters, all before paging: `filter[lang]` (a target language; any other matches nothing), `filter[manual]` (the modules a manual includes, also through module references, as the reader reads it; a manual they may not see matches nothing), `filter[folder]` (a folder of the content browser: `<type>`, or `<type>/<value>` of the first configured facet), `filter[state]`, `filter[q]` (text in the source title or the module ID, case and diacritics aside, 1 to 200 characters), `filter[module]` (#694: source modules by ID, comma-separated, 1 to 200, as `filter[id]` on `/module-summaries` takes them; no page design and no manual; the content browser reads its page's states with it and `page[by]=row`).
- **`GET /translations/status/:lang/:moduleId`**: one, with `meta.blocks` (`{ blockId, state }` per translatable unit of the source, in document order). 404 `not-found`, exactly like a module that does not exist, for a language that is no target language, a source module the reader does not see, or a translation they would not see.
- **Who**: `translation.manage` or `content.read.draft`, in any scope (`authorizeTranslationStatus`, `core/statuses.ts`; 403 `forbidden` otherwise), on top of `content.read`, which opening the ref needs (the OpenAPI document names `translation.manage`; `ON_TOP_OF_READING` in `tests/routes.ts` gives the access walk a holder of both). **Scope**: read through `ReadableContent`: a source module outside the reader's scope is not listed, and a language counts only where the reader would see the module in it (`wouldSee`, so a scope without a language leaves its column out), so neither the list nor `meta.page.total` counts anything outside it. A translation that exists but lies outside the scope (another audience, say) is `missing` to that reader, as resolving treats it. Its record is read only where the reader sees the translated file (`ReadableContent.translationRecord`).
- **Caching**: `@BeyondTheCommit()`, since `at-provider` comes from the jobs, not the commit. Not part of the delivery API.
- **Checks**: `outdated-translation` (warning, see "Checks") on a translation whose source blocks changed since it was made: on every translation a manual's checks read, and, in a change set's checks, on the translations of each changed source-language module (`params.lang`, `params.blocks` with the number of blocks). A manual's checks also give one at the manual (#656) per target language its cover image text, or its page design's region text, has units with a version of their own there made from another source text (or without a record): `params.at` `coverImage` or `params.design`, `params.blocks` the units, the language as the check's.

- **Page designs' region text** (#568, `core/design-translations.ts`): each page design with region text is a status too, `attributes.kind` `design` with `designId` (a module's is `kind` `module` with `moduleId`), `id` `<lang>/designs/<designId>`, its title the design's name, its blocks keyed `<slot>.<blockId>`, against the record in the design file (`PageDesign.translations`, `@openfolia/content-model`). A region with no text of its own in the language is `missing`, as is the design while no region has; a source edit leaves the record, so it shows `outdated`. A design without region text has no status. Designs carry no audience: every reader of the status sees them, in every target language. In the list they come after the project labels and before the manuals (by name, then ID); `filter[manual]` keeps the manual's design (its manifest's `design`, else `pdf.design`), `filter[folder]` none, `filter[q]` matches the name or the ID, and `filter[kind]=module|design|manual` keeps one kind. **`GET /translations/status/:lang/designs/:designId`** is one, 404 `not-found` for a design that is not there or has no region text.
- **A manual's own text** (#656, `core/manual-translations.ts`): its title and its groups' titles (#731: units `title` and `group.<12 hex>`, the SHA-256 of the group's NFC source title, so groups of one title share a unit and reordering keeps the key) and the alternative text of its cover images (`Manifest.coverImage`: the manual's own and each variant's, the first entry for a variant only) are a status of `kind` `manual` with `manualId`, `id` `<lang>/manuals/<manualId>`, its title the manual's (in the source language), its units `title`, `group.<hex>`, `coverImage.default` and `coverImage.variant-<12 hex>` (the SHA-256 of the variant's slug: stable when entries are reordered), against the record in the manifest (`Manifest.translations`, shaped as a design's: per language `{ sourceCommit, blocks }`). An alternative text with no version of its own in the language (one text for every language, or none for it) is `missing`; one with a version but no record naming its current source hash `outdated` (so a text typed in the manual builder shows outdated until marked current). Since every manual has a title, every manual has a status (#731); a manual counts in a language where the reader reads it (`seesManualIn`). In the list these rows come after the designs and before the modules (by title, then ID); `filter[manual]` keeps that manual's, `filter[folder]` none, `filter[q]` matches the title or ID. **`GET /translations/status/:lang/manuals/:manualId`** is one, 404 as for a module.
- **The project labels** (#731, `core/config-translations.ts`): the configuration's labels (`configUnits` of `@openfolia/content-model`: module types, audiences, facets and their values, variant attributes and their values, text attributes, safety symbols, callout styles) are one status of `kind` `config` (no `moduleId`, `designId` or `manualId`), `id` `<lang>/config/project` (two segments, so no module ID takes it), keyed `config.project` where a module ID goes, its title `Project labels`, its units the `ConfigUnitKey`s (`moduleTypes.<id>`, `facets.<id>.<value>`, …), against the record in `openfolia.yaml` (`ProjectConfig.translations`, shaped as a design's). A label with no version of its own in the language is `missing` (so the status is `missing` until one has); one with a version but no record naming its current source hash `outdated`. Labels carry no audience: every status reader sees the row in every target language, as a design's. In the list it comes first, before the designs; `filter[manual]` keeps it (a manual's title page and metadata show labels; not for a manual the reader may not see), `filter[folder]` and `filter[module]` do not, `filter[q]` matches `Project labels` or `config.project` (`labels`, `config`), `filter[kind]=config` keeps it alone, and `page[by]=row` counts it as one row. **`GET /translations/status/:lang/config/project`** is it, 404 `not-found` for a language that is no target language or a configuration without a label in the source language.

### Delta exports (XLIFF 2.0)

A translation manager sends a provider only the blocks whose source changed since they were last translated (#50). Each export is a **translation job** in Postgres (`translation_jobs`, `translation_job_units`, migration `0020_translation_jobs`; `DrizzleTranslationJobs` implements `TranslationJobs`); the XLIFF itself is written by `@openfolia/xliff` (`packages/xliff/README.md`) and is never stored.

- **`POST /translations/exports`** (`ExportTranslationsAction`): `attributes.languages` (target languages, each once), `attributes.subject` (`{ type: 'manual', manualId }`: the modules the manual includes, also through module references; `{ type: 'modules', moduleIds }`, at most 500; `{ type: 'folder', folder }`: `<type>` or `<type>/<value of the first facet>`), `attributes.ref` (default `main`), `attributes.dueDate` (`YYYY-MM-DD`, optional) and `meta.dryRun`. Per language it sends each unit of the subject's modules that is `outdated` or `missing` in the translation status and not at a provider already; a module reached through several references counts once. Answers the `TranslationJob` with `meta` counts (`modules`, `units`, `words`; per language in `meta.languages`): 201 with `Location`, or 200 with `meta.dryRun: true` for a dry run, which writes nothing. Words are the source text's word-like segments (`Intl.Segmenter`). Errors: 422 `invalid-parameter` (`source.pointer` at the language) for a language that is no target language or that the exporter does not manage, checked before any module is read; 422 `nothing-to-translate`; 422 `too-many-units` past 20,000 units (`MAX_EXPORT_UNITS`); 409 `export-conflict` when an export at the same time sent one of the units (a unit is open in at most one job: a partial unique index), try again.
- **Who** (`core/exports.ts`): `translation.manage`, and `content.read` to open the ref. A module counts in a language only where the exporter reads it, would see a translation in that language (`readTranslation`) and holds `translation.manage` on it there (`moduleTarget` with the target language: every dimension of the scope). **A job is there** (list, read, file, cancel; `seesJob`, `core/translation-jobs.ts`, and the same in SQL) for its exporter, and for whoever holds `translation.manage` for each of its target languages and `content.read` for its source and target languages, in roles whose scope restricts nothing but languages (`jobReaders`), so neither its subject (module IDs) nor its counts tell anyone of content outside their scope; a job exported from a ref that was not released (a draft's ref and commit; `released` holds only for `main`, a tag or a commit ID at released content) only for holders of `content.read.draft`, and for anyone but its exporter only where roles holding `content.read.draft` with nothing but languages restricted cover its source and target languages, since a draft is read through that scope. Anyone who sees a job may cancel it. To anyone else it is 404, exactly like one that does not exist, and it is not listed.
- **Page designs** (#568): `{ type: 'designs', designIds }` (1 to 100) exports designs' region text, and a manual's subject includes its design. Each design is a `<file id="design.<designId>">` of its own (`@openfolia/xliff`, "The document"), its due units first; the job keeps them under `design.<designId>` where a module's ID goes. A design counts in a language only for an exporter with `translation.manage` in a role whose scope restricts nothing but languages, that language among them (`managesDesignIn`): a design is every manual's look, so a role limited to some audiences or module types manages none. The audit event's `subject` names the design IDs.
- **A manual's own text** (#656): `{ type: 'manuals', manualIds }` (1 to 100) exports manuals' own text (their titles and groups' titles since #731, their cover image alternative text), and a manual's subject includes it; the title and group units are `ccms:kind="section"` with one `title` segment, path notes `title#manual` and `group#<hex>`. Each manual is a `<file id="manual.<manualId>" original="manuals/<id>.yaml">`, one unit per cover image (`ccms:kind="image"`, one `alt` segment, plain text: no inline codes), its path note `coverImage#default` or `coverImage#<variant slug>`; the job keeps them under `manual.<manualId>`. A manual counts in a language for an exporter who reads it there and holds `translation.manage` in a role whose audience covers the manual's and whose languages hold that one (`managesManualIn`).
- **The project labels** (#731): `{ type: 'config' }` exports them, and a manual's subject includes them (only for a manual the exporter reads), as it includes its design. They are a `<file id="config.project" original="openfolia.yaml">` (title `Project labels`), one unit per label (`xliffUnitsOfConfig`: `ccms:kind="section"`, one `title` segment, plain text, path note `label#<unit key>`); the job keeps them under `config.project`. They count in a language for an exporter with `translation.manage` in a role whose scope restricts nothing but languages, that language among them (`managesConfigIn`, as `managesDesignIn`), who may also change the configuration (`changesConfig`), as the import requires (`translatesConfigIn`); for anyone else an export, and a job's file, leave them out, so none waits at a provider for an import that could not apply it. The audit event's `subject` is `{ type: 'config' }`.
- **`GET /translations/exports`** (paged, newest first) and **`GET /translations/exports/:jobId`**: `attributes` `languages`, `subject`, `ref`, `commit`, `state` (`sent`, `returned` once a translation import brought back every unit, `cancelled`), `dueDate`, `createdBy`, `createdAt`; `meta` the counts.
- **`GET /translations/exports/:jobId/file?lang=`**: that language's XLIFF (`application/xliff+xml; charset=utf-8`), or without `lang` a ZIP of one per language (`translation-<jobId>-<lang>.xlf`), always `Content-Disposition: attachment`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, `nosniff`. Made again on each request from the job's commit and units, through the downloader's reading scope (`jobDocument`), so a file never holds a module they may not read now (404 when nothing is left). The commit opens with `content.read` when it was released content at export (`released`, `OpenSnapshotAction`'s `releasedOnce`), even once `main` has moved on; a draft's needs `content.read.draft`.
- **`POST /translations/exports/:jobId/cancel`**: a `sent` job becomes `cancelled` and its units are no longer `at-provider` (the next export sends them again); 409 `job-not-open` otherwise.
- **Audit**: `translation-job.export` (languages, subject, ref, commit, due date, counts) and `translation-job.cancel`, each in the transaction of its change.
- In-request, not a background job: an export reads the subject's modules once (as the status list does) and the file is written in memory; 20,000 units is the bound.

### COTI packages (#140)

A translation management system that speaks COTI (the open exchange standard between content and translation management systems; level 1, specification 1.1) takes a job as one package and returns it the same way. Translation integration is part of the open core: no feature gate. Behind the port `TranslationExchange` (`core/translation-exchange.ts`; its adapter `CotiPackageExchange`, `repos/coti-package-exchange.ts`, writing and reading `COTI.xml` with `writeCoti` and `readCoti` from `@openfolia/xliff`), so COTI's API levels 2 and 3, a watched folder or another system's connector can be added beside it. Download and upload only: the server makes no outbound call and sends nothing anywhere.

- **`GET /translations/exports/:jobId/package`** (`TranslationJobPackageAction`): a ZIP (`application/zip`, `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="<timestamp>_translation-<jobId>.coti"`, the time as `2026-11-20T093015Z`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, `nosniff`) holding one translation project per target language, in a folder `<jobId>-<lang>_<source>_<target>` (the standard's `<project-id>_<source>_<target>`), each with `COTI.xml` and the job's XLIFF in `translation files/translation-<jobId>-<lang>.xlf`, exactly as `GET …/file` gives it (`TranslationJobFileAction`: the same reading scope, the same 404s). A cancelled job is 409 `job-not-open`. Who: as the file.
- **`COTI.xml`**, what is filled: `<coti version="1.1" level="1" creator="OpenFolia" creation-date package-id>` (`package-id` the job's ID), `<project name="Translation job <id>" project-id="<jobId>-<lang>" checkout-date due-date proposal="no">` (`checkout-date` the export's time, `due-date` the job's due day at 00:00 UTC, left out without one), `<subject>` what was sent in a line (`Manual <id>`, `3 modules`, …), `<meta>` `job`, `ref` and `commit`, `<translation name source-language target-language status="none">` and one `<translation-file file-ref mime-type="application/xliff+xml" encoding="UTF-8" creation-date>`. Languages are COTI codes, `<language>-<region>` as the standard's schema has them: a tag's own region, else its most likely one (`cotiLanguage`: `de` → `de-DE`, `en` → `en-US`; a script is not carried). No reference files, no `tms-id` (the translation system's to set), no DTD. The output is checked against the standard's schema rules in the tests (`cotiSchemaErrors`), and was checked once against the published schema file itself.
- **A returned package** goes to `POST /translations/imports` like any upload (the bytes decide, not the name): a ZIP holding a `COTI.xml` (any case) at the top of a project's folder, or of the package, is one (`CotiPackageExchange.unpack`). Read: only each description and the translation files it lists (relative to its folder: no `..`, `.` or empty segment, no backslash, colon, control character or leading `/`, at most 255 characters; `.xlf`/`.xliff` or an XLIFF MIME type); the translation system's `tms-id`, `status` and reference files are not read, and an XLIFF the descriptions do not list is not imported. Each XLIFF then goes through the import's own reading, checks and apply unchanged (#51), so a package's report, decisions and change set are exactly its XLIFF's. Its job is the description's `package-id` (or its `job` meta entry); the XLIFF's own `ccms:job`, where it has one, must name the same (422 `coti-job-mismatch`), and its languages must be the project's as COTI codes (422 `language-mismatch`). An unknown job is 404 `unknown-job`, a cancelled one 409 `job-not-open`, as for an XLIFF. Applying returns the job's units (`returned` on the Jobs tab).
- **Untrusted input.** The ZIP is read by `readZip` as an XLIFF ZIP is (no path out of its folder, no links, at most 100 entries and `CCMS_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` or 32 MiB unpacked, the smaller, the compression ratio bounded: 422 `invalid-archive` with `meta.reason`). A `COTI.xml` is read by the same strict reader as XLIFF (`readXml`: no DTD, so no entity declared, external or internal, and none expanded; UTF-8 only; at most 1 Mi characters, 8 deep, 20,000 nodes, 32 attributes per element; past 4 MiB it is not decoded at all). Every refusal of a package is 422 `invalid-coti` with `meta.reason` (an XML reason, `not-coti`, `invalid-coti`, `duplicate-description`, `no-job`, `missing-file`, `duplicate-file`, `unsupported-file`) and `meta.path`. Kept as an XLIFF import is (in memory, an hour, `envelope` beside each document). No migration.

### Translation imports

A translated XLIFF document of a job (or a ZIP of them, one per language) comes back and goes into a change set (#51; `TranslationImportsModule`, `core/actions/translation-imports.action.ts`). Two steps: the upload is checked unit by unit and kept, then applied.

- **The XML is untrusted** (`readXliff` and `targetParts` in `@openfolia/xliff`): no DTD (`<!DOCTYPE>`, so no entity declaration, internal or external) and no entity but XML's five predefined ones, so nothing is fetched and nothing expands; bounded whatever `CCMS_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` is: at most 32 Mi characters (`MAX_XLIFF_LENGTH`), 1,000,000 nodes, elements 64 deep, 64 attributes per element and 16 namespace prefixes in scope, 5,000 files and 50,000 units (a `<file>` without a unit refuses the document: `invalid-xliff`); read in linear time; UTF-8 only. The upload is at most `CCMS_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` (422 `too-large`, the request read no further), a ZIP read by `readZip` as an import's is (no path out of its folder, no links, at most 100 entries and `CCMS_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` or 32 MiB unpacked, the smaller; `.xlf` and `.xliff` files only). A document refused is refused whole, with a code of its own: `xliff-dtd-forbidden`, `xliff-not-well-formed`, `xliff-too-large`, `xliff-too-deep`, `xliff-too-many-nodes`, `xliff-too-many-attributes`, `xliff-unsupported-encoding`, `not-xliff`, `invalid-xliff` (`meta.path` names the file of a ZIP). At most 2 checks or applies run at once (503 `import-busy`), a slot taken only once the upload is read, so a slow upload holds none.
- **`POST /translations/imports`** (`multipart/form-data` with `X-Ccms-Request`: `file`, optional `dryRun` (`true`, the only value) and `baseRef`): the documents' target languages must be ones the actor manages translations into (403 otherwise, before anything is looked up), each once (422 `duplicate-language`), all of one job (422 `mixed-jobs`; `ccms:job`) the actor sees (404 `unknown-job` otherwise, as reading it would be), not cancelled (409 `job-not-open`), with its languages (422 `language-mismatch`). Each unit is then decided against what the job sent (read at its commit as the actor reads it there) and the base branch (`baseRef`, default `CCMS_DEFAULT_REF`): `ok`; `source-changed` (the source block's hash on the base is not the one exported); `broken-markup` with `reason` (its `<pc>` and `<ph>` codes missing, extra, unknown, of another kind or reordered across nesting, a line break, an empty target; or `source-hash` when the unit's `ccms:sourceHash` is not the one the job sent), refused alone; `unknown-block` (`not-sent`: not a unit the job sent; `removed`: gone from the source, a callout's legend unit too when its callout is gone, #548); `unknown-module` (no module the actor may read, at the base or the job's commit); `outside-scope` (a module they read but do not manage translations of in that language, or whose translation they would not see), with no detail, so it tells nothing the reader could not read anyway. A code's meaning (bold, a link's target, an attribute's name) always comes from the source as the server writes it again, never from the uploaded file, so a target can neither change a link nor add one. Answers 201 with a `TranslationImport` (`attributes`: `languages`, `baseRef`, `baseCommit`, `state`, `createdBy`, `createdAt`, `expiresAt`; `relationships.job`, `relationships.changeSet`; `meta.counts`; `meta.report`, per module and language, paged by `page[number]` and `page[size]`). For the import screen (#244) the report names each module's source `title` and each unit's `kind` (its block type, `item` for a list item or step, `callout` for an image callout's legend, #548) where the check read them (never for `unknown-module`, `outside-scope` or `not-sent`), and a `source-changed` unit carries `texts`: the source as sent (`exported`, at the job's commit), as it is now (`current`, at the base) and the uploaded `target`, as plain text, each cut at 2,000 characters (`truncated`), while the report holds at most 4 Mi characters of them (`MAX_REPORT_TEXT_CHARACTERS`; past it a unit has none); they count with the documents in the store's bound. `GET /translations/imports/:importId?filter[state]=` pages only the units in that state (and the modules with one); `meta.counts` stays the whole report's. **Nothing is written**: no change set, no Git object, no row, no audit event.
- **Kept in memory, not in a table** (`MemoryTranslationImportStore`): the documents and the report for an hour (`expiresAt`), at most 20 on a server, 128 Mi characters of documents and report texts together, and 3 per person (a fourth replaces their oldest, only once it fits; a full server is 503 `too-many-imports` and removes nothing); an applied import keeps its report, not its documents, gone after a restart and not shared between servers (the apply has to reach the server that checked; otherwise the file is uploaded again). No migration. An import is its uploader's only: 404 for anyone else (`GET /translations/imports/:importId`).
- **`POST /translations/imports/:importId/apply`** (no body, or `{ meta: { decisions: [{ lang, moduleId, blockId, decision }] } }`, `keep` or `skip` per `source-changed` unit, `keep` by default): the stored documents are checked again against the base as it is now, then, as the uploader, through the change-set actions (never around them): `CreateChangeSetAction` (titled after the job, from the import's base), `AcquireLockAction` per target module, `SaveWorkingCopyAction` per module with its units merged in by block ID (`mergeTranslation`, `core/translation-merge.ts`: only the translatable text changes; a block the target lacks comes from the source's structure, in the nearest slot both have, after the nearest preceding block both have; the target's image and attachment references, variant conditions and IDs stay; without a target file the source is the start; a callout's legend, #548, goes into the target's callout of that ID, only its legend changing, and a callout the target lacks is added from the source to its image, after the nearest preceding callout both have), and one save point (`CommitWorkingCopiesAction`) holding the working copies and each module's translation record (`modules/<lang>/<id>.translation.yaml`: the source hash each unit was exported from, the job's commit as `sourceCommit`, the record's other blocks as they were). A `keep` unit thus stays `outdated`; a `skip` one is left out. In the save point's write transaction: the job's units imported are returned (`TranslationJobs.returnUnits`; the job becomes `returned` once none is left at the provider, a returned job may be imported again), one `translation.import` audit event (subject `translation-import`; job, change set, commit, languages, counts) and one `translation.imported` outbox row (keyed by the import; `{ importId, importer, actor, changeSetId, jobId, languages, modules, moduleCount, designs, designCount, manuals, manualCount, configLanguages }`; `designs` to `manualCount` since #656, `configLanguages` (the languages whose project labels it changed) since #731, added beside the others so a reader of the old fields is unaffected; the audit event's `data.config` says whether it changed them). Every check of those actions applies (`content.edit` and every module's scope, the author, the locks: 409 `locked`), so apply also needs `content.edit`. A failure abandons the change set (so nothing of the import reaches review; a save point that landed in Git just before its transaction failed stays on that abandoned branch) and leaves the import `validated`; once applied it is 409 `invalid-state`. 422 `nothing-to-import` when no unit can be imported; `unreadable-translation` when a target file cannot be read.
- **Page designs** (#568): a design's `<file>` is checked unit by unit like a module's (`translation-import-check.ts`: the codes of the source, `<ph>` placeholders included, so an unknown placeholder is `broken-markup` naming its unit; the source hash against the job's), and its report entry has `moduleId` `design.<designId>` and `designId`; a decision names it the same way. Importing needs `translation.manage` as for the export and `content.edit` in a role whose scope restricts nothing (`changesDesigns`, as a change set's design change needs); without it the design's units are `outside-scope`. Apply takes one lock and one working copy per design, all its languages together: each translated region gets the language's own text (the source's text and structure kept where the region was the same for every language, so no `dropped-text` and no `region-languages-differ` in the manual's checks), refilled block by block, and the record's keys for the imported units with their source hashes and the job's commit; other keys stay. Region text goes into the PDF as Typst string literals (`@openfolia/render-typst`), never as markup, whatever a translation holds. Designs are in `mark-current`, the `outdated-translation` check and the coverage report since #656.
- **A manual's own text** (#656): its `<file>` is checked like a design's, against the manifest at the job's commit and at the base as the importer reads them; its report entry has `moduleId` `manual.<manualId>` and `manualId`. `unknown-module` for a manual not there for the importer; `outside-scope` where they do not read it in the language, do not manage its translation there (`managesManualIn`) or may not change its manifest (`changesManual`: `content.edit` over its audience with no entry hidden from them, as locking it and saving it check again). A `<file id>` naming another manual only reaches units the job sent for that manual (`unknown-block` otherwise), and an ID that is no manual ID is no manual. A translation is plain text, trimmed at its edges; one that is not one line of at most 500 characters is `broken-markup` (`invalid-content`). Apply takes one lock and one working copy per manifest, all its languages together: each imported unit's cover image gets the language's own text (`{ <source>: …, <lang>: … }` where it had one text for all), the rest of the manifest as it was, and the record's keys for the imported units with their source hashes and the job's commit. The text reaches the PDF as a Typst string literal and the editor as text, never as markup. Since #731 a manual's title and group titles are units of it, checked and written the same way: each gets the language's own version (`mergeManifestTranslation`).
- **The project labels** (#731): the `<file id="config.project">` is checked like a manual's own text, against the configuration at the job's commit and at the base (every reader of a commit reads it, so never `unknown-module`); its report entry has `moduleId` `config.project` and `config: true`. `outside-scope` (each unit, no title) where the importer does not manage the labels' translation into the language (`managesConfigIn`) or may not change the configuration (`changesConfig`: `checkConfigChange`, unrestricted `content.edit` and `admin.roles`, as locking and saving it check again). A translation is plain text, trimmed; one that is not one line of at most 500 characters is `broken-markup` (`invalid-content`). Apply takes the configuration's lock and saves one working copy of it (`SaveWorkingCopyAction`, `StrictProjectConfig`), all its languages together: each imported label gets the language's own version (`mergeConfigTranslation`: `{ <source>: …, <lang>: … }` where it had one text), the record's keys for the imported units take their source hashes and the job's commit, the rest of `openfolia.yaml` as it was. A decision names it by `moduleId` `config.project`.
- **`POST /translations/mark-current`** (`{ data: { type: "TranslationMarkCurrent", attributes: { changeSetId, moduleId | designId | manualId | config: true, lang } } }`, exactly one of the four): for a source fix that needs no new translation. In a draft change set of the actor's, the record of the module in `lang` takes the source's current hash for each block the translation has (the branch head as `sourceCommit`), the text unchanged: the module's lock (`AcquireLockAction`) and a save point writing only the record (`CommitWorkingCopiesAction`'s `translationRecords`), with a `translation.mark-current` audit event. Needs `translation.manage` on the module in `lang` (403 otherwise) and `content.edit`; 422 `invalid-parameter` for no target language, 422 `translation-missing` without a translation, 409 `uncommitted-working-copies` while the change set holds a working copy of it. With `designId` or `manualId` (#656), a page design's region text or a manual's cover image text: their record is part of their file, so the save point holds a working copy of the design or manifest with only its record changed (each unit with a version of its own in `lang` takes the current source hash), after taking its lock; a design needs `managesDesignIn` and `changesDesigns`, a manual `managesManualIn` and `changesManual` (403 otherwise); 422 `translation-missing` without text of its own in `lang`. The response's `moduleId` is the key (`design.<id>`, `manual.<id>`), beside `designId` or `manualId`. Its audit event names the design or manual as a lock does (`design`/`manual` and its ID), `lang` in its data; a save point refused (a blocking issue, the branch moved) discards that working copy again. With `config: true` (#731), the project labels the same way: the configuration's lock and a working copy of `openfolia.yaml` with only its record changed; needs `managesConfigIn` and `changesConfig` (403 otherwise), 422 `translation-missing` without a label of its own in `lang`; the response has `moduleId` `config.project` and `config: true`, the audit event's subject `config`/`openfolia.yaml`.
- **Who writes a design's, manual's or the configuration's record** (#663, #731): a page design's, a manifest's and `openfolia.yaml`'s translation record (`translations`) is part of its file, so a working copy or a commit could write it; only a translation manager changes it (`core/translation-records.ts`). A language's entry, added, changed or removed, needs `translation.manage` for that language (a design or the configuration: in a role restricting nothing but languages, as `managesDesignIn`; a manual: one covering its audience, as committed and as written, as `managesManualIn`); anyone else keeps it as the branch head has it, or takes it as the change set's base commit has it, or, while an update from the base reports the file conflicting, as the base branch's head has it (`changeSetRecordBases`; the update's own save point of the kept working copies takes the head it merges): no record none of them holds. Otherwise 422 `translation-record-protected` (`meta.subject`, `meta.languages`). Checked when a working copy is saved (after the stale check, so a stale copy is 409 first) and again for every commit onto a change set's branch, whatever wrote it: `SavePoints.prepare` checks the commit's files against the head and the base before it writes the tree, and a save point's write step takes only a tree it made (`CheckedTree`), so a save point, taking changes out, a module rename and a carry-forward all pass it. An update from the base (`POST /change-sets/:id/refresh`) is a merge, not a save point: what it takes in is checked against the base's head before it is written (`refuseBranchRecords`): a design or manifest kept whole (`keep`, `keepMine`) must hold the base head's record, and any other the branch changed since its base commit the base head's or the base commit's (the base's then wins the three-way merge), each unless the person manages the language (422 `translation-record-protected`; save it with the base branch's record as it is now first, which anyone may while the conflict is reported). Submitting (`POST /change-sets/:id/submit`) checks those of the files an update reported conflicting once more, each entry the base commit's unless the submitter manages the language (422 `translation-record-protected`): an update that failed after its own save point landed leaves the base head's record there, which the merge would bring back after a manager withdrew it on the base (update again, or save it with the base commit's record, then submit). A record a translation manager wrote otherwise is not asked about again, whoever submits it. A revert puts the base's file back, its record with the text it was made from, so it always may; a carry-forward leaves such a file as it is there (conflict kind `translation-record`). A deleted file takes its record with it, as a module's does. The import format has no record (`ImportedManifest`): an import updating a manual keeps the committed one. The XLIFF import and `mark-current` write it as the translation manager they run as.
- **Translation records at a save point**: `CommitWorkingCopiesAction` takes `translationRecords` (and `after`, more of its write transaction) from callers in the API only, never from a request body. Each record is written beside its module, under that module's lock, scope-checked as a change of the module, and counted in `MAX_COMMIT_CHANGES`; an unchanged one makes no change.

## Reports

The `reports` component (`src/reports/`, #56, FR-19): how complete each manual is per language, and where each module is used, for leads. Read-only and derived: nothing stored but what the rebuildable module index holds, which both read (#480). Both need `content.read.draft` (in any scope), on top of `content.read`, which opening the ref needs; both read through `ReadableContent` (the translation status and the module index as the reader sees them), so **nothing outside the reader's scope is named or counted**, in a row, a count or `meta.page.total`. Not part of the delivery API.

- **`GET /reports/translations`** (`ReportTranslationsAction`, `core/translation-coverage.ts`): one `TranslationCoverage` (`id` `<manualId>/<lang>`) per manual the reader may see (a manifest that cannot be read is left out) and target language they may read it in (`seesManualIn`). `attributes`: `manualId`, `lang`; `meta`: `title`, `deprecated`, and `modules` and `blocks`, each `{ total, upToDate, outdated, atProvider, missing }`: the modules the manual includes (also through module references) counted once each by their state in the translation status, and their blocks by theirs; since #656 also `design` and `cover`, the same counts over the units of the manual's page design's region text and of its own text (its cover image alternative text; since #731 its title and group titles too), and since #731 `labels`, over the project labels' units (the same for every manual in a language), as the status lists them (all 0 for none). A module counts exactly where `GET /translations/status` lists it, so that list with `filter[manual]` and `filter[lang]` names the modules counted (the editor's link from a cell). Ordered by the manual's title, its ID and the configured order of target languages; paged; `filter[manual]`, `filter[lang]` (a manual the reader may not see, or a language that is no target language, matches nothing). `@BeyondTheCommit()`, as the status (`at-provider` comes from the jobs).
- **`GET /reports/module-usage`** (`ReportModuleUsageAction`, `core/module-usage-report.ts`): one `ModuleUsage` (`id` the module ID) per module the reader may see in some language (a file that cannot be read gives none), from `ReadableModuleIndex`. `attributes`: `moduleId`, `title`, `type`, `deprecated` (its source-language file's, else its first one's); `meta`: `languages` (those the reader sees it in), `usage` (`manuals`, `modules`) and `usedIn` (at most 50, as on `GET /modules`). `filter[unused]=true` lists the modules no manual includes, directly or through module references (a module only other unused modules refer to is unused too); `false` the others; `filter[type]` a module type. `sort`: `id`, `title`, `type`, `manuals`, `modules`, `usage` (their sum), comma-separated, `-` for descending; ties by title, then ID; default `title`. Filtered and sorted before paging.
- **CSV**: a `/csv` sub-route of each (`GET /reports/translations/csv`, `GET /reports/module-usage/csv`; a sub-route rather than `Accept: text/csv`, so a plain link downloads it), with the same `?ref=`, filters and sort, unpaged: the whole report. `text/csv; charset=utf-8; header=present`, `Content-Disposition: attachment` (`translation-coverage-<commit>.csv`, `module-usage-<commit>.csv`, the commit's first 12 digits), `nosniff`, `X-Ccms-Rows`. RFC 4180 with CRLF; a value a spreadsheet would run as a formula starts with `'` (`csvCell`, the term export's). Translations: one row per manual and language with the counts and the percentages up to date (whole percent, rounded down), then its page design's, its own text's and the project labels' unit counts (#656, #731: `design units…`, `cover units…`, `label units…`, 0 without); module usage: one row per module, the manuals and modules using it as their IDs, one per line in their cell, all of them. Errors stay JSON:API; any other query parameter is 400 `unsupported-parameter`.

## Releases

The `releases` component (`src/releases/`, #40): a publisher cuts a release, an immutable, reproducible snapshot of the whole of `main` at one commit, tied to a product revision (defaults from #2 and #241: the whole repository; the product a value of a configured facet).

- **The product** is a value of the product facet: `release.productFacet` in `openfolia.yaml`, else the first configured facet (`productFacet`, `@openfolia/content-model`), read at the commit released. Never hardcoded. The revision is free text in the tag name's character set (`ReleaseRevision`: one ref component of `A-Za-z0-9._-`, at most 100 characters).
- **`POST /releases`** (`CreateReleaseAction`) takes `{ data: { type: "Release", attributes: { product, revision, notes?, notesByLanguage?, major? } } }` and answers 201 with the `Release` and `Location`. It needs `release.publish` in a role whose scope covers the product in every language, module type and audience (a role restricted in any of them, or to another product, is 403: the release holds all of it), and `content.read`, which opening `main` for `openfolia.yaml` needs (`ON_TOP_OF_READING`). `notes` (optional) is plain text with line breaks, 1 to 10,000 characters once trimmed (`\r\n` stored as `\n`), no control characters but tabs and line breaks, no line starting with `---` and none of the form `[notes:<lang>]`; notes ending in `Key: value` lines are 422 `invalid-notes` (Git would read them as trailers). `notesByLanguage` (#138, optional) holds more summaries by language tag, each like `notes`; a language not configured at `main` is 422 `invalid-language` (`meta.lang`), and all notes together make at most 60,000 characters (`MAX_MESSAGE`, else 422 `invalid-notes`). `major` (default false) marks a major change. Errors: 409 `release-exists` (`meta.releaseId` when it is a release), `release-pending`, `branch-missing`; 422 `invalid-product` (`meta.facet`), `no-product-facet`, `invalid-release-name`, `invalid-notes`, `invalid-language`.
- **The tag** (`ReleaseTags`, below): annotated, on the commit `main` pointed to when the request read `openfolia.yaml`; the message is `Release <product> <revision>` (` (major)` added for a major one), then the notes, then each language's notes after a line `[notes:<lang>]` (by language tag), so the tag alone reproduces them (`releaseTextOf` reads them back), then the trailers `Product`, `Revision` and `Actor`; the publisher as tagger; the tagger date is the release's `publishedAt` (whole seconds). Read the content as released with `?ref=<attributes.tag>`; a module's `meta.status.release` names the newest release tag holding it (see "Changes and status").
- **Immutable.** A product and revision, or a tag name (in any case), is taken once: 409 `release-exists`, whatever the commit. No code path moves or deletes a tag, and the database refuses to change or delete a published row (trigger `releases_immutable`, migration `0017_releases`; `TRUNCATE` too).
- **Order: the database, Git, the database.** The release is first reserved (a row without `tag_object`; the unique indexes on `(product, revision)` and `lower(tag)` make two requests for one release never both reach Git), then tagged with the reservation's exact data, then published (`tag_object` set) with a `release.publish` audit event (product, facet, revision, tag, tag object, commit, `major`) and a `release.published` outbox row in that transaction (below). A client error from the tag writer drops the reservation. One cut off between the steps (the process stopped, Git or the database failed) stays reserved, invisible to reads: a later `POST` of the same release, once the reservation is `RESUME_AFTER_MS` (60 s) old, finishes it with the reservation's data, never its own (answering 201 with it; the audit event says `resumed` and who published it), and until then it is 409 `release-pending` (`meta.releaseId`, `meta.retryAfter` in seconds), so a client tells it from a release that exists and tries again. Errors the tag writer gives before writing anything (`invalid-message`, `invalid-identity`, `invalid-trailer`, `invalid-commit`) drop the reservation. A tag of that name is taken as the release's only if its commit, tagger date and message are exactly the reservation's (trailers are never trusted); any other is a tag made outside the API, and the reservation is dropped (409).
- **Reading:** `GET /releases` (paged, newest first by `publishedAt`, withdrawn ones too; `filter[product]`, `filter[major]=true|false`, `filter[state]=published|withdrawn` (#89)) and `GET /releases/:releaseId` (404 for an ID that names no published release, malformed or not) need `content.read` in any scope: releases are released content, so every reader may know them; what a reader sees at a tag is limited to their scope. Both are part of the delivery API.
