- **Notes are for every reader** (by design, #241's defaults): `notes` and every language's `notesByLanguage` are shown to whoever may read releases, whatever their scope, as the release itself is; a publisher writes them as public text and names nothing a reader may not know.
- **`Release`**: `attributes` `product`, `productFacet` (as configured then), `revision`, `tag`, `tagObject`, `commit`, `notes` (null for none), `notesByLanguage` (`{}` for none), `major`, `publishedBy` (`{ kind, id }`), `publishedAt`, `withdrawal` (`{ by, at, reason }`, null while it stands).
- **Changes** (#138): **`GET /releases/:releaseId/changes`** (`content.read`, `ListReleaseChangesAction`; delivery API) lists, paged, one `ReleaseChange` per manual and language that changed between the release's commit and the product's previous release (the newest one cut before it, by `publishedAt` then ID, that is not withdrawn; a maintenance line's release counts within its line, see "Maintenance lines"; `meta.previous`, null for the first): `id` `<lang>/<manualId>`, `attributes` `manual`, `lang`, `meta` `change` (`added`, `removed`, `changed`), `fields` (the manifest's members that differ) and `modules` (each module the manifest names, on either side, whose file in that language differs: `moduleId`, `change`, `fields`, `blocks`, the block diff of #23). A product's first release lists every manual as `added`, in every language. A release holds the whole repository, so every manual counts, whatever the product. Derived from Git: a pair of releases is diffed once, as `RELEASE_READER` (a service reading all released content) sees it (`releaseDiff`, `core/release-changes.ts`, over `ChangeSetBranches.changedPaths` and `seenOnBoth`), and kept, in memory (the last 1,024 pairs, `DIFFS_KEPT`, more than the changelog scans; `ReleaseChangeReader`) and in the database (#478: `release_diffs`, `ReleaseDiffStore`, by the two commit IDs), so the first changelog after a restart reads them instead of working them out from Git again (a release's are worked out when it is cut, for its event and outputs). That table is rebuildable state: a pair of commits never changes, who may see what is not in it, any row may be deleted (it is worked out again on the next read), a row of another `RELEASE_DIFF_FORMAT` is not read (raise it when what `releaseDiff` gives changes), and a diff that cannot be written is logged and still answered. Only the manuals that may have changed for someone are in a diff (a manual on both sides, its manifest the same, naming no changed module, is left out), each naming only its changed modules; what a reader sees is filtered from that (`changesFor`) without reading a module again (a manifest whose `entries` changed is compared again as they see it). Both tags are read through `ReadableContent` as the reader reads them, so a manual or module outside their scope is not there, exactly like a missing one, and is neither listed nor counted; a manual is listed in a language only when they may read it in that language (`ReadableContent.seesManualIn`). A module that leaves the reader's scope between the two reads as `removed`, as for a change set. More than 10,000 changed files is 422 `too-many-changes`, for every reader: counted over the whole repository, it tells a scoped reader only that one release was very large. That outcome is kept like a diff (decided once per pair, before any manifest is read), and a reader's part of a diff is kept too (per pair and reading key).
- **Changelog** (#138): **`GET /changelog`** (`content.read`, `ListChangelogAction`; delivery API) lists the releases newest first, withdrawn ones too (marked, with the reason), each a `Release` with `meta.changedManuals`: how many manuals it changed as the reader sees them (null past `too-many-changes`). Filters: `filter[product]`, `filter[major]=true|false`, and `filter[manual]`, `filter[lang]`: only releases whose changes, as the reader sees them, hold that manual or language, filtered before paging (so neither the list nor the total tells of a manual outside their scope). Those two are worked out from Git per reader, so only the `CHANGELOG_SCANNED` (200) newest releases matching the other filters are looked through then; `meta.truncated` says when there were more. A release too large to compare is listed whatever those filters ask (it may have changed it), with `meta.changedManuals: null`.
- **Withdrawing** (#138): **`POST /releases/:releaseId/withdraw`** (`WithdrawReleaseAction`) with `{ meta: { reason } }` (required; plain text with line breaks, 1 to 2,000 characters once trimmed) marks the release withdrawn and answers 200 with it. It needs what cutting it needs: `release.publish` in a role whose scope covers the product in every language, module type and audience (judged by `openfolia.yaml` at the release's commit), and `content.read`. In one transaction: a row in `release_withdrawals`, a `release.withdraw` audit event (the reason as its comment; product, revision, tag, commit) and a `release.withdrawn` outbox row. Nothing else changes: the tag stays (no code path moves or deletes one) and reads at it keep working; the release no longer counts as a module's newest release (`meta.status.release`, the module history, a chunk's `meta.release`: `ReadableRefs.releases` leaves out the tags of withdrawn releases, `WithdrawnReleases`, `DrizzleWithdrawnReleases`), and the next release of the product counts its changes from the one before. Where it is read by tag, it is marked (#330): `GET /refs` gives the tag `meta.withdrawal`, and a content read at the tag answers `X-Ccms-Withdrawn: true` (`ReadableRefs.withdrawalAt`, set by `SnapshotInterceptor`, which also puts it in the ETag). It cannot be undone: subscribers are told, so a second withdraw is 409 `release-withdrawn`, and the database refuses to change or delete a withdrawal (trigger `release_withdrawals_immutable`, migration `0023_release_notes`; `TRUNCATE` too). A screen (#89) confirms it with the reason rather than offering an undo.
- **Outbox** (#138, `ReleaseEvents`, `DrizzleReleaseEvents`; keyed by the release's ID, once per topic): `release.published` `{ releaseId, product, revision, tag, tagObject, commit, major, level ("major" or "minor", which the notifications read), actor, manualIds, modules: [{ moduleId, lang, change }], complete }`, the manuals and modules worked out as `RELEASE_READER` (a service that reads all released content) sees them, at most 1,000 of each (`complete: false` when cut, past `too-many-changes`, or when the previous release cannot be read (not found, invalid content): then every manual, and no modules, or none when even that cannot be read; an outage leaves the release reserved for a later request to finish); a consumer tells each person only of what they read (the notifications judge a manual by its audience, so a reader restricted by language or module type may hear that a manual they read changed although the change lies outside their part of it; past 1,000 manuals the rest are not told; webhooks' `manual.released` and `module.released`, #42, one per changed manual and module). `release.withdrawn` `{ releaseId, product, revision, tag, commit, reason, actor }`.
- **Tables** `releases` (`repos/releases.tables.ts`, `DrizzleReleaseStore`): `id` (UUID version 7, its time to the millisecond, so releases cut in one second keep their order), `product`, `product_facet`, `revision`, `tag`, `tag_object` (null while reserved), `commit`, `notes`, `notes_by_language` (jsonb, `{}` for none) and `major` (both since `0023_release_notes`), `published_by_kind`, `published_by_id`, `published_at`, with the formats checked by the database too; indexes for the list and its product filter. `release_withdrawals` (`0023_release_notes`): `release_id` (the release, primary key), `withdrawn_by_kind`, `withdrawn_by_id`, `withdrawn_at`, `reason`; its own table, so the release row stays immutable.
- **Outputs** (#249): every release has a full set of files, one PDF per manual, variant (its manifest's) and configured language at its tag, and its offline packages (#59, below), made in background jobs and kept in the blob store.
  - **Triggered from the publishing transaction**, not from the outbox: `CreateReleaseAction` enqueues `release.plan-outputs` (`{ releaseId }`, keyed by it) in the transaction that publishes the release and writes `release.published`, so the job exists exactly when the release does (a resumed release is published once, so planned once), with the jobs component's retries and crash recovery and no polling consumer of its own.
  - **Planning** (`PlanReleaseOutputsAction`): the manuals at the tag as `RELEASE_READER` sees them. The **affected** ones are rendered: the manuals and languages the release's changes list (as `RELEASE_READER`, against the **nearest earlier release already planned**, #497: the walk back the changelog's previous release takes, `ReleaseChangesAction.previousOf`, to the first one with rows, at most `NEIGHBOURS_SCANNED` (200) steps; `ReleaseChangesAction.against` and `changedPathsWithin` compare the two tags), every language of a manual changed in the source language (translations fall back to it), and everything for a product's first release, when any file but a module or a manifest changed (the configuration with its labels, a page design, its own template under `templates/`, a snippet, a symbol, a term, …) or the two cannot be compared (`too-many-changes`, unreadable content). The rest **reuse** the previous release's `done` output of the same manual, variant and language: a `done` row with the same `sha256` and `meta.reusedFrom` naming the output that holds the file (however many releases reused it since); one without a good file there yet (queued or rendering) joins its **reuse group** (#497, `reuse_group`: the first output's ID, carried on by each release that reuses it) and waits for that file instead of rendering it again; one whose previous output failed is rendered. A PDF whose page design prints the release or its date (#395, `DesignsInUse.printsRelease`: `pageDesignShowsRelease` of the manual's design; the built-in design only with a cover image of the manual's own) is never reused: each release renders it, with its own release and date. The rows and one `release.outputs` job per queued row (`{ outputId }`, keyed by it) are written in batches of `PLAN_BATCH` (500), each in one transaction, and a row the release has already is left alone (unique per release, manual, variant, language and kind), so a planning attempt cut off and run again adds nothing twice and goes on where it stopped. **Bounded** (#477): a release holds the whole repository, so its outputs are every manual at its tag × its variants × the configured languages, whatever the product (a manual names no product). More than `CCMS_RELEASE_MAX_OUTPUTS` (default 20,000; a repository of 300 manuals in 8 languages has about 6,000) plans nothing: rendering is costly, and a release starts no unbounded work. The refusal is recorded (`release_output_refusals`: reason `too-many-outputs`, the count, the limit) and logged once, as a warning; run again (a retry, a restart, planning it again) at the same limit, the job reads nothing and logs nothing, as a tag's count never changes. Once the limit is raised, planning it again plans it, and the first batch's transaction deletes the refusal. **Strictly in the order cut** (#527): in its product's turn (`PlanningTurns`), a release's planning first plans the product's releases cut before it whose planning is still to come (`earlierToPlan`, `planning-order.ts`: not planned to the end, `ReleaseOutputStore.plannedWholly`, and a plan job not ended, `ReleaseStore.before` and `JobQueue.openKeys`, the nearest `NEIGHBOURS_SCANNED`), oldest first, then itself, so each release is compared with the one cut just before it and joins the reuse groups of that one's queued outputs, whichever worker took the turn and in whatever order the plan jobs were claimed. One of those whose planning throws is logged and passed over (its own job, retried, plans it), so a release that cannot be planned never holds up those after it; one whose planning ended without being planned to the end (refused for its size, failed for good) is not waited for. At most `EARLIER_PLANNING_MS` (10 of the attempt's 15 minutes) goes to earlier releases: past it the release is planned next and the rest in later turns (they then join its groups, below), counted from the attempt's start (waiting for the turn included), so a long backlog does not use up an attempt on other releases. A planning that ran to its end is recorded (`release_plannings`, `ReleaseOutputStore.markPlanned`, migration `0061_release_plannings`: the release and the database's time, only added to): a release planned in another's turn is not planned again by its own job (or a publisher's "plan again"), which only tops up its render jobs and queues lost ones again; a planning cut off part way has no record and goes on from where the rows stop. **Later releases planned first** (#497; since #527 only when an earlier release's planning was passed over, or ended without rows and is planned again by a publisher): when a release is planned, the same product's releases cut after it may have rows already (`ReleaseStore.after`, `ReleaseOutputStore.planned`, the nearest ones: those whose walk back reaches this release before any other planned one). Each of their outputs that this release has unchanged (not affected between the two, compared as above; same manual, variant, language, kind, audience and QR code; neither printing its release; theirs not failed) is joined to this one's reuse group: this output takes their group, or, when it already reuses an earlier release's, their group is merged into its own (`ReleaseOutputStore.joinGroups`, in the batch's transaction). The file is then rendered once, at the oldest release still waiting for it (below), and given forward. A file a later release rendered before this one was planned stays theirs: it carries their commit, so this release renders its own (#328). What changes the bytes is the key, so nothing else is shared: the app's branding (database only) never reaches a PDF, and labels and titles per language (#752) are the configuration and the manifests, compared above (`release-outputs.spec.ts`, "what a file is reused for").
  - **Rendering** (`RenderReleaseOutputAction`, `release.outputs`: 5 retries from 30 s doubling, 10 minutes an attempt, `CCMS_RENDER_CONCURRENCY` at a time per server, below, so outputs leave PDF workers to readers): at the release's tag only, through `RenderManualPdfAction`, as a service actor whose scope is the manual's audience (`outputRenderer`: `content.read` up to it; the most restricted level for none), never the publisher's scope or any reader's, so the file is the same for everyone who may read the manual. The PDF goes into the blob store (`application/pdf`, at most 512 MiB) and the row becomes `done` with its hash and size. An attempt moves `queued` or `rendering` to `rendering` (`attempts` one more); a `done` or `failed` row is left alone, and so is one queued again since its job was (the job carries the row's `retries`; starting and failing compare it), so a late attempt or a late report of a job failing for good never acts on the newer job's output, and `done` is never left, so a late attempt changes nothing and a failure never replaces a good file.
  - **Order and scale** (#497): planning writes every output's row, but only `QUEUED_PER_RELEASE` (40) of a release's outputs have a render job at a time (`scheduled_at` set; `ScheduleReleaseOutputsAction`, under an advisory lock per release, `ReleaseOutputStore.schedule`); each output that ends (done, failed, or found finished) queues the next, so pg-boss's queue stays small however many releases wait. Renders run newest release first: a release's render jobs have its `releasePriority` (the seconds from 2020 to its `publishedAt`, whatever the product), so a new release's files never wait behind an older release's backlog. Plan jobs are claimed newest first too (the plan job at the release's `releasePriority`; planned again by a publisher at `INTERACTIVE_PRIORITY`), so a new release's planning starts within moments whatever other products' releases still wait to be planned; within a product the releases are planned strictly in the order cut (#527, above): a new release waits only for its own product's earlier releases still to be planned, planned first in its turn. One planning per product at a time (`PlanningTurns`). Interactive work goes ahead of all of them: a publisher's retry at `INTERACTIVE_PRIORITY`, and just behind those, at `PROMOTED_PRIORITY`, a file a reader asked for before it was rendered (`GET …/file` still answers 409 `output-not-ready`; that output is scheduled at once or its job raised, and marked `promoted_at`). **Bounded:** at most `PROMOTED_PER_RELEASE` (8) of a release's unfinished outputs are moved up at a time, counted under the same per-release lock, so a reader asking for every file of a backlog (any reader of the release may: a download is the interactive request the order is for) adds at most 8 jobs to its 40 and never holds up a publisher's retry; the rest wait their turn. **Per reader** (#497, `ReaderPromotions`): one reader (by actor) moves at most `PROMOTIONS_PER_READER` (4) outputs up within a sliding minute, on this server (in memory, as the stable addresses' limit); past it the file waits its turn at its release's priority and the answer is the same 409, no other error. Only a move counts: a place is reserved before the move is tried (synchronously, so a reader's requests in parallel never overrun it) and given back when nothing moved, so asking again for one moved up already, or for one not queued, costs nothing. So one reader asking for every file of a backlog leaves the release's other 4 places to other readers. PDFs only: an offline package (#59) is never moved up (it is built once the release's PDFs are done) and costs no place. Before rendering, a render job takes a `done` file of its reuse group if one exists, made for its release or an earlier one (a reuse, no render). Otherwise it renders the file at the release of its group's **oldest output still to render** (`oldestPendingIn`; usually its own), finishes that output with it (should rendering at that older release fail, the error is that release's: the output is rendered at its own release instead), and every queued or rendering output of the group cut at or after that release gets the file (`finishGroup`). Both match the group's ID and the output's manual, variant, language, kind and audience, so a file is reused only for the very same inputs, and only forward, as reuse always went: no file carries a later commit than its release's (#328). So one file is rendered once for every release that has it unchanged, even when the newest release's job runs first. **Lost jobs:** a render job dropped without ending its output would hold one of its release's places; render jobs are kept a year while they wait (`retentionSeconds`), and `ReleaseOutputSweeper` (two minutes after start-up, then every ten minutes) gives the releases whose outputs still to render none moved for 30 minutes (`stalled`, at most 100 a sweep by release ID, each sweep going on after the last one's, so every such release comes round however many merely wait behind newer ones) their scheduled outputs' jobs again (keyed, so one still there is left alone; a moved-up one at `PROMOTED_PRIORITY`) and tops them up. With several workers a product's releases are planned one at a time, in the order cut (#527), so none is planned while one before it is; only when the planning turn's connection is lost (below, "What one process kept") may two be planned at once, which costs reuse, never correctness. Release renders are **background** work in the PDF pool: they wait behind readers' PDFs and are not kept in the renderer's cache. A background render's compile deadline (`CCMS_PDF_TIMEOUT_MS`) counts from when a worker starts it, not while it waits (it gives up after `BACKGROUND_WAIT_MS`, 5 minutes, of waiting: `renderer-busy`, retried), and a reader asking for the same PDF while it waits (the same cache key; the renderer joins one render per key) raises it to interactive (`WorkerOptions.raise`), so a reader never waits at background priority. `CCMS_RENDER_CONCURRENCY` (default half the PDF workers, at least 1, and all of them for a worker; at most `CCMS_PDF_WORKERS`, checked at start) is how many a process renders at once. The sweep's cursor is shared in `release_output_sweeps` (#527), so several workers' sweeps go on from each other's.
  - **Failing:** an error that says the output can never render at that tag (a `DomainError` but `UnavailableError`, `ContendedError` and `TooManyRequestsError`: `pdf-accessibility`, `render-timeout`, `render-failed`, `invalid-content`, …) fails it at once; any other (the renderer busy, the blob store or the database down) is noted on the row (`error`, state `rendering`) and thrown, so the queue retries it, and once its job failed for good the handler's `failed` fails it with the error noted last. Failing (`FailReleaseOutputAction`) writes `failed`, the error (`code`, and a `detail` of at most 1,000 characters: a domain error's message, else a generic text, the cause only in the log) and a `release.output-failed` outbox row `{ releaseId, outputId, product, revision, tag, commit, manualId, variant, lang, kind, audience, code }` in one transaction, keyed `<outputId>/<retries>` (once per failure), for notifications (#252: the release's publishers hear of it). The previous release's output stays the latest good one.
  - **Recovered (#551):** a failed output can still become `done`: a late file (an attempt still rendering when its job gave up), a publisher's retry, or its reuse group's file. Whenever an output that was told failed (it was `failed`, or it has `retries` above 0, since only a failed output is retried) becomes `done`, a `release.output-recovered` outbox row with the same fields but `code` is written in the transaction that finishes it, keyed `<outputId>` (once: a `done` output never fails again), so the publishers who heard of the failure hear the file is ready after all. A corrective event rather than holding the failure back: a failed output is final as far as anyone can know, and only something outside its job (a late file, a retry) changes that.
  - **`GET /releases/:releaseId/outputs`** (`ListReleaseOutputsAction`; delivery API): paged, by manual, variant, language (the offline packages first); `filter[manual]`, `filter[lang]`, `filter[kind]` (`pdf`, `offline-package`), `filter[state]` (`queued`, `rendering`, `done`, `failed`). **`GET /releases/:releaseId/outputs/:outputId/file`** (`ReleaseOutputFileAction`; delivery API): the file from the blob store, never rendered on request (409 `output-not-ready` until `done`), `Content-Disposition: attachment` with `<manual>_<variant>_<lang>_<revision>.pdf` (`outputFileName`: ASCII letters, digits, `.` and `-` per part), `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable` (by design: the bytes never change, and who may read released content is decided per request; a browser that downloaded it keeps it), the hash as `ETag`, `nosniff`. Both need `content.read` at the release's tag (opened as the reader reads released content) and show an output only to a reader with one role that covers all of its manual: the manual's audience at the tag (`audience`, kept on the row), the output's language and the source language (an untranslated module falls back to it), with no module type and no facet left out (`ReadableContent.seesWholeManualIn`). An output holds whatever the manual's audience reads, so a reader restricted by module type, facet or language would get what their scope leaves out: they read the manual's PDF route (rendered for their scope) instead. Others get 404, exactly like a missing one, and the list filters before paging (the distinct audiences and languages of the release's rows are judged, then the page is queried among them), so neither list nor total tells of one outside their scope. A withdrawn release's outputs stay readable, `meta.withdrawn: true`.
  - **`GET /releases/:releaseId/outputs/archive`** (`ReleaseArchiveAction`; delivery API, #469): all of the release's files as one ZIP. It holds every output the reader may read, judged exactly as the list judges it (`content.read` at the tag, `seesWholeManualIn` over the distinct audiences and languages), that is `done`; the others (queued, rendering, failed) are left out. `X-Ccms-Archive-Files` says how many it holds, `X-Ccms-Archive-Left-Out` how many readable ones are not in it. Entries are `<manual>/<lang>/<variant>.pdf` in the list's order (`archiveEntryNames`: each part ASCII letters, digits, `.` and `-`, anything else `-`, never empty and never starting with a dot, so no part is `.` or `..`; a name taken already, ignoring case, gets `-2`, `-3`, … before the extension). The ZIP downloads as `<product>_<revision>.zip` (`Content-Disposition: attachment` with `filename*`, RFC 6266). It is streamed, never built in memory (`zipStream`, `base/core/zip-stream.ts`, on fflate's `Zip`): entries are stored without compression (PDFs are compressed already), one blob open at a time, and a chunk is read only once the one before went out, so memory stays at a few chunks (the response's stream buffers at most 16; 2 GiB streamed with a peak RSS of about 80 MiB). Its exact length is known beforehand (`zipSize`), so it carries `Content-Length`; every entry's time is the release's `publishedAt`, so the same files make the same bytes. `Cache-Control: private, no-store` (it holds more once more are done), `nosniff`. No ZIP64, so an archive is at most 4 GiB less one byte; past `CCMS_RELEASE_ARCHIVE_MAX_BYTES` (default 2 GiB) it is 422 `release-archive-too-large` (`meta.size`, `meta.limit`), checked before a file is read. None done (or none readable) is 409 `release-archive-empty` (`meta.total`, `meta.notReady`). A blob store failing before anything went out is 503 `blob-store-unavailable`; once part of it went out (or a file is gone or not the length its row says), the connection is cut, so no client keeps a short archive. Not audited, as a single file's download is not. A query parameter is 400.
  - **`POST /releases/:releaseId/outputs/:outputId/retry`** (`RetryReleaseOutputAction`, no body, 202): queues a `failed` output again (`queued`, error cleared, `retries` one more), its job and a `release.output-retry` audit event in one transaction. Needs what withdrawing needs: `release.publish` in a role whose scope covers the product in every language, module type and audience (at the release's commit), and `content.read`. The output need not be one the publisher may download (a role scoped to the product by its facet reads none): retrying shows nothing of the file, and the answer is the output in full only to a publisher who may read it (`seesWholeManualIn`, as `GET` judges it); anyone else gets only its `state`, `meta.attempts` and `meta.retries`, every other attribute and `meta` member absent (#340), so retrying never shows what reading would not. At most `MAX_RETRIES` (10) times per output, then 409 `retry-limit`; 409 `output-busy` (nothing changed) while the job that failed it is still ending. Anything but `failed` is 409 `output-not-failed`, so no request renders what is queued, rendering or done: the only request that starts a render is this one, once per failure.
  - **`POST /releases/:releaseId/outputs/plan`** (`ReplanReleaseOutputsAction`, no body, 202 `{ meta: { queued } }`): queues the planning again (keyed by the release; `queued` false while one is queued or running) and a `release.outputs-plan` audit event, in one transaction, for a release whose planning failed for good (an outage longer than its retries: 5, from 30 s doubling) or was cut off. Planning writes only the rows the release does not have and renders only those, so it renders nothing twice. Needs what retrying needs. It answers only `queued`, nothing of any output. A release whose planning was refused (the record the planning job wrote) and is still over the limit is 422 `too-many-outputs` with `meta` `{ wanted, max }`, after the permission check, and nothing is queued or audited; no tag is read for it.
  - **Progress** (#456): `GET /releases/:releaseId` gives the release `meta.outputs` (`ReleaseOutputsProgressAction`), where its outputs stand, so a client shows that they are being prepared instead of "no files" while a fresh release has no rows yet: `{ state, total, queued, rendering, done, failed, refused, queue }`. `queue` (#497) is `{ ahead, releases }`, null when nothing is ahead: while the release renders and some of its outputs are queued behind later releases', `ahead` counts the queued and rendering outputs of the same product's releases cut after it (which go first; other products' releases are not counted, nor the caller's to know of) and `releases` is 0; while its planning waits (its plan job queued, waiting to retry, or running while the earlier ones are planned in its turn; a job from before the release was planned to the end, `release_plannings`, is not its planning any more, so one planned in another release's turn reads as rendering, never `failed` should that job later run out of tries), `releases` counts the same product's releases cut before it still to be planned (`earlierToPlan`, the nearest `NEIGHBOURS_SCANNED`), which are planned first (#527: in the order cut). Only for one who may plan the release again (`release.publish` for its product, as `refused.wanted`): anyone else gets `queue: null` whatever the queue holds (the count is not even made), since it counts manuals they may not read. `state` is `planning` while the release's `release.plan-outputs` job (keyed by it, `JobQueue.latest`) is queued, running or waiting to retry, whatever the rows say; then `rendering` while any output is queued or rendering; then `failed` when any failed, when planning failed for good or was cancelled (planned in batches, it may have left some: the set is incomplete), or when planning was refused; else `done` (`total` 0: no outputs the reader may read, no manuals at its tag or none they read whole). `refused` (#477) is `{ reason: 'too-many-outputs', wanted, max }` for a release whose planning was refused and is still over the limit, `max` the limit now (null otherwise and while it is planned again; once the limit is raised past it, the state is `failed` with `refused` null until it is planned again); `wanted` counts every manual at the tag, so only a reader who may plan it again (`release.publish` for its product) gets it, others null. Derived from the job and the rows, never stored. The counts are those of the outputs the reader may read, judged as the outputs list judges them (`seesWholeManualIn` over the distinct audiences and languages, then one grouped count), so they tell nothing of an output outside their scope; whether planning is under way says only that the release's files are being made. Null when the tag cannot be opened as theirs; any other failure of the Git read or the job lookup fails the read (a 5xx), as the outputs list would. Only the release's own read has it (the changelog and the other answers do not; the list has `meta.outputs` with `queue` always null, so a row costs no count over the other releases); a client polls the release while `state` is `planning` or `rendering`.
  - **Offline packages** (#59, `kind` `offline-package`): planning adds one per configured language and audience level up to the highest any manual at the tag has (a manifest without an audience counts as the last level; one package with `audience` null when no levels are configured; none without manuals; `packagesAt`), counted against `CCMS_RELEASE_MAX_OUTPUTS`, and none at all with `CCMS_OFFLINE_PACKAGES=false` (default true). Its row has `manual_id` `*` and its level in `variant` (`*` for none; `packageVariant`), so the table's unique key holds; the resource gives `manual` and `variant` null and the level in `audience`. It is never reused and has no reuse group. `ReleaseOutputStore.schedule` holds a release's packages back until none of its PDFs is `queued` or `rendering` (a PDF retried afterwards is not in a package built before it), and the file route never moves one up the queue. A job of its own builds it, `release.offline-package` (`BUILD_OFFLINE_PACKAGE`, `BuildOfflinePackageJobHandler`: two hours an attempt, 2 retries from 60 s, one at a time per server; the attempt stops between files once it is cut off), through `RenderReleaseOutputAction`, which hands it to `BuildOfflinePackageAction`: at the release's tag only, read through `ReadableContent` as a service actor of exactly the package's audience (`outputRenderer`), so it holds only what that level reads: each manual it may read, resolved per variant as that actor reads it, rendered by `HtmlRenderer` (render-html) into one page (`manuals/<manual>/<variant>.html`, `OfflineSite`, `repos/render-html-offline-site.ts`: render-html's `offlinePageHtml`, `offlineIndexHtml`), the images and attached files those pages show (`media/<sha256><ext>`, from the blob store, else the repository's `assets/`), the symbols' artwork, the release's done PDFs of those manuals in that language whose audience the actor reads (`pdf/<manual>/<lang>/<variant>.pdf`), the theme's stylesheets, fonts and their licences (`assets/`), `index.html` (every manual and variant, a search over titles and headings: a JSON data block and `assets/search.js`), and last `manifest.json` (`format` `openfolia-offline-package`, `version` 1, the release, product, revision, language, audience, every other file's path, size and SHA-256, and `leftOut`: a manual's variant that cannot be resolved at the tag, a file stored nowhere, `unsafe-svg` for an SVG that cannot be made inert). **Untrusted files:** a reader opens the files from disk, with no response header to protect them, so every SVG (an image's or a symbol's artwork) is written through `sanitizeSvg` (`@openfolia/xml`: read strictly, no DTD, then written again with drawing elements only, no `script`, `foreignObject`, animation or `image`, a `style` sheet or `style` only when it reaches nothing outside, an `a`'s shapes without the link, no `on*` attribute, no link but `#id` and no `url(…)` but `url(#id)`), and any other type a browser shows as a page or runs (`DANGEROUS_TYPES`, any XML) is named `.bin`. Every link is relative; the pages carry a Content Security Policy that allows the package's own files only, and no script but the index's search. **Streamed and bounded:** `zipStreamOf` (`base/core/zip-stream.ts`) writes the ZIP while the blob store stores it, one entry at a time, never the whole package in memory; past `CCMS_RELEASE_ARCHIVE_MAX_BYTES` or 50,000 files (`MAX_PACKAGE_ENTRIES`) it stops and the output fails with 422 `offline-package-too-large` (the PDFs stay); its bytes count as the PDF archive's in the storage quota. A reader sees and downloads a package as any output, judged by its level and language (`seesWholeManualIn`), else 404; its file is `application/zip`, named `offline_<audience>_<lang>_<revision>.zip`. Not in the archive (`…/outputs/archive`), the iiRDS and DITA packages or the stable addresses (all PDFs only). Its failure notifies the publishers who read its level (`release.output-failed` with `kind` and `audience`). `tests/offline-packages.e2e.spec.ts` checks the ZIP's contents per level, the manifest's checksums, the reader's 404 and opens a package in headless Chromium from the file system with every network request refused.
  - **`ReleaseOutput`**: `attributes` `manual`, `variant` (both null for an offline package), `lang`, `kind` (`pdf` or `offline-package`), `audience` (an offline package's level; null for a PDF), `state`, `sha256` and `size` (null until done), `error` (`{ code, detail }`, null), `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `finishedAt`; `meta` `reusedFrom` (`{ releaseId, outputId }`, null), `attempts`, `retries`, `withdrawn`; `links.file` (null until done).
  - **Table** `release_outputs` (`repos/releases.tables.ts`, `DrizzleReleaseOutputStore`, migration `0024_release_outputs`): `id` (UUID version 7), `release_id`, `kind`, `manual_id`, `variant`, `lang`, `audience`, `state`, `sha256`, `size`, `error_code`, `error_detail`, `reused_from_release`, `reused_from_output`, `attempts`, `retries`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `finished_at`, `scheduled_at`, `reuse_group` and `promoted_at` (migration `0042_render_queue`, #497, with partial indexes on the unfinished rows per release and on the groups); unique on `(release_id, manual_id, variant, lang, kind)`, the list's order. Kept forever (#241); retention of the blob store (not built) must count these hashes as referenced. `release_output_refusals` (migration `0035_release_output_refusals`, #477): `release_id` (primary key), `reason`, `wanted`, `max`, `refused_at`: a planning refused, until one plans the release.
- **Reproducible:** a PDF built from a release tag is the same every time (`tests/releases.e2e.spec.ts` renders one twice, past the cache; `tests/release-outputs.e2e.spec.ts` compares a release output with the PDF route at the tag): the tag fixes the content, the configuration, the template and the images, and the PDF carries the commit's time, never the time of rendering (#328: `committedAt` of the rendered commit as Typst's `creationTimestamp`; the generated document sets no date of its own).
- Out of scope here: the screens (#89), a notification of a withdrawal (`release.withdrawn` is written; the fan-out has no rule for it yet). A release is cut from `main`, or from a maintenance line's branch (below); blocking checks do not hold it back yet (#89 shows them before the dialog allows it).

## Downloads

The `downloads` component (`src/downloads/`, #139): the download centre, a read model over what releases hold. It has no upload path and no table of its own: an attachment is uploaded and attached in a module ("Attachments"), a manual's PDF is a release output ("Releases", "Outputs"); this lists them as versioned files.

- **A download** is one of two kinds, each with an ID that names it across releases: `attachment:<product>:<module>:<block>:<lang>` (an attachment block of a module in a language) or `manual:<product>:<manual>:<lang>:<variant>` (a manual's PDF; the variant slug last, empty for none). Parts are checked as the content model's IDs (`parseDownloadId`); an ID that does not parse names no download (404 `not-found`).
- **The product rule:** an attachment is a product's when its module's product-facet values (`release.productFacet`, "Releases") include the product or name none; every manual's PDFs are its product's (a release holds every manual).
- **`GET /downloads`** (`ListDownloadsAction`, `content.read`; delivery API): each product's downloads at its **latest release** (the newest that stands, cut from `main`, never a maintenance line's: `ReleaseStore.latestOfEach`, `latest`), paged: one per attachment block of the product's modules the reader may see at the tag (`ReadableContent.attachments`, worked out once per commit and reading key), and one per PDF that is `done` of a manual they read whole there (`visibleIn`, as the release's outputs list judges it). Filters, applied before paging, so neither the page nor `meta.page.total` tells of a file outside the reader's scope: `filter[product]`, `filter[lang]`, `filter[kind]` (`attachment`, `manual`), `filter[mediaType]` (`type/subtype` or `type/*`), `filter[q]` (every word found in the name, description, file name, module title or module or manual ID, folded as keywords are). Ordered by product, then attachments (module, language, block order) before PDFs (manual, variant, language). Read cost: one tag opened per product.
- **`Download`**: `attributes` `kind`, `product`, `lang`, `name` (the block's, or the manual's title at the tag), `description` (the block's; null), `sha256`, `size`, `mediaType`, `fileName` (the block's, or the PDF's served name), `moduleId` and `blockId` (null for a PDF), `manualId` and `variant` (null for an attachment), `release` (`{ id, tag, revision, publishedAt }`); `meta.moduleTitle`; `links.versions`, `links.file` (the latest release's file).
- **`GET /downloads/:id/versions`** (`DownloadVersionsAction`): the download's versions, newest first, paged: what each of the product's main-line releases holds of it (withdrawn ones too, at most `MAX_VERSION_RELEASES`, 500, the newest; `meta.truncated` past them), collapsed to one version per run of releases with the same file (a release that holds the same hash as the version after it adds none; `versionsOf`). A `DownloadVersion` (its ID the release's that first held that file) has `state`: `current` when its releases include the product's latest standing release, `withdrawn` when all of them are withdrawn, else `superseded`; `sha256`, `size`, `mediaType`, `fileName`, `release`, and `changeNote` `{ notes, description }`: that release's notes (in the download's language when it has them, `notesByLanguage`) and the attachment's description; `links.file`. 404 when the product's latest release (the newest that stands; with none standing, the newest) does not hold the file for the reader, decided before any other release is read and before anything is kept for the ID (#764): an ID naming nothing there (invented, a block no longer in the latest release, an attachment in a module above their audience there, a PDF not done there) is 404 at once, exactly like the list, which has no row for it.
  - **Bounded** (#139, #764): the releases' tags are resolved from one ref listing for all of them (`OpenReleasedSnapshotsAction`, `ContentRepository.atEach`, `ContentRepo.atEach` in `@openfolia/content-repo`), never one per tag; per release an attachment's versions read only its module file and the configuration at the tag's commit through the long-running file reader (parsed once per file revision), never the tree. So a cold request starts two git processes (a ref listing for the latest release, one for the rest) whatever the number of releases (`tests/download-versions-bound.e2e.spec.ts` counts them). What each release holds is kept per reading key and download by release (`DownloadObservations`, the 256 last asked for, an entry only once the download is found at the latest release): a tag never moves, so a second request reads only the releases cut since. A PDF's are one query across the releases (`ReleaseOutputStore.across`), and only the tags of releases with a done PDF are opened; a PDF still rendering is asked again next time, never kept. Measured (`tests/perf/download-versions.perf.ts`, nice'd, the attachment changed every 20 releases, at load 30 to 45 on 16 cores): 300 releases cold 0.14 to 0.46 s (#139's walk: 7.1 to 30 s on the same run), 60 releases cold 0.10 to 0.67 s (0.58 to 7.6 s), warm 3 to 8 ms for both.
- **`GET /downloads/:id/versions/:releaseId/file`** (binary): that release's file. An attachment's bytes come from the blob store with the attachment headers ("Attachments": `Content-Disposition` with the block's `fileName` when it fits the stored file (`nameFits`), else the hash; `nosniff`; the sandboxing CSP), `Cache-Control: private, max-age=31536000, immutable` and `ETag` the hash; a PDF goes through the release output's file route (`ReleaseOutputFileAction`, its headers and 409 `output-not-ready` until it is done). Any release of the download's product is taken, a maintenance line's and one past the 500 the versions list too (the reader is judged at its tag all the same). 404 for a release of another product, one that does not hold the file, or one the reader may not read it in. No query parameters (400 `unsupported-parameter`).
- **No new error codes**, no migration. The MCP server reads both lists (`list_downloads`, `list_download_versions`, `backend/mcp`). #59 (the offline package) can list a release's files the same way.

## iiRDS export

The `iirds` component (`src/iirds/`, #431; research note and mapping table: `docs/iirds-export.md`): a release output (a manual in one variant and language) as an iiRDS package, the standard exchange format for technical documentation (iiRDS 1.3, the iiRDS Consortium's; specification and schema CC BY-ND 4.0). Its mapping to the standard's vocabulary is configuration (`iirds` in `openfolia.yaml`, `@openfolia/content-model`'s README), never code.

- **Routes** (`content.read`; delivery API): **`GET /releases/:releaseId/iirds-packages`** (`ListIirdsPackagesAction`): paged, one `IirdsPackage` per release output the reader may read (its `id` is the output's), judged exactly as `GET /releases/:releaseId/outputs` judges them (`seesWholeManualIn`, filtered before paging); `filter[manual]`, `filter[lang]`; attributes `manual`, `variant`, `lang`, `fileName` (`<manual>_<variant>_<lang>_<revision>.iirds`), `pdf`. Listing makes nothing. **`GET …/iirds-packages/:packageId`** (`GetIirdsPackageAction`) makes the package and describes it: `meta.size`, `meta.topics`, `meta.media`, `meta.warnings` (`{ code, id, detail }`, each once). **`GET …/iirds-packages/:packageId/file`**: the package, `application/iirds+zip`, `Content-Disposition: attachment`, exact `Content-Length`, `X-Ccms-Iirds-Warnings`, `private, no-store` (it gains the PDF once that is done), `nosniff`; no query parameters (400).
- **Errors:** 404 for a release that does not exist, or an output that is not the release's or that the reader may not read (the same answer, and checked before anything is made); 422 `iirds-not-configured` when the configuration at the release's tag holds no mapping; 422 `iirds-package-too-large` past `CCMS_RELEASE_ARCHIVE_MAX_BYTES` (the archive's limit, #469) or ZIP's limits without ZIP64; 429 `rate-limited` (too many being made, or sent: **Sends**); 503 `blob-store-unavailable`.
- **Made as released:** at the release's tag (content and configuration), as the manual's audience reads it (`outputRenderer`, as its PDF was rendered), never as the reader does: the same output makes the same package for every reader who may read it, and the same bytes (entries dated with the release's `publishedAt`). `IirdsPlans` keeps the last 32 made (at most 64 MiB of their own bytes), keyed by output, PDF and withdrawal (a plan holding more than a quarter of that is not kept), and every plan still being made on top of those, never dropped, so a second request for one joins it (#551); at most 2 are made at once, server-wide, up to 16 others wait (past that 429 `rate-limited` with `Retry-After`); files read from Git are read again when sent, never kept. Who may read is judged before a plan is looked up. One exception to "the same bytes": an image of the old layout (in Git) that is later also stored in the blob store is then taken from there, named with its stored type.
- **Sends** (#654): a plan too large for `IirdsPlans` to keep is held by its download until it ends, so the downloads are bounded by `PackageSends` (`base/core/package-sends.ts`, shared with the DITA packages; `takeSendTurn` in `base/api/package-send.ts` takes the turn in the controller): at most 4 files of a kind of package (iiRDS: `IirdsSends`; DITA: `DitaSends`, turns of their own) are sent at once, server-wide, and at most `CCMS_PACKAGE_SENDS_PER_ACTOR` (default 1, at most 3, so one reader never holds every turn) of them one reader's; past either, 429 `rate-limited` with `Retry-After`, before any header of the file is set. A client that leaves while the package is made takes no turn, and one that leaves during the send frees it. A download no byte of which is read for `CCMS_PACKAGE_SEND_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS` (default a minute), or still going after `CCMS_PACKAGE_SEND_TIMEOUT_MS` (default half an hour), is cut, freeing its turn, and so is one read more slowly than `CCMS_PACKAGE_SEND_MIN_BYTES_PER_SECOND` on average (default 16 KiB a second) once its first idle timeout has passed; a cut ends the connection, never passing a short file off as whole. The settings apply to both kinds of package; their old names from the DITA export (`CCMS_DITA_SEND_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `CCMS_DITA_SEND_TIMEOUT_MS`, `CCMS_DITA_SEND_MIN_BYTES_PER_SECOND`, `CCMS_DITA_SENDS_PER_ACTOR`) are still read when the new ones are unset, with a warning at start-up; set beside a new one, an old one must hold the same value.
- **The package** (`zipStream`, every entry stored): `mimetype` first (`application/iirds+zip`, no line break), `META-INF/metadata.rdf` (RDF/XML, `iirds-metadata.ts`), the topics `content/<module>.html` (each module of the manual, rendered by `@openfolia/render-html` as the preview renders it, in an HTML document of its own, no CSS; images and cross-references relative), the images and attached files they show `media/<sha256><ext>` (the extension from the stored type), the safety symbols' artwork `symbols/<id>.<ext>`, and the PDF `pdf/<output file name>` once the output is `done` (else the warning `pdf-not-included`). Only files the topics show are in it; one stored nowhere is left out (`missing-asset`). **An empty manual is no exception to the list (#551):** a manual holding no module its audience reads in that variant makes a package of its document alone (and its PDF once done), with no topic and no table of contents, and the warning `manual-empty` (until #551 it was 422 `iirds-manual-empty`, which the list could not tell beforehand without making every package).
- **Metadata:** one `iirds:Package` (version 1.3, title, language, revision, date of creation: the release's), the manual as an `iirds:Document` (document types, `Released` or `Withdrawn` with its date, its PDF as a rendition), each module as an `iirds:Topic` (topic type and subjects from its module type, its HTML file as a rendition), each a version of an `iirds:InformationObject` (`<base>manuals/<id>`, `<base>modules/<id>`), the table of contents as `iirds:DirectoryNode`s (groups with their titles), and the product metadata and qualifications they relate to, declared with labels from the configuration: the audience (`<base>audiences/<id>`, as `Role` or `SkillLevel`), facet values (`<base>facets/<facet>/<value>`) and the variant's values (`<base>variant-attributes/<attribute>/<value>`) as the mapped product metadata class, the release's product as a value of the product facet, a lifecycle facet's values as `iirds:ProductLifeCyclePhase`s. Every IRI is absolute under the configured `baseIri` (each part percent-encoded); every text is XML-escaped (characters XML cannot hold become U+FFFD). Something the mapping does not name is left out of the metadata with a warning (`unmapped-module-type`, `unmapped-audience`, `unmapped-facet`, `unmapped-variant-attribute`, `unmapped-lifecycle-phase`), never refused.
- **The configuration check:** `GET /config` gives `meta.iirdsIssues` when the configuration holds a mapping: what it leaves unmapped and keys naming nothing (`iirdsMappingIssues`).
- **Version and checks:** iiRDS 1.3 is written, with only the terms of the 1.2 core vocabulary (1.3 adds to it and removes nothing; its RDF file is published only after registration). Tests check packages against the vendored 1.2 schema (`tests/iirds/iirds-schema.ts`: classes, domains, ranges, cardinalities, defined instances, information objects) and the iiRDS Validation Tool's own rules (`tests/iirds/official-validator.ts`, no MUST rule broken). The schema is vendored unchanged with its licence (`tests/iirds/vendor/README.md`); the validator's rules quote the specification (with translations), so they are never kept in the repository: Vitest's global setup fetches them once, pinned to a commit and checked by SHA-256, into the git-ignored `backend/api/.cache/` (`tests/iirds/fetch-validator.ts`; without network the iiRDS e2e test fails and says why). Licences: `docs/iirds-export.md`.
- **Defaults** (#431's open questions): made on request and kept a while, not with the release; the lifecycle phase is a facet the mapping names (`lifecyclePhases.facet`), not a module attribute; plain iiRDS (not iiRDS/A or /H), no stylesheet and no glossary; no editor screen yet.

## DITA export

The `dita` component (`src/dita/`, #433; mapping and what has no counterpart: `docs/dita-export.md`): a release output (a manual in one variant and language) as a DITA 1.3 package, written by `@openfolia/dita`'s `exportDita` with the configuration's DITA mapping (`dita` in `openfolia.yaml`) read backwards; no mapping is the import's default one.

- **Routes** (`content.read`; delivery API): **`GET /releases/:releaseId/dita-packages`** (`ListDitaPackagesAction`): paged, one `DitaPackage` per release output the reader may read (its `id` is the output's), judged exactly as `GET /releases/:releaseId/outputs` judges them; `filter[manual]`, `filter[lang]`; attributes `manual`, `variant`, `lang`, `fileName` (`<manual>_<variant>_<lang>_<revision>-dita.zip`). Listing makes nothing. **`GET …/dita-packages/:packageId`** (`GetDitaPackageAction`) makes the package and describes it: `meta.size`, `meta.languages`, `meta.references`, `meta.topics`, `meta.media`, `meta.warnings` (`{ code, module?, block?, lang?, detail, count }`) and `meta.warningsDropped`. **`GET …/dita-packages/:packageId/file`**: the package, `application/zip`, `Content-Disposition: attachment`, exact `Content-Length`, `X-Ccms-Dita-Warnings`, `private, no-store`, `nosniff`. Both take `languages` (`output`, the default, or `all`: every configured language the reader may read the whole manual in, `all` in the file name) and `references` (`conref`, the default, or `resolved`); another value is 400 `invalid-parameter`, another parameter 400 `unsupported-parameter`.
- **Errors:** 404 for a release that does not exist, or an output that is not the release's or the reader may not read (the same answer, checked before anything is made); 422 `dita-package-too-large` past its bounds (`MAX_PACKAGE_MODULES` module lookups per language, a module in a language found or not; `MAX_PACKAGE_NODES` elements and texts; `MAX_PACKAGE_CHARS` characters as written, escaped) or `CCMS_RELEASE_ARCHIVE_MAX_BYTES` or ZIP's limits; 422 `dita-notices-unsupported` for a derived manual gathering safety notices (#670), whose content is parts of modules, regrouped, not topics; 429 `rate-limited`; 503 `blob-store-unavailable`.
- **Made as released:** at the release's tag, as the manual's audience reads it (`outputRenderer`), the modules (the manual's and those its references name, in each language and the source language) read first, then exported in one pure pass. The same output and options make the same bytes for every reader who may read it. `DitaPlans` keeps the last 32 (64 MiB of their own bytes), keyed by output, languages and references, makes at most 2 at once and lets 16 wait, as `IirdsPlans` does; the downloads are bounded and timed out as the iiRDS packages' are, with turns of their own (`DitaSends`; "iiRDS export", **Sends**). The export runs on the event loop, as the iiRDS packages do; its bounds keep a pass to seconds. Images and attached files come from the blob store (else, the old layout, from Git, read again when sent); safety symbols from the repository.
- **The package** (`zipStream`, every entry stored, sorted by name): `<lang>/<manual>.ditamap`, `<lang>/<module>.dita`, `filters/<variant>.ditaval`, `images/<sha256><ext>`, `files/<sha256><ext>`, `symbols/<name>`. It imports again through the `dita` importer (the e2e test does).
- **Defaults** (#433's open questions): content references (`conref`), DITA 1.3, one package per release output with its variant's DITAVAL file, made on request; no specialisations, no drafts, no editor screen yet.

## Maintenance lines

Fixing an older product revision (#60) while `main` moves on: a line is a branch started at exactly a release's tag, change sets start on it and merge into it, releases are cut from it, and a fix merged there is carried forward to `main` on request (`src/releases/` for the line, `src/change-sets/` for carrying forward).

- **`POST /maintenance-lines`** (`StartMaintenanceLineAction`) takes `{ data: { type: "MaintenanceLine", relationships: { baseRelease: { data: { type: "Release", id } } } } }` and answers 201 with the `MaintenanceLine` and `Location`. It needs `release.publish` in a role whose scope covers the release's product everywhere, as cutting a release does (403 otherwise; `content.read` too, for `openfolia.yaml` at the release's commit). **Git first:** it creates the branch named by `CCMS_MAINTENANCE_BRANCH` (default `maintenance/{product}/{revision}`, the base release's product and revision; never under `change-sets/` or `refs/`, never the same as `CCMS_RELEASE_TAG`) at the release's commit by compare-and-swap (`GitWriter.createBranch`, `MaintenanceBranches`), then records the line and a `maintenance-line.start` audit event in one transaction. A branch of that name already at that commit (a start cut off after Git) is taken (`branchExisted` in the event); one elsewhere, or clashing with another ref, is 409 `branch-exists`. One line per release: 409 `maintenance-line-exists` (`meta.maintenanceLineId`); an unknown release 404; a name the pattern cannot make 422 `invalid-maintenance-branch`.
- **Reading:** `GET /maintenance-lines` (paged, newest first, `filter[product]`, `filter[state]` `open` or `closed`), `GET /maintenance-lines/:maintenanceLineId` and `GET /maintenance-lines/:maintenanceLineId/releases` (the releases cut from it, paged, newest first) need `content.read.draft`: a line's branch holds unreleased fixes. `MaintenanceLine`: `attributes` `product`, `revision`, `branch`, `baseCommit`, `state`, `createdBy`, `createdAt`, `closedBy`, `closedAt`; `relationships.baseRelease`; `meta.head` (where the branch points now, null if it is gone).
- **`POST /maintenance-lines/:id/close`** (`release.publish`, as starting; optional `meta.comment`) closes a line for good, under a lock on its row, with a `maintenance-line.close` audit event: its branch stays, change sets on it may still be merged and releases still cut, but no new change set starts on it (409 `invalid-state`, `meta.state: "closed"`). Closed already: 409 `invalid-state`.
- **Change sets on a line:** `POST /change-sets` with `attributes.maintenanceLine` (in place of `baseRef`; both is 422 `invalid-base`, an unknown line too) starts from the line's branch; naming a line's branch as `baseRef` is the same. A closed line is 409 `invalid-state`, checked again in the creating transaction while holding the line's row (`FOR SHARE`), so a close and a create never both win. The `change-set.create` event names the line. Merging works as for any base: into the line's branch.
- **Releases from a line:** `POST /releases` with `attributes.maintenanceLine` tags the line branch's head (409 `branch-missing` without it) as a new revision of the line's product (another product is 422 `invalid-product`; an unknown line 422 `unknown-maintenance-line`). The release is linked to the line in its reservation's transaction (`maintenance_line_releases`) and its `release.publish` event names the line and branch. `Release` itself is unchanged. Its outputs are planned as any release's (#249). Its changes (`GET /releases/:releaseId/changes`, the changelog, `release.published`), and so the outputs it reuses rather than renders, count from the line's newest release before it that is not withdrawn, else from the line's base release (withdrawn: from what that one counts from); a release of `main` counts from `main`'s newest before it, never from a line's.
- **`POST /change-sets/:changeSetId/carry-forward`** (`CarryForwardChangeSetAction`; the change set's author, or a `content.edit` holder whose scope covers all it changed, else 403; 404 for a change set the actor may not see): for a change set merged into a line (409 `invalid-state` otherwise, `not-on-maintenance-line` for one merged elsewhere), a new draft change set on `main` (`Carry forward: <title>`, the actor as author) with the change set's own diff (its base commit to its reviewed head) applied to `main`'s head by a three-way merge-tree without rename detection (`GitWriter.applyChange`, `content-repo`); only the paths the change set changed are listed. Files that apply cleanly are committed in one save point, their subjects locked to the new change set; a file `main` changed differently is a conflict, left as it is on `main` and listed in `meta.carryForward.conflicts` (`path`, `subject`, git's `kinds`, the `blockIds` the change set changed in a module), as are a subject another change set holds (`locked`), one past the change set's lock limit (`too-many-locks`), one the actor may not lock, or whose merged content lies outside their scope as a save would judge it (`forbidden`, alone and without blocks, so it tells nothing of content they may not see), a merged file that does not parse, or a module naming more than 200 files (`invalid`), a module naming a file the actor may not use (`unknown-asset`, by the rule a save applies, `ReadableAssets`, #335: used by a module they see on `main` or the line, or uploaded into a change set of theirs), a file that is not a content subject (`unsupported`) and a non-text file (`binary`). Its author fixes those in the editor. `meta.carryForward` also has `from`, `commit` (null when nothing applied) and `applied`. Never automatic: one active carry-forward per change set (409 `already-carried-forward`, `meta.changeSetId`, until that draft is abandoned); nothing to carry (`main` has it all) is 409 `no-changes`. It records a `change-set.create` event (`carriedForwardFrom`), the save point's own events, and a `change-set.carry-forward` event on the original with an outbox row of that topic (`changeSetId`, `sourceId`, the line, the commit, what applied and what conflicted), linking the two (`change_set_carry_forwards`). The new branch is created first (Git, then the database): one cut off before the row is removed by the reconciler as any orphan.
- **Tables** (migration `0025_maintenance_lines`): `maintenance_lines` (`id`, `product`, `revision`, `base_release` → `releases`, `base_commit`, `branch`, `created_by_*`, `created_at`, `closed_at`, `closed_by_*`; unique on `base_release`, `(product, revision)` and `lower(branch)`); a trigger (`maintenance_lines_guard`) allows only closing an open line once, never another change, a delete or a `TRUNCATE`. `maintenance_line_releases` (`release_id` → `releases`, `line_id`) and `change_set_carry_forwards` (`change_set_id`, `source_id`, both → `change_sets`), whose rows are never updated (trigger `maintenance_links_immutable`).
- Out of scope here: the screens.

## Stable addresses

The `addresses` component (`src/addresses/`, #450): an address per product, manual, variant and language that keeps pointing at the newest release, for links and for QR codes on products, labels and printed manuals.

- **The address** is `/d/<product>/<manual>/<variant>/<lang>`: `product` a value of the product facet, `manual` the manual's slug (`delivery.manuals.<id>.slug` in `openfolia.yaml`, else its ID), `variant` its variant's slug (`model=large`), `lang` a configured language. It opens the manual as a web page (the same page as `GET /manuals/{id}/variants/{variant}/{lang}/html`) at the product's **latest release**: the newest published release of `main` that is not withdrawn (`ReleaseStore.latest`; a maintenance line's releases never count). `<lang>@<revision>` pins one release (`/d/north/operating/model=large/en@2.1`), withdrawn ones included. `…/pdf` is the release's PDF (its output, #249: never rendered on request, 409 `output-not-ready` with `Retry-After` until done, only for a reader of all of the manual as the outputs routes judge it). `…/qr.svg`, `…/qr.png`, `…/pdf/qr.svg` and `…/pdf/qr.png` are QR codes of the address's URL (`CCMS_PUBLIC_URL` and the path; 409 `public-url-not-set` without it), made by the API without a network call (`@openfolia/render-typst` `qrCodeSvg`: error correction Q, a quiet zone of four modules; the PNG eight pixels a module). `…/assets/<sha256>` and `…/symbols/<id>` serve the page's images; `/d/-/<name>` and `/d/-/fonts/<name>` its stylesheets and fonts. Every link a page holds is relative, so the pages work at the API's root and behind the editor's `/api/` alike.
- **Who may open them** is `delivery.access`, read from `openfolia.yaml` on `main` (as `RELEASE_READER`, so a change applies with the next commit), at most the deployment's `CCMS_DELIVERY_MAX_ACCESS` (#553; `public`, the default, caps nothing; `signed-in`; `off`): a project asking for more is served at the cap (`deliveryAccess`, in the routes, `GET /stable-addresses`'s `meta.access` and the PDF's QR code alike), and each of its manuals' checks report it (`delivery-access-capped`, a warning), so one reviewed change on `main` cannot make a project public on a deployment that does not allow it (a PDF's QR code is decided when its release's outputs are planned, so lowering the cap reaches the releases planned after it): `signed-in` (default) needs a token or a signed-in session (401 without one) and reads in the actor's scope; `public` reads as the public reader (`publicReader`: `content.read` up to `delivery.publicAudience`, default the first audience, in every language, type and facet), **always, whoever asks** (#608): a session, a token or `X-Ccms-User` sent with the request is never looked at, so nothing is refused (a wrong or revoked token reads as anyone) or counted as a failed authentication, a signed-in person sees exactly what the public sees (one who wants more opens a `signed-in` address), and the answer varies by no credential; `off` is 404 for everyone. The routes are `@OptionalActor(StableAddressAccessAction)`: while the addresses are `public` (`StableAddressAccessAction.answersEveryone`, the same reading of `main` and the cap), `ActorGuard` lets a request through without an actor and without reading its credentials, and the action reads as the public reader even if an actor got through (the setting changing between the two reads); otherwise a request with no `Authorization` and no `X-Ccms-User` reaches them without an actor, one with either is authenticated as everywhere, and a session cookie acts when it is valid and is ignored when not (#37).
- **Nothing unreleased, nothing outside the reader's scope.** Only a release's tag is read, never a branch or a commit. A manual, variant or language the reader may not see is 404, exactly like a missing one; a moved address (a slug, a former one in `delivery.manuals.<id>.formerly`, a language's case) is 301 to the canonical one only once the reader may see what it leads to. An older release is looked up under the manual's former IDs too (`manualIdsOf`).
- **Tracking parameters** (#553): the query parameters `CCMS_DELIVERY_IGNORED_QUERY` names (comma-separated names or prefixes ending in `*`; default `utm_*`) are dropped before a `/d/` route sees the request (`IgnoredQueryInterceptor`, `withoutIgnored`), so a link shared with them answers as the address without them, and a redirect goes on without them. Every other parameter a route does not take is still 400 `unsupported-parameter`, and a parameter the route takes (`theme`) is never dropped. An exception to the API's rule on unknown parameters, on purpose: these are addresses readers follow, not the JSON:API.
- **Addresses that would collide** (#553): `openfolia.yaml` refuses two manuals listed in `delivery.manuals` sharing an address, but a manual not listed keeps its ID as its address, which `openfolia.yaml` alone cannot see. So a manual's checks report `address-taken` (blocking) when its slug or a former address is the ID of another manual the reader sees, and when the slug or a former address of another manual the reader sees is its own ID (`params.address`, `params.manual` the other manual, `params.as` `slug` or `formerly`): that address opens the configured manual, never the one of that ID.
- **In the OpenAPI document**, the page route lists its 300 (the picker), 301, 302 (`Location`) and 410 (the notice page) answers, and the PDF, QR code, image and symbol routes their 301, 302 and 410 `release-withdrawn` (`tests/stable-addresses.e2e.spec.ts` checks each answer it gets is documented).
- **A language the release does not have:** `delivery.missingLanguage: picker` (default) answers 300 with a page linking the languages the reader may read the manual in; `source` is 302 to the source language. A pinned release that was withdrawn is 410 with a notice (English, German or French, by the address's language) linking the latest address.
- **Caching:** the latest address is `no-cache` with an ETag, so a new release shows at once; a pinned one revalidates too, so a withdrawal (410) or a change of `delivery.access` or `publicAudience` reaches every cache; so does a QR code (it holds `CCMS_PUBLIC_URL`), and a page's images are `max-age=86400`. A moved address's redirect is `no-cache`. `public` for a `public` address, with no `Vary` on any credential (the same answer for everyone, #608); `private` otherwise, with `Vary: Authorization, X-Ccms-User` (and `Cookie` when a session acted).
- **Rate limit** (#574): one client may ask a server for `CCMS_DELIVERY_PER_MINUTE` pages, PDFs and QR codes a minute (default 60; 0 for none; `DeliveryLimit`, a `SlidingWindowLimit` in memory), past it 429 `rate-limited` with `Retry-After` (in the OpenAPI document on those routes). A request without a token is keyed by its address (an IPv6 one by its /56; one that embeds an IPv4 address, mapped, NAT64, 6to4 or Teredo, by that IPv4 address), one with a token or a session by its actor, so signed-in readers never share the anonymous budget; on a `public` address, which reads no credentials (#608), everyone is keyed by their address. A client already past it is refused before anything of the address is read (only `delivery.access` on `main`, which `ActorGuard` reads first, #608); a page is counted only once it is to be rendered and a PDF once it is to be sent, so a 304 revalidation counts nothing (a QR code counts before it is drawn, its ETag being the image's). A page's images, symbols, stylesheets and fonts never count: a page loads many, and the browser keeps them. The address is `req.ip`: the connection's, unless `CCMS_TRUST_PROXY` names the proxies in front (`none` by default; a hop count; or addresses, subnets and `loopback`, `linklocal`, `uniquelocal`, a subnet at least a /8 or, IPv6, a /16; `setUpTrustProxy`), so a client's own `X-Forwarded-For` changes nothing. The same address (`clientAddress`, `src/base/api/client-address.ts`) is what failed authentications count against, an IPv6 one by its /64 only: a lockout never reaches a neighbour, and a valid service token still goes through a locked address. The container setup sets `CCMS_TRUST_PROXY=1` for the editor's nginx in front and publishes the API's own port on `127.0.0.1` only (`OPENFOLIA_API_BIND`), as a hop count believes whoever connects.
- **`GET /stable-addresses`** (JSON:API, paged, `content.read`): one `StableAddress` per output of a release the reader may read (filtered before paging, as `GET /releases/{id}/outputs` is): `latest` and `pinned`, each `{ html, pdf }` with `path` and `url` (null without `CCMS_PUBLIC_URL`), `meta.latestRelease`, `meta.withdrawn`, `meta.pdfReady`. `filter[release]` for one release, else each product's latest release (`filter[product]` for one); `filter[manual]`, `filter[lang]`. The document's `meta` holds `access` and `publicUrl`.
- **A QR code in the PDF:** `delivery.pdfQrCode: title-page` or `back-cover` (default `none`), read at the release's tag, prints the latest address's QR code and URL in every release output. Planning decides it per output (`releaseQrCode`, stored as `release_outputs.qr_code`, migration 0044), and the render prints the row's (`RenderReleaseOutputAction`, through `RenderManualPdfAction`'s `qrCode` and the renderer's `PdfExtras`, part of `RenderOptions`; part of the render key). A file is reused, and joins a reuse group (#497), only for an output that prints the same code: a new `CCMS_PUBLIC_URL` renders every file that prints one again, while a change of `delivery` is a change of `openfolia.yaml`, which renders everything anyway. Only when `CCMS_PUBLIC_URL` is set and access is `public` (after the cap, `CCMS_DELIVERY_MAX_ACCESS`): a browser following a printed code carries no token, so a signed-in address could not open from it. The manual's own PDF route prints none.
- **Settings:** `CCMS_PUBLIC_URL`, `CCMS_DELIVERY_MAX_ACCESS` and `CCMS_DELIVERY_IGNORED_QUERY` (`.env.example`), provided by the global `StableAddressSettingsModule`; `CCMS_DELIVERY_PER_MINUTE` and `CCMS_TRUST_PROXY` (#574). The editor image proxies `/d/` to the API without its token (`deploy/README.md`).

## Merges and release tags (Git primitives)

The Git side of approval (#21, see "Review workflow") and releases (#40). They write no database row themselves: a merge or a tag either moved its ref or did not, and the caller reads the ref to know which.

- **`ChangeSetBranches.merge`** (`GitChangeSetBranches`, over `GitWriter.merge`) merges a change set's exact head commit into a branch outside `change-sets/` (its base), moving that branch from the head the caller read by compare-and-swap. A merge commit has the two parents (the target first), the actor as author and committer (the same `.openfolia.invalid` identity as save points) and the trailers `Merged-change-set: <id>`, `Merged-by: <kind>/<id>` and one `Approved-by: <kind>/<id>` per approver: never a save point's `Change-set` and `Actor`, and recovery matches commit intents only (`catchUp`), and never a commit with two parents, so a merge commit is never taken for a save point. `head(name)` reads where any branch points; `mergedInto(into, head, id)` tells whether `head` is in `into`'s history and finds the merge commit among its first-parent commits since `head`'s history (`GitRepo.history`'s `excluding`: however old the merge, without a fixed window; at most `MERGE_SEARCH`, 20,000). `fastForward: 'allow'` (the default) moves the target to the head when it can, recording no merge commit and so no trailers; a merge with `approvedBy` is never a fast-forward, so approvals are always recorded. It answers `merged` (with `fastForward`), `up-to-date`, `moved` (another update won the race: read the target again and retry; nothing was written), or `conflicts` (each path with git's conflict kinds, `contents`, `modify/delete`, …; never resolved automatically, nothing written). A message ending in trailers is 422 `invalid-message`, histories with nothing in common 409 `unrelated-histories`. Needs git 2.40 or later (`merge-tree --write-tree`, `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE`), checked at the first merge with a clear error.
- **`ReleaseTags.create`** (`releases/`, `GitReleaseTags`, over `GitWriter.createTag`) makes an annotated tag on an exact commit, named by `CCMS_RELEASE_TAG` (default `release/{product}/{revision}`; product and revision are one ref component each of `A-Za-z0-9._-`, and the name must not start with `refs/`, else 422 `invalid-release-name`), with the actor as tagger (the tagger date as given, else now) and the trailers `Product`, `Revision` and `Actor`. `nameOf` gives the name the pattern makes; `find(name)` reads an annotated tag back (its commit, tagger date and message without trailers; `GitRepo.tag`). Tags are immutable: a name in use, or one clashing with a branch or tag (another case, a folder of it), is 409 `release-exists`, and no code path moves or deletes a tag. A missing commit is 422 `invalid-commit`.
- Fakes: `FakeChangeSetBranches.merge` models fast-forwards, up-to-date, the compare-and-swap (`beforeMerge`) and conflicts (a path changed on both sides to different results); `FakeReleaseTags` refuses a name in use in any case.

## Storage usage

The `storage-usage` component (`src/storage-usage/`, #404): how much storage an instance uses, so administrators see it and a hosted installation can set a limit. Self-hosted installations see no change unless they set one.

- **What counts:** media, every blob in the blob store but the ones below (uploaded images and attachments, each stored once by its hash however many change sets hold it, the branding's images (#559), and a blob no upload records, such as one the old layout's import stored), and the archived PDFs (the files of `done` release outputs, each hash once; kept forever). **Not counted:** renditions (made again on request), an import's uploaded bundle (removed when the import expires), the PDF render cache, the Git content and the database; the last two are reported for information.
- **Kept totals, no counting on request:** one row, `storage_usage` (migration `0030_storage_usage`, which inserts it): bytes and files per category, the Git and database sizes, `recounted_at`, and the limit last recorded. Moved in the transaction making the change (`StorageUsage.adjust`, never below 0): the first upload record of a hash adds its bytes in its recording transaction (`StorageQuota.addMedia`), decided under the hash's lock whatever the blob store's put said, so of uploads of the same bytes at the same time exactly one counts them; deleting an unused file (`DeleteAssetAction`) takes its bytes off as the last step of the transaction that deletes it (after the blob store's call, so the totals' row is not held across it); a release output that stored a new PDF adds it once done (best effort: the output is done either way). The same bytes uploaded again add nothing, and neither counts nor deletes a hash the media meter leaves out (`StorageUsage.apartFromMedia`): uploading a release's archived PDF adds nothing to the media, and deleting that upload removes its records but keeps the archived file (audited with `fileKept: true`); an archived PDF no upload records is neither listed as unused nor deleted (409, #429), so the media library never offers room it cannot free. A blob stored without an upload record (the old layout's) is counted again by its first upload, until the next recount (counted high). The other way round, an upload of an import bundle's bytes counts nothing, and when the import expires and the upload keeps the blob, the media are low by it until the next recount.
- **Recount** (`RecountStorageAction`, on a timer, `StorageRecounter`: a minute after start-up, then every 15 minutes it checks): when the last recount is `CCMS_STORAGE_RECOUNT_HOURS` old (default 24), the totals are replaced by what the meters find: `MediaMeter` (`BlobStoreMediaMeter`, the blob store's listing page by page, less the PDFs, renditions and import bundles the database names) and `PdfArchiveMeter` (`DrizzlePdfArchiveMeter`, over `release_outputs`), with `InstanceSizes` (`LocalInstanceSizes`: the content repository's files on disk, without following links, at most a million entries; `pg_database_size`), each null when it cannot be read. Servers side by side recount once: a recount is claimed by compare-and-swap on the row (a claim older than an hour is taken over). While it is claimed, what uploads add is also gathered apart (`media_added_*`, `pdf_added_*`) and added to what the recount found, so a change made while it counts is counted at worst twice (refusing a little early), never lost (letting uploads past the limit); the next recount sets it right. A recount stops after 30 minutes (`RECOUNT_DEADLINE_MS`) or at shutdown, keeping the totals, and its claim lapses. A freshly migrated installation is counted a minute after start-up.
- **Limit** (`CCMS_STORAGE_LIMIT_BYTES`, bytes; unset: none, the default): media and archived PDFs together.
