An upload is refused with 409 `storage-limit-reached` (`meta.limit`; what is used only administrators read) and nothing is stored: before a byte is read when nothing is left; while reading, as `BlobStore.put`'s `maxBytes` is capped at the room left (a file past it is refused as the limit, not as `too-large`, even when it is larger than the upload limit too); and in the recording transaction, which holds the totals' row, so uploads at the same time never both slip past it (the blob the refused one stored is removed as any unrecorded upload is). Imports' files take the same upload path. Release PDFs are never refused. The largest single upload stays `CCMS_MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` / `CCMS_MAX_ATTACHMENT_BYTES` ("Image uploads", "Attachments").
- **Audited:** a refused upload, `storage.upload-refused` by the uploader (subject `storage/instance`, `data`: `changeSetId`, or `target: "branding"` for a branding image, `limit`, `used`), in a transaction of its own (the upload's rolled back; best effort, logged when it fails), at most once per actor every 10 minutes on a server, the next event counting the refusals meanwhile (`refusedMeanwhile`), so a loop of refused requests cannot flood the append-only log; a change of the limit, `storage.limit-change` (`data`: `from`, `to`, null for none) by `service:storage`, recorded at start-up when the setting differs from the limit last recorded, under the row's lock, so servers starting together record it once. An installation that never sets a limit records nothing.
- **`GET /storage-usage`** (`admin.roles`, as the audit log): a `StorageUsage` (`id` `instance`): `attributes` `media` and `archivedPdfs` (`{ bytes, files }`), `gitContent` and `database` (`{ bytes }`, null when not known), `limitBytes` (null: none), `recountedAt`; `meta` `usedBytes`, `usedShare` (of the limit; null without one), `level` (`unlimited`, `ok`, `warning` from `warnAt`, 80 %, on, `reached`) and `warnAt`. **`GET /storage-usage/files`** (`admin.roles`, paged): the uploaded files largest first, one `StoredFile` per hash (`id` the hash; `reference`, `size`, `contentType` and `fileName` of the first upload, `lastUploadedAt`): file names of every change set, which administrators read in the audit log's upload events already.
- Out of scope: billing, plans and prices (the hosting's, not the product's), bandwidth metering, clearing the render cache.

## Backups

The `backups` component (`src/backups/`, #529; the operator's guide is `deploy/README.md`, "Backups"). The API holds the settings, the backup key's public half and the history; a separate process, the backup service (`backup.main.ts`, `CCMS_ROLE=backup` in the backup image), does the work. They meet only in the database: the service reads the settings at every pass (no restart), takes "Back up now" and "Test location" as rows of `backup_requests`, and reports itself in `backup_service` (a heartbeat every 15 s, with the settings its environment fixes).

- **Routes** (all `admin.backups`, a global permission; 403 `forbidden` otherwise, also for `admin.roles`; `Cache-Control: no-store`):
  - `GET`, `PATCH /backup-settings` (`BackupSettings`, `id` `instance`): `enabled`, `frequency` (`daily`, `weekly` with `weekday` 0-6, `hours` with `every` 1-23, `cron` with a five-field `expression`), `time` (HH:MM), `timeZone` (IANA), `retention` (`daily`, `weekly`, `monthly`), `location` (`local` with a `subfolder` of the backup volume, or `s3` with `endpoint`, `region`, `bucket`, `prefix`, `accessKeyId`, and in a PATCH only the write-only `secretAccessKey`: a string sets or replaces it, null removes it, left out keeps it for the same endpoint, bucket and access key, else 422 `secret-required`), `verify`, `verifyByDecrypting`. Answered: `location.secretAccessKeySet`, never the secret; `meta` `fixed` (setting to variable), `schedule` (the cron expression), `nextRuns` (the next three), `service` (`running`, `lastSeenAt`), `key` (the current key's fingerprint) and `updatedAt`. Refused: 422 `invalid-schedule`, `invalid-time-zone`, **`backup-path-outside-volume`** (absolute, `..`, a backslash, a hidden or odd segment; the service resolves it again, links included, and refuses an escape), `invalid-endpoint`, `invalid-bucket`, `invalid-prefix` (several at once: `invalid-backup-settings`), `backup-identity-missing` (verifying by decrypting turned on without a kept identity); 409 **`backup-setting-fixed-by-environment`** (`meta.setting`, `meta.variable`). Audited `backup-settings.update`: each field changed, `{ from, to }`, and for the secret only `set`, `replaced` or `removed`.
  - `GET /backup-keys` (paged, newest first: `recipient`, `fingerprint`, `createdAt`, `retiredAt`, never the identity) and `POST /backup-keys` (201): makes the backup key (an age X25519 identity) and answers the identity once, in `meta.identity`. 409 `backup-key-exists` unless `{ meta: { rotate: true } }`, which retires the current key (its identity dropped) and makes a new one. The identity is kept, sealed under the instance secret key (`backup-key:v1:<id>`), only while `verifyByDecrypting` is on. Audited `backup-key.create` / `backup-key.rotate` with the fingerprints.
  - `GET /backup-runs` (paged, newest first): `trigger` (`schedule`, `manual`, `command`), `status`, `scheduledFor`, `startedAt`, `finishedAt`, `sizeBytes`, `sha256`, `location`, `name`, `keyFingerprint`, `verified`, `verifyMode` (`decrypted`, `before-encryption`), `error` and `errorDetail`.
  - `POST /backup-requests` (`kind` `run` or `test-location`; 202 with `Location`), `GET /backup-requests/:requestId`: 409 `backup-service-not-running` (no heartbeat within a minute) or `backup-key-missing`. A request of the same kind still waiting is answered again. Audited `backup.request`.
  - `GET /backup-status`: `meta` `enabled`, `keySet`, `lastRun`, `lastGoodRun`, `warnAfterHours` (`CCMS_BACKUP_WARN_AFTER_HOURS`, 26) and `warning` (`not-set-up`, `service-not-running`, `failed`, `overdue`, or null).
- **A run** (`RunBackupAction`): a session advisory lock (`PgBackupLock`) keeps it to one at a time; the source (`ShellBackupSource`) dumps the database in one snapshot transaction (`pg_dump --snapshot`, row counts, the referenced blob hashes), bundles the content repository (`git bundle create --all`) and streams one tar of those, the blob folder and the instance secret key into age (`age-encryption`, encrypted to the key's recipient; no key: the run fails `backup-key-missing`, never unencrypted). The verifier (`ShellBackupVerifier`) restores into `<db>_verify_<time>_<run>` (always dropped), compares the row counts, clones and `fsck`s the bundle and hashes the referenced blobs; by decrypting the finished file, or, with no identity kept, the parts before encryption and the file's age header, size and SHA-256. Then the target (`LocalBackupTarget`, `S3BackupTarget`) stores it and retention (`core/retention.ts`, newest of each day, ISO week and month in the time zone; the newest always kept) prunes. A failed run tells the `admin.backups` holders once per incident (outbox `backup.failed`, key `failed:<last good run>`), as does a last good backup older than the limit (`backup.overdue`).
- **Restore** (`repos/restore-backup.ts`, `backup restore <name>`): needs the key; checks the archive's entries (files and folders only, safe names) before extracting; refuses an instance that holds anything unless `--force`.
- **Sealed:** the S3 secret (`backup-s3-secret:v1`) and the kept identities are `SealedSecretSource`s, so the instance key's check and rotation cover them.
- **Tables** (`repos/backups.tables.ts`): `backup_settings` and `backup_service` (one row each), `backup_keys` (at most one current), `backup_runs`, `backup_requests`.

## Public sandbox

The `sandbox` component (`src/sandbox/`, #826): with `CCMS_PUBLIC_SANDBOX=on` an instance is a throwaway one for a single stranger on a public demo, who must not be able to reach anyone else or send anything out. The settings are in `src/config/sandbox-env.ts` and `.env.example`.

- **At start** (`checkSandboxEnv`): only with `CCMS_AUTH_MODE=demo`. Every setting that would reach outside refuses to start, naming it (`SANDBOX_REFUSED_SETTINGS`): OpenID Connect, password and email-code sign-in turned on, the first admin's email, mail, webhooks to http or private addresses, the style review and embedding providers, the audit sink, the S3 blob store, and a `CCMS_ROLE` other than `all`. The backup service (`BackupEnv`) refuses to start at all. Downloads and stable addresses open at most to signed-in people (`CCMS_DELIVERY_MAX_ACCESS` `public` becomes `signed-in`), and the PDF workers and render concurrency are capped at `CCMS_SANDBOX_RENDER_JOBS`.
- **Nothing leaves** (the ports): `Mailer` is `NoMailer` and the outbox drops every email with a log line, `WebhookSender` is `SandboxWebhookSender` (every delivery fails `blocked-url`, nothing sent), `Embedder` `NoEmbedder`, `StyleReviewer` `NoStyleReviewer`, `AuditSink` `NoAuditSink`, no identity providers, and `BlobsModule` refuses any driver but `fs`. `tests/sandbox-outbound.spec.ts` lists every file of the API and its packages that can reach the network (Node's network modules, the network packages, `fetch`) with its decision, and every package imported with whether it can: a new one fails the test until it has a decision. `tests/sandbox.e2e.spec.ts` runs the app in a sandbox, checks the adapters and that nothing connects beyond the loopback.
- **Refused** (403 **`sandbox-not-allowed`**, `meta.refused`; `Sandbox.refuse`, at the start of the action's run: after the permission its policy names, before anything is read, so also before an action's own finer checks, such as an import's `content.edit`): `users` (adding users), `service-accounts`, `tokens`, `invitations` (invitation and reset links), `uploads` (images, attachments, fonts, branding images), `imports` (imports and translation imports), `templates` (saving a page design's template), `webhooks` (creating, changing, rotating, testing, retrying), `mail` (the test email), `backups` (settings, keys, requests).
- **Bounded** (422 **`sandbox-limit-reached`**, `meta.limit` and `meta.max`): a working copy's bytes (`moduleBytes`), a comment's characters (`commentLength`), the change sets made, carried-forward ones included (`changeSets`), the modules and manuals made (`modules`, `manuals`: a working copy saved for a file that does not exist yet), the releases cut in the last hour (`releasesPerHour`), and the bytes written over the session (`writeBytes`, `Sandbox.spend`, counted when offered): every working copy saved (of any kind, the same one again included), every comment written or edited, every commit, whichever action makes it (its files' bytes, every tree object it rewrites, each touched folder with all its entries, its message and trailers, and 2 KiB for the root tree and the commit, in `GitChangeSetBranches.prepare`: save points, renames, reverts, carries, translations marked current; a merge into the base or a refresh is not spent, bounded by the change sets), and every file stored in the blob store (`SandboxBlobStore`: release files, offline packages, renditions; past the budget the put fails and stores nothing). So it bounds what reaches the database, Git and the disk, whichever route writes it. A release plans at most 50 files (`CCMS_RELEASE_MAX_OUTPUTS` capped). `Sandbox.counted` counts one only when it was made, and one being made already counts against the cap; a change set started again on a rewritten base counts too. The launcher still caps each sandbox's disk and database, and the rate of its requests (rows that grow with each request, such as the audit log, are not counted).
- **`GET /sandbox`** (any actor; 404 `not-configured` when off): `Sandbox` `current`, `attributes` the limits, `idleMinutes` and `maxMinutes`; `meta` what was used, `startedAt` and `endsAt` (the last request plus the idle minutes, at most the start plus the maximum). Every request but `/sandbox` and `/health` counts as activity. The editor learns the mode from it and shows its banner, the time left and no upload controls.
- **One process**: the counts and the clock are kept in memory, for one API process per sandbox (`CCMS_ROLE` other than `all` refuses to start). The API does not end itself at `endsAt`: the launcher that started it deletes it.

## Licence and source (`GET /about`)

The software is AGPL-3.0-only (`LICENSE` at the repository root; #370). `GET /about` answers without a token, like the health check, with the licence (`license`: the SPDX identifier, `licenseUrl`: its published text) and `sourceUrl`: where this deployment's source code is, from `CCMS_SOURCE_URL` (an `http://` or `https://` URL without credentials, normalised; null when unset). AGPL-3.0 section 13 asks a modified version run as a network service to offer its users the source: the editor links to it from the account menu and the sign-in page. It is read from the settings alone, so there is no action behind it. `docsUrl` (#471, #221): where the user guide is, from `CCMS_DOCS_URL` (default `https://docs.openfolia.com/`; an `http://` or `https://` URL without credentials, always ending in `/`; `off` gives null), under which the editor opens a page by its path (`guide/change-sets/base-rewritten`, after `de/` in German) for its "Learn more" links.

## Notes

- The server binds to `127.0.0.1` by default. Expose it only behind TLS.
- PDFs compile on a pool of long-lived worker processes, so rendering never blocks other requests. Each job has a deadline including queueing (when missed: 503 with `Retry-After` if the time went mostly to waiting for a worker, 422 if the document itself compiled too long); when the queue is full, new jobs get 503 with `Retry-After`. Tune with `CCMS_PDF_WORKERS`, `CCMS_PDF_QUEUE`, `CCMS_PDF_TIMEOUT_MS` and `CCMS_PDF_MEMORY_MB` (a worker's memory limit, default 2048, at least 256: a compilation past it is stopped and answers 422 `render-memory-exceeded`, #396; see `.env.example`). Workers are recycled after 50 jobs, which returns the memory the compiler keeps. Each worker compiles in its own private (0700) temporary folder, which is checked before jobs are handed out and replaced if it changed (workers also check it before each job); Typst cannot read files outside it, and a template cannot import Typst packages: a worker process gets an empty home and no network, and none of the server's environment (`packages/render-typst/README.md`, "What a template can reach"). Results are cached per commit, manual, variant, language and what the reader may see.
- **PDF/UA-1** (#58): every PDF is tagged and declares PDF/UA-1 (structure tree with headings, figures with their alt text, table headers, lists and links; the manual's title and language), unless `openfolia.yaml` sets `pdf.standard: none` (`@openfolia/content-model`). A document that cannot meet it (an image without alt text, a heading without text, a character no font has, a repository template that leaves a decorative image untagged) is **422 `pdf-accessibility`**, `meta.problems` holding the renderer's reasons (`message`, `hints`; each once, at most 50, `meta.more` counting the rest); never a plain PDF in its place. A refusal is cached like a PDF (same key), so asking again answers at once. The manual's checks render each variant in each language the same way (same cache key, one at a time) and report each reason as a blocking `pdf-accessibility` check (see "Checks"). How to check a PDF with veraPDF: `@openfolia/render-typst`'s README, "Accessibility".
- Shutdown (SIGINT/SIGTERM): running PDF jobs end with 503, the HTTP server lets other requests finish, then workers, temporary folders and the repository are cleaned up. A second signal, or 10 s passing, ends the process at once. The exit code is the signal's (143 for SIGTERM), which orchestrators treat as a normal stop.
- A repository template (`templates/manual.typ`) cannot import Typst packages (`@preview/…`, `@local/…`) and cannot read files outside its own sandbox (`packages/render-typst/README.md`, "What a template can reach").
